Prologue to Book 3 One Year Later

 
4
Prologue
THE VOTE
Nothing could have prepared us for that day. The decision was made so fast, 
and all of the covens were expecting us to fail. I tell this not as a story by the 
campfire, but as a true event. My name is Kaery, and I was as much to blame as 
any Mancer who was responsible for the fate of the world. This is what happened 
so long ago. This is what started Earth’s first war.
It was a beautiful day, like every day that came before it in Pangaea. The 
world was new and full of possibility. Humans lived among Supernaturals as they 
belonged. Mancers ruled both factions. Witchbreed were the sages and teachers of 
all. My coven was a new coven, and we were chastised every turn we took for our 
former allegiances to the covens we broke off from. 
“I can’t do this anymore, Kaery,” Ewm said frantically to his audience of 
eight, “I have to go back to my coven or they will curse you all, and I can’t have 
that. Anshar of all people was happy we got chosen, and he’s just waiting for us to 
fail miserably. Did you see that look in his eye? I’m blind, and even I saw it!” 
His blood-red eyes were very peculiar. He was blind to most things that 
were not Supernatural or Magickal by nature. His black hair was always kept neat 
and shorter than that of the other boys, even though the style was to wear it slightly 
longer. Ewm paced back and forth in his ornate robes with his large book of spells 
not too far from his reach. 
Kaery was getting ready to speak when she was interrupted by Ahlina. Of all 
the people on the planet, these were the ones she trusted with her life. Ahlina had 
been Kaery’s best friend since the day she saved her from being eaten by a 
prehistoric beast. Ahlina was a wild nature girl with exotic green eyes and grand 
thoughts of unifying all of the covens, like she had done with her best friends. 
“You don’t think we all want to just call it quits and go the easy route?,” 
Ahlina asked Ewm directly, “We can go back to being belittled within a large 
coven instead of being bullied by the ones much older than ours. We have proven 
ourselves time and again that, with our combined Magicks, we can do wonderful 
things, have we not?” 

 
5
“Well, yes. But—” Ewm was interrupted by Ahlina’s lecture.
“And we have stood together against Battle covens that were out to teach us 
‘lessons on spellcrafts.’ We’ve defeated them all every time, have we not?”
“Yes, barely a couple times. But—”
“And we have broken the oaths that we pledged to the covens we were born 
into, so that we can form our own sanctuary. We are a coven that even the Grand 
Mancers have noticed. Today they made a decision, charging our Coven with the 
creation of the most amazing spell the world has ever known. Not too many 
Witchbreed get this opportunity. This is what we’ve dreamt of: recognition. I say 
we should take it.” Ahlina smiled righteously as she looked over her unique friends 
for approval.
Khenebu was from a coven of Witchbreed that had mastered the 
Necromantech Spellcrafts. These monks mimicked powerful dark Magicks that 
they saw the most powerful Necromancers and Technomancers use. They also 
used abilities from other planes of existence, which they believed would benefit the 
world at large. Because their methods were clouded by mystery like all other 
covens, Khenebu chose to be the first Witchbreed to break away and expose many 
of their secrets. 
“Though I agree with both sides, I would like to say my piece,” Khenebu 
said, “Ewm is right, assuming it is easier to crawl back to our original covens, for 
they would protect the secrets they have built their very foundations upon. To 
answer your question, Ewm, yes, I saw that exact look in Anshar’s eyes, and I 
don’t trust it at all.” 
Ewm smiled, nervously awaiting the continuation of Khenebu’s diplomatic 
speech. 
“Ahlina is correct in assuming the sanctuary and eternal bond which we 
have cast upon each other as the new Coven of Unity within the Great Circle,” 
Khenebu said, “There are twelve other covens out there that have time and again 
wished for us to fall flat on our faces. It is our perfect love and perfect trust that 
keep us victorious.” 
Ahlina smiled at Khenebu’s agreeing words, awaiting the climax of his 
speech.
“I, however, believe this spell is disgustingly wrong!,” he said, “It is a 
blasphemy against the balance of things and has nothing to do with our integrity as 
users of Magick or our bond as a coven. It has to do with our role on this planet. To 

 
6
give humans our powers would be to go against everything we have been taught as 
Witchbreed, keepers of the planetary balance.” Khenebu concluded with resolve, 
calmly leaving the speaker’s circle to sit next to his beloved Kaery. 
Ewm couldn’t help but agree with Khenebu, who seemed to be correct most 
of the time, even though he was the least outspoken of the coven. 
“I support Khenebu,” Ewm said, “I mean, I understand that no one’s ever 
attempted such a spell before, so I get that it’s a big deal. However, a spell of 
ultimate Enlightenment would be catastrophic for those who have no control, don’t 
you think? Cause and effect to Gaia would be devastating.” 
The youngest of the coven said this so matter-of-factly that the others took a 
moment to ponder.
“No I don’t think it would,” Scape said, siding with Ahlina, “Have you seen 
how many Witchbreed inhabit the planet and how many Mancers control the 
Armies of Jreamers? We all have, and I think we’ll have it under control.”
The Shaman’s low-cut robes exposed an ever-changing mural of tattoos that 
twisted and swirled over his body, giving him an air of mysticism as he appeared 
in the center of the speaker’s circle. 
“My old coven could control the very elements themselves, even morphing 
into them at will. Though we don’t generally try to control the fate of the world, 
preferring to live peacefully as nature intended, we are still hypocrites. We have 
altered the elements on this planet so that the weather is always beautiful for 
humans and Supernaturals alike. We have purified the air and water to make the 
planet habitable for everyone. We have provided them with fire and metal so they 
might invent and evolve. What is another alteration in the planet’s fate?”
Scape sat down next to Ahlina. His handsome face accompanied his 
objective words perfectly. 
Kaery stood up to enter the speaker’s circle, and all went silent.
“We have argued this point many a night,” she said, “As a matter of fact, it 
was a split decision even when we heard such a spell was going to be demanded of 
the Wicasht by the Mancer years ago. One thing we will not do is cast a spell 
before considering the repercussions. I love you all and will always love you until 
the end of time, no matter the outcome. We are to perform a task that will, as 
Scape so eloquently said, alter the planet’s fate. I will say this: We are on this 
planet to serve the greater good. We all have the ability to see the code of Magick 
that is ever-confusing and ever-amazing. We chose to be the readers of that code, 

 
7
and with the responsibility of deciphering it, we also chose to teach others the 
meaning of Magick. This includes our masters and mistresses, the Mancer.” 
Kaery looked each member of her coven in the eyes as she continued to 
speak while walking around the hall.
“Ewm, you came from a coven that has been nicknamed the Warlocks. They 
practice destructive and offensive Magicks as if our planet were on the cusp of 
war. 
This coven practices malevolent curses that cast fear into the hearts of most, but 
how can we fear an intellectual so committed to what’s right?” Kaery petted the 
youngster on his head, assuring him that she was not being judgmental. 
“Ahlina, you come from a coven known for disrupting the physics of a 
world that thrives on order. The other covens have nicknamed you the Chaos Kids 
for that very reason. We here embrace your chaotic nature and welcome you with 
open arms.” Kaery smiled at Ahlina from across the sacred hall. 
“Khenebu, you left a misunderstood coven nicknamed the Gore-hounds, 
who revel in the darker side of the universe, playing with the dead and unknown 
like children play with dolls. Yet in our midst, you are one of the wisest and most 
innovative beings the planet has ever seen. Because you had the courage to leave 
your coven first, we have gained the strength to follow suit.” Kaery looked across 
the hall, her eyes falling on the white hair, pale skin and timid demeanor of Never, 
who lowered his head in self-conscious embarrassment . 
“Never, you left a coven of Wyrm worshippers also known as the Dragons, 
the most feared Dehmn beasts of all realms, yet you are one of the gentlest spirits 
to walk the earth, your kindness rivaled only by that of Scape.” Kaery turned to 
Scape and brushed his hair from his eyes.
“Scape, your spirit is as wondrous and free as the elements your old coven 
mastered. However, people still label them as Beasts because they are so close to 
nature.” Kaery noticed Scape wince as she uttered the derogatory name. 
“I’m sorry for the crass words, but I must remind you all for the sake of the 
inevitable vote.”
“Heaven and Star come from twin sister covens that split into two 
completely different entities. Heaven’s coven preferred the company of the Fae to 
that of their own kind, which resulted in the nickname Faerykin. Star’s coven 
preferred their own looks and solitude to the world at large, earning the title of The 

 
8
Misunderstood Bitches.” Kaery closed her eyes, trying not to grow enraged at the 
stupidity of these stereotypes and prejudices.
“Last, but not least, Ssiah, oh beautiful celestial girl! Coming from a coven 
of Aenjelhn who were born with the ability to summon their own relatives and 
protectors, who lost their founder in a war with the Dehmn, they have been called 
the Orphans. This name is not very becoming for such a pure heart, and it was 
meant to break your spirit.” Kaery walked over to Ssiah and held her hand, the 
delicate girl’s blond locks falling as she turned her gaze to the ground.
“I myself have left the first Witchbreed coven to ever grace Earth, the Anu, 
who have been nicknamed the Banshees for the truth we tell. It’s not usually 
something people want to hear, so they compare us to creatures from another realm 
whose voices are capable of making innocent listeners die of madness.” 
Kaery looked around the room, confident that the honest critique of her own 
ex-coven had gained everyone’s attention. 
“So you all see this is not a matter of where we come from, but where we’re 
going. Blue and Sky have chosen us over the most powerful Witchbreed on the 
planet to perform a spell that the Mancers believe will help them reign. They are 
our Kings and Queens, and it is our sacred duty to follow their orders. We have 
ignored our duties before, and we can choose to do so now, but it is up to you all to 
vote on the fate of the planet tonight and not turn back. For if we decide this night 
to conjure a spell of this magnitude, we will have to take ownership of it.”
After a careful look at her coven and quiet understanding of the severity of this 
decision, Kaery broke the silence with a thunderous voice. “It is time to vote.” 

 
9
Chapter 1
HEAVEN SENT
My Spellcraft was perfected through the song and dance of many lifetimes. I 
found that I was able to look into the depths of the Soulstream and use enchanting 
Magick. Through movement, I can change the environment itself, and with song, I 
can open portals in time and space, summoning ancient supernatural beings and 
harnessing their abilities. I am Heaven, and I belong to the Coven of Unity. 
Ever since I was a young girl, I’ve known I was a Wicasht. For some reason, 
our beautiful name was butchered into an aberration that even the Supernaturals 
use: Witchbreed. I guess it means the same thing through rough translation, but it 
still makes me cringe. I love my coven, even though I’ve yet to meet them in this 
lifetime. 
I know that sounds weird, but I’ve dreamt of them my entire life. I still 
remember the first dream I ever had of them. We all were in Pangaea and having 
an amazing time, laughing. It was a bright sunny day, and we all had just finished 
swimming when the world changed. Well, all of us but Never and Star, who sat on 
the bank, and Khenebu, who was nowhere to be found. Six of us were splashing 
around that day so long ago. 
(Pangaea: 250,000,000 B.C.E.)
Heaven swam through the clear lake as if she were one of the primitive life 
forms that dwelled in the depths of the New World’s magnificent oceans. Her 
bright purple hair gracefully floated around her chocolate skin in long strands that 
looked as if they were part of the blue water itself. They seemed to change color 
with every inch she swam toward her friends. Heaven was an extraordinary 
specimen as Gauntlets would often tell her. 
She could use powers from other dimensions as if she were actually present 
in them. The Fae’s dimension was where she manipulated Magick, and it was 
marvelous. By accessing this dimension, she could alter her personal attributes on 
planet earth with every movement she exerted, such as the color of her body or 

 
10
hair. Most of the time, she used this power in friendly games of stealth. This was 
one of those games, and she was positive that no one would be able to see her. 
She watched her friends floating on the surface of the lake as if they were 
using Magick, though she could tell that they weren’t. They were all simply 
relaxing in the sun and talking. Kaery swam on the surface, not knowing that 
Heaven was but a few feet below, watching her mischievously. 
Kaery looked about at her friends and wondered what new mysteries this 
world called Earth would have in store for them in the many years to come. It just 
seemed so perfect. In the midst of deep thought, she looked at her friend Ahlina 
and sensed an aura of anxiety. Scape, Heaven and Ahlina had always been the 
playful and unpredictable ones in the coven. Kaery had watched them play games 
of stealth and Spellcraft for many hours at a time, all while amusing the younger 
Ewm and Ssiah, who currently liked to swim closer to the shore. With Ahlina’s 
anxiousness as well as Scape and Heaven’s absence, Kaery knew that a game of 
stealth had already started. She was excited to see how this would play out as the 
Witchbreed attempted to catch each other off guard. 
Never was a quiet man and didn’t like water, so he would sit this game out, 
but he looked forward to the upcoming flight and combat games. He was 
exceptionally cunning and introverted, yet still very competitive, and knew that 
Kaery’s enforcement of the games would benefit each Witchbreed when the time 
came to use their powers on the fly. This day, he sat not too far from where Star 
was sunbathing. 
“Why must they play these idiotic games with their Spellcrafts?,” Star asked, 
not bothering to make eye contact while tanning, “You’d think they were human or 
something.” 
Her glossy lips and milky skin gleamed as the sun rolled over them 
flawlessly. She flipped her silky red locks and turned over to let the sun warm her 
back. 
Kaery overheard Star’s complaint while floating on the surface of the water. 
She knew the Witchbreed were just having fun practicing their Spellcrafts with 
each other and dismissed the cynical words. Maybe Ahlina was just worried about 
the backlash of the ex-covens, who now despised them all for divulging secrets 
that were ten thousand years old. 
“Is something on your mind, Ahlina?”

 
11
Just as Kaery finished her question, Ahlina suddenly yelped and slipped into 
the water as if something were pulling her under. Kaery yelped almost instinctively 
and began to propel her naked body toward the shore. Ewm and Ssiah started 
laughing , as they knew the game had begun. According to their ever-changing 
rules, Ahlina had lost because she cried out when she was taken by surprise.
Khenebu must have been at the Temples, probably lost in study or still in the 
midst of diplomatic discussion, but Scape was nowhere to be found. He had not 
come to the lake with them, so Kaery assumed that Heaven was the culprit who 
pulled Ahlina under the water. She almost stopped swimming toward Ewm and 
Ssiah when she heard a high-pitched noise.
Heaven was screaming as she flew through the air and landed on the shore 
with incredible grace despite her surprise. Her violet hair splashed the already 
annoyed Star, who responded with an irritable groan. Overwhelmed by his own 
astonishment, Never went to aid Heaven.
“Are you alright, Heaven?” Never asked as he looked into her purple eyes 
and marveled at her brown skin, noticing that she was battered from the high fall. 
Heaven simply laughed and smiled playfully. 
“I’m ok, Never,” she said, “but you’re sweet. They caught me off guard.” 
Heaven’s voice was among the planet’s most beautiful sounds. Consisting of 
three distinct vocal tones at once, each with its own frequency, her singing was 
music to anyone’s ears. Never smiled every time he heard it.
“They?” asked Never, looking over the still waters, confused that Kaery, 
Ewm and Ssiah were the only Witchbreed in sight.
The waters began to swirl inward, forming a whirlpool. The currents pushed 
and pulled at the shores. Kaery pulled Ewm and Ssiah safely to shore as Star stood 
up in protest. Ssiah’s wet blond ringlets covered her hazel eyes as she ran onto 
shore while looking over her shoulder, giggling. Ewm squeezed Kaery’s hand tight 
as he watched the event. 
“I’m not amused,” Star said, pausing mid-sentence as she walked toward the 
cyclone of water. 
Ahlina started to emerge from the center with a proud grin. 
“We so caught Heaven trying to lurk on us.” 
She was walking across the water toward shore while Scape materialized 
from the passing waves. His flesh formed from the liquid, its sacred markings a 
moving tapestry as he stepped comfortably up to Ahlina. The pair walked across 

 
12
the top of the water, pointing and laughing at Heaven on the shore. When they 
reached the warm sand, Never put his arm around Heaven as he stared at the show 
of Scape’s new Shapeshifting Spellcraft. It was astonishing to see him shift from 
one elemental to the next, from fire to water, from wind to earth. 
“It’s ok, Heaven, you always win anyway—” Never’s words were cut short 
when he looked down to see that his arm was wrapped around a large fish with 
seaweed hair. He spun around to see Ahlina and Scape on the water, high-fiving 
each other a little too prematurely, Heaven standing behind them, blowing a kiss 
toward shore. A double dropkick launched them into the air, their bodies flailing 
about until they landed directly on top of Star. 
Heaven flipped into the air and performed a back handspring off of the water 
as if it were solid ground and stood victorious on the surface of the lake. Her hair 
was as long as she was tall, draped over her body like cascading water. 
“You all fell for a simple illusion. By the way, nice new Spellcraft, Scape.” 
Heaven floated toward the banks and helped her friends up, relieving Star of 
the excess weight. 
Ewm let go of Kaery’s hand and brushed his black hair out of his crimson 
eyes. His pale skin showed goose bumps, either from lack of warmth or from the 
excitement of the battle itself; either way, he was entertained as always. 
“That was absolutely impeccable,” Ewm said, “Two new Spellcrafts and 
Ahlina acting like a damsel in distress!”
“I don’t know why you’re so excited,” Ssiah said, “I was terrified when 
Heaven flew out of the water. How did you do that, Heaven?”
Ssiah helped Star out of the sand, who slapped the girl’s hand hard enough 
for it to sting. 
“I can help myself up.” Star hopped to her feet, ready to berate her 
insubordinate friends.
Kaery looked past Star and saw Khenebu riding toward the lake on one of 
his coven’s technologically advanced contraptions. It was made entirely of 
planetary minerals, and it flew at speeds that rivaled those of the most advanced 
flying Spellcrafts. As he got closer, Star almost forgot why she was upset, gazing 
up at the machine fueled by Magick. 
“I’m sorry to intrude on your games,” Khenebu said, “I bring dire news. The 
Mancers are making a decision as to which coven will perform the Spell of 
Enlightenment.” 

 
13
The friends looked at each other with a new level of understanding that 
relieved all ill will and brought the realization that the games were over. They 
scrambled to retrieve their robes as one by one, they all climbed into Khenebu’s 
flyer and traveled toward the pyramids in the distance with a mix of anxiety and 
awe.
*
(Present-Day)
Heaven woke from the dream and gazed into the sky at the stars and the 
moon in a moment of clarity.
“I have to find my coven.”
*
Scape sat up from a good night’s sleep, gasping a single word: “Heaven!”

Chapter 2
ANSWERS
Gemini, a tall dark tower of a man, stood near the Port Rune and watched 
the glowing mark of Magick as it transported his students to this safe haven, the 
forbidden city of Zalomoni. As the wind blew his long black hair and charcoal 
cloak around him like living shadow, he pondered his next move in this struggle 
against Neflym and OZONE. He knew there would be consequences for bringing 
so many of the Supernaturals to his Pagoda, and it was surely compromised, but in 
his mind, the sacrifice was worth it. OZONE was definitely setting up an ambush 
for the children, but they hadn’t anticipated the use of this Port Rune. Gemini knew 
he was one step ahead, as they had yet to discover countless other Port Runes that 
could be accessed for inter-dimensional travel. 
There was a small window to save the children, but enough time to make 
OZONE believe they had won some glorious battle this evening. In actuality, 
Gemini got more than he once expected with the arrival of Juno, the assassin and 
OZONE double Agent who tracked down a lover from another lifetime, the 
Neuromancer, Ninala. A new Mancer and a most intriguing one, Surge-Overload, 
who was instrumental in saving many Supernaturals, never really saw herself on 
the run from the threatening organization, but here she was coming through the 
portal. Finally there was Maia, the coy oracle whom Gemini personally escorted 
from a home where she was going to be studied then murdered after manipulation 
at the hands of OZONE.
The moment of Blue’s realization that Sky wasn't going to come through the 
portal was what Gemini braced himself for. He knew that Sky would have to go 
back to Braintech; her father would stop tracking them as long as he had his 
daughter back. According to Maia's visions, it was the only way to get OZONE off 
of their trail once and for all.
Blue sat on a stone awaiting his friends. He saw Maia trying to regain her 
composure, but Nina and Surge-Overload seemed to be having the hardest time. 
He remembered the feeling when he was first teleported here. It seemed like 
several days ago that he traveled to this place for the first time, a city that looked 

 
15
like it had been uninhabited for millions of years. Though it had only been a matter 
of hours since his first visit, Blue struggled to place the experience in relation to 
other recent memories. After arriving in Zalomoni for the first time and engaging 
in a long meeting with Gemini, Jayde and Juno about betrayal, love, responsibility 
and destiny, Blue sort of checked out as the conversation was too deep for him. 
Despite losing track of time, he was able to gather that, in a previous lifetime, Juno 
went AWOL after Nina’s death in Egypt, slaying countless Supernaturals out of 
blind vengeance. Apparently, Nina and Juno had been watching each other die for 
many lifetimes already. Something about this most recent death of her lover had 
made Juno snap, and she had been known as a cruel assassin of Mancers, 
Witchbreed and Jreamers ever since. Jayde didn't like Juno at all. Gemini was 
playing peacekeeper because if Jayde killed Juno, then Nina wouldn't have a 
chance to use her full potential...or something like that.
Blue was eager to see his friends again and stirred in his seat. He finally 
stood up and walked over to Surge-Overload. “You’ll be ok, little girl. The Port 
makes everyone feel like they’re being torn apart, but your body gets used to it 
eventually. You do look kind of frail though, even by human standards.” 
He looked over to Gemini and asked, “What's wrong with her?”
Gemini calmly walked over to Surge-Overload and placed his dark hand on 
her pale forehead. 
“Her body isn't used to the shock of Port Rune traveling. We expect 
everyone to be sick the first few times, and yes, Surge-Overload looks a bit sicker 
than most, but this is completely normal. Your body has been trained by Jayde to 
endure such pains as these and regenerate a lot faster than the rest. Surge-Overload, 
however, has not experienced such physical stress before. She will recover in due 
time.”
“Where is Sky?” Blue looked around as if he'd somehow missed her high 
maintenance, fashionably late entrance.
“She will not be joining us tonight,” Gemini flatly stated as he signaled for 
Jayde to come take Surge to a bed.
“Wait, I think I heard you wrong. Sky won't be joining us?” Blue screamed. 
He watched Juno nurse Nina to a smile and Maia sit up after a long cough.
“That is correct, Blue. OZONE has her.”
“Oh, no. Oh god, no. Okay, we can save her. I'll go back through the Port 
Rune and—”

 
16
“You'll do no such thing. The Pagoda is no more. She will be saved another 
day when it is her time.”
“That's all wrong! Sky needs me! She saved my life!” Blue protested his 
mentor’s decision-making for the first time. He knew it was like talking to a 
marble wall, so he stopped.
Jayde walked over to the small teenaged girl and picked her up as easily as a 
baby. 
“I rarely question your judgment, Gemini, but I am with Blue. Why here and 
why now?” Jayde shot Juno a glance that could kill. 
“You know how I feel about her and the Forbidden City, Zalomoni.”
Gemini looked into the shining emeralds that were Jayde’s eyes before 
turning his attention to the dark sapphires that were Blue’s.
“You will have your answers soon enough. Just trust me.”
Blue couldn’t take any more bad news and ran into the city before anyone 
could see the tears streaming down his face. He was frustrated for feeling so 
helpless in this game of cat and mouse. The lack of control he held over his own 
destiny infuriated him so much that he just ran as fast as his Supernatural speed 
would carry him, and he didn't stop.
*
“I think we have all of the answers we were looking for, Commander in 
Chief,” Grin said, broadly showing his broken yellow teeth. “The girl's memories, 
as hard as they were to pry from her head, were validated. All of the fugitives were
killed in Japan. She will have no memory of the last few weeks as if they never 
existed, thanks to Post-Traumatic Amnesic Response.”
“You'd better be right, Grin, or this is the end of your career!” The 
Commander stormed out of the room with a smirk before turning to his assistant 
with a hardened scowl. “Call the leaders and give them an answer! Tell them 
mission complete.”

 
17
Grin slicked his greasy hair back and bent forward so that his face hovered 
over Sky's sleeping body in creepy observation. “So, you all had a mentor named 
Gemini,” he whispered, “an ancient immortal. I'd love to meet someone like that 
and pick his brain.” His phlegmy laugh was so alarming, the Commander peeked 
into the room to make sure Sky was still in one piece.
“Return the girl to her father and keep a close eye on her. According to you, 
she has no memories, never had powers to begin with, and was only being used for 
ransom purposes because of her father’s association with the company, so she's 
harmless. Besides, we need that crackpot of an inventor to keep making our 
weapons. Don't think about touching her. Send his precious daughter back to him 
under surveillance. Am I understood?”
“Sir! Yes Sir!”

 
18
Chapter 3
ROLE CALL
Gemini is the self-proclaimed advisor to the powers that be. In this life, it happens 
to be a group of misfit Mancer kids and their old friends, the Coven of Unity. He is 
a Duomancer, an elite Supernatural with unknown powers of both destruction and 
creation who can split his form into two separate beings. He is a mastermind with 
an agenda that has yet to be uncovered, though his allies seem to trust him 
implicitly. He is as old as Planet Earth itself. 
Jayde is the right hand enforcer of Gemini. Her long black hair, milky Asian skin 
and green eyes only distract her enemies. She is one of the most dangerous beings 
to ever walk the earth. Once revered as the Goddess of Martial Arts and Grace, 
Jayde is a Metamancer, a Supernatural with control over every cell in her body. 
She taught the world the very first forms of martial arts, now called the Lost Arts. 
Currently, Jayde must rethink the vows of peace she took in Zalomoni as the war 
to save the world approaches.
Blue is a Soulstreamancer, also known as a Bad Boy and high school drop-out who 
killed his family by accident when his powers were unlocked. Now he is being 
trained by Gemini for a vital role in the fight against OZONE, though he doesn’t 
know exactly what this role will be. His abilities include hyper speed, regeneration, 
and immortality as well as the unique power to open the Soulstream. He finds out 
through memories and meditation that he is one of the gods responsible for the 
destructive world today and the creation of OZONE itself.
Maia is the intelligent home-schooled girl from a religious family. Through her 
worst nightmares, she has come to understand her identity as the only 
Celestiamancer, but the very one responsible for the disappearance of an entire 
civilization. She has been working closely with Gemini, developing her psychic 
and oracular abilities, which include telepathy, scrying, healing and clairvoyance. 
While her powers have yet to show any limitations, Maia suffers from self-doubt 

 
19
like many other sixteen-year-olds. She possesses a mysterious metaphysical 
connection to blood that she has yet to understand, but it has served as one of her 
greatest strengths.
Sky is the spoiled and beautiful fashion icon born into a life where there is no limit 
to luxury. Her Jewish father is the founder of the most technologically advanced 
company in the world, and her African supermodel mother is the heiress to a 
throne of diamonds. Sky has found out that she is a Spheromancer who can see in 
all directions at once while controlling anything within the biosphere she creates, 
including weather, sound, gravity, time, space and health. However, she doesn’t 
believe her identity as a Supernatural, which stunts her progress. Recently, after 
understanding her culpability for the fragile state of the world today, she has been 
suffering from Post-Traumatic Amnesic Response. She believes she was 
kidnapped by an unknown terrorist organization and has subsequently pushed the 
memories out of her head in order to live a normal life again. 
Kaery is the sixteen-year-old, golden-eyed all-American girl next door. She 
transferred from New York to San Francisco where she met the people she now 
considers her best friends. Recently, she and her friends were faced with danger as 
they fled a night club and found themselves in a Japanese Pagoda where they all 
discovered their identities as Supernaturals. Kaery has the ability to see, hear and 
speak the truth, no matter the circumstances. She is also a Witchbreed with 
oracular powers very similar to Maia’s, as well as immunity to Magick. Her 
dreams have revealed that Blue was her lover in previous lifetimes. 
Star is one of Kaery’s best friends and a recent addition to the narcissists of the 
world. She found out she is a Witchbreed with the ability to change her appearance 
and that of anyone near her for a temporary amount of time with Glamour Magick. 
Her six-foot frame and red hair stand out as she stomps around like her idol, Sky. 
She is a member of the Coven of Unity, though she doesn’t seem to be concerned 
with the war between Supernaturals and OZONE. Currently, she is far more 
obsessed with Sky’s appearance and the latest fashion trends than she is with the 
wellbeing of the planet.

 
20
Crux is a young Magnemancer and Gypsy from the Petsha family. He was born 
into a life of mystery and intrigue, but it all came to an end when his recently 
murdered uncle, Loiza, saved him from being sacrificed by their own people 
before his thirteenth birthday. He has been saved by Blue and well-trained by 
Jayde, but now that he is squatting at Star’s house, he doesn’t know what to do 
next. One thing he knows for sure is that all four Gypsy families, including the 
Shandor bloodline of Vampyl, are hunting him down in order to fulfill an ancient 
prophesy. He has refined his powers to control the density, velocity and 
momentum of small bits of matter. He can essentially turn any minute object into a 
missile of deadly force, and with his limited control of probability, he can strike 
targets that would otherwise be impossible to hit. With his unique combination of 
powers, he seems extremely lucky to be surviving the war that is in his midst.
Ahlina is the sixteen-year-old punk rock computer hacker, best friend of Kaery, 
Scape and Star. She too has learned of her identity as a Witchbreed and one of the 
nine members of the Coven of Unity, though she doesn’t yet know what kind of 
powers she possesses. All of her regained memories are full of adventure, martial 
arts, explosions and mayhem. She has dreamt of Jayde and the other kids in 
previous lifetimes for as long as she can remember.
Scape is a seventeen-year-old member of the Coven of Unity and a close friend of 
Kaery, Star and Ahlina. He has been regaining his memories of other lifetimes in 
which he was a powerful Druid and Elementalist, serving the gods as a mentor and 
peacekeeper. Currently in high school, he takes his job as Student-Faculty 
Welcoming Committee President very seriously. His dreams are so vivid that he is 
reluctant to share them with his friends.
Surge-Overload is a fourteen-year-old Technomancer with unique and 
unparalleled powers over any and all technological devices. The strongest 
Technomancer to ever live, she has tormented the secret society that currently rules 
most of the planet, OZONE. She recently stole a laundry list of their files, 
including conceptual designs for technological advancements as well as her 
favorite hack, a complete list of OZONE Agents and their information. When she 
found her parents and uncle on this roster, her world came crashing down, and 
OZONE tried to exterminate her. After her rescue by the Supernaturals, she is now 

 
21
with Gemini and his followers, learning how to use her abilities properly in order 
to fight OZONE once and for all.
Guardian is the world’s number one secret agent and assassin for OZONE. His 
high marks have time and again validated his status and title. Demonstrating the 
prowess of his enhanced strength, catlike agility, predatory stealth, Olympic speed 
and unparalleled marksmanship, Guardian has secured his position as the most 
deadly human being on the planet. This unique skillset supported his undying faith 
in patriotic American life until OZONE sent him on a mission to kill an innocent 
Supernatural, Isja, which caused him to question his employer for the first time. It 
happened once again when they sent him to kill the international terrorist threat, 
Surge-Overload, who turned out to be his niece. Guardian has been hiding his own 
secret from everyone, including OZONE, his identity as a Jreamer capable of 
adopting a limited range of supernatural abilities from each victim he eliminates. 
Juno is the deceptively beautiful super assassin who earned her title as the second 
most deadly agent in the world until she went rogue on OZONE. Having murdered 
countless Supernaturals to appease OZONE in previous lifetimes, she is now trying 
to redeem herself in the eyes of Jayde and Gemini. She is a Witchbreed who 
concealed her true identity from her employers while spending countless years in 
search of Nina. Now that she has found her Mancer lover, she will do her best to 
help Nina harness her supernatural abilities to fight against the threat no one 
understands quite as well as she does, OZONE. She has mastered her Spellcrafts to 
mimic the natural abilities of a Jreamer almost identically. 
Nina is the innocently naïve Mancer in the group thus far with the power to control 
the emotions of others. Being a Neuromancer comes with an appropriate cost, as 
she barely has any control over such an ambiguous power. She doesn’t know why 
she seems to be the only one who trusts Juno, but she does, and that is something 
she will have to explore in the future. She is currently a refugee living in Zalomoni 
with other the other kids who escaped an OZONE assassination. 
Ewm, pronounced ‘Oh-M,’ is the youngest member of the Coven of Unity. He is 
an extremely intelligent nine-year-old with a knack for anything Magickal. He was 
born blind, but with his enlightenment came a blessing: anything Magickal he can 

 
22
see and understand, never forgetting a Rune or other entity created by or blessed 
with Magick. His black hair and red eyes are slightly creepy in contrast to his pale 
skin, and he is obsessed with any Magickal tactic that is offensive in nature. 
Ssiah is Ewm’s twin and elder sister by one minute and thirty seconds. Her parents 
adopted her because she was the loveliest looking baby with beautiful hazel eyes. 
She almost seemed like an angel, and this wasn’t too far from the truth. She is a 
member of the Coven of Unity who can read the secret language of the Aenjelhn, 
or as humans call them, angels. She has been able to summon and control powerful 
Aenjelhn throughout many lifetimes. 
Khenebu is a Witchbreed member of the Coven of Unity who has learned the 
Necromantic Arts as well as the Quantum Shadow Arts. He lived in Egypt until he 
accidentally released a creature that led him to San Francisco where he meets 
Kaery, Ahlina, Star, Never and Scape. This mysterious teenager is a lot smarter 
than he lets on, as he has been enlightened since his fifteenth birthday. 
Never is a part of the Equality Program as a low income student at the same prep 
school that Scape, Ahlina, Star and Kaery attend. He is teased and ignored by most 
of the students in the prestigious school until he finds out that he is a member of 
the Coven of Unity. He is able to morph into a dragon of epic proportions and use 
Dragon Magick. 
Heaven is one of the nine members of the coven of Unity who can use the powers 
of the Fae to control her own physical shape as well as the conditions of the 
environment in close proximity. Her extraordinary urge to be with the Coven of 
Unity caused her to flee her utopia, Ballade, the eighth continent and homeland of 
the Faeries. She was washed up along the coast of Europe where she has been held 
captive by the Shandor, a bloodline of Vampyl Gypsy assassins and reality shifters. 
Prince Lord Ka is the ancient King of the most deadly Vampyl on the planet, 
including the Shandor, and also one of the masterminds who rule OZONE. His 
dark power and presence is felt and feared among the shadows across the planet.

 
23
Chass is the Vampyl Prince and Executioner for none other than Prince Lord Ka as 
well as leader of the Gypsy family known as the Shandor. His assassin skills are 
known throughout the world, and his equally charismatic personality serves him 
well. This relentless predator has been sent on many missions, and his current 
objective is to find and eliminate the Mancer Crux. After intercepting the boy’s 
whereabouts, Chass almost met his own death when he clashed with Mancers he 
thought had long been dead, Blue and Sky. Among his most trusted Shandor 
Generals are Sasha, Emilian, Jilian and Poet.
Deme-God is the human Technomage and self-proclaimed ultimate hacker. This 
teenager was adopted by OZONE at an early age and now spends his time hunting 
down terrorists and other threats to the organization. His most recent triumph was 
the alleged murder of his Nemesis, Surge-Overload. He is a Broken Human with 
the power to use Magick over the ether as well as technology itself with 
specialized Spellcrafts and rituals. He leads a surprisingly terrifying group of 
Technomages, a recent phenomenon in Supernatural society. 
Neflym is one of the masterminds behind OZONE. He is a timeless Immortal with 
unimaginable battle prowess. His schemes are said to be the very ones that caused 
OZONE to emerge in the first place. For countless millenniums, he has been 
lurking in the shadows, hunting any Supernaturals he deems a threat.
Grin is a Broken Human known as a Psiel. His abilities to read minds, cause 
hallucinations and melt brainwaves are frightening to humans and deadly to 
Supernaturals as he tracks and hunts both populations for the glory of OZONE. A 
devout elite Agent and equally creepy partner to Guardian, Grin stops at nothing to 
fulfill his objectives. His latest assignment is to observe Sky under the guise of a 
bodyguard. 
Narles was one of OZONE’s rising stars until he recently botched a mission, 
losing all of his limbs in an explosion. His heart is devoted to the glory of OZONE, 
and he wishes for nothing more than to be Guardian’s equal. His military 
background and well-rounded skills on the battlefield have won him many awards, 
and he considers himself OZONE’s ultimate soldier. With his Christian worldview, 

 
24
he hunts down anyone and anything to appease his Commanders in the name of
Christ. 
Loiza is the uncle of Crux and a Gypsy assassin from the Petsha clan. His abilities 
to cleave through any known target brought him notoriety from all four Gypsy 
families. Recently he was killed by his cousin, Chass Shandor, while trying to 
protect Crux. 
Reynolds and Lisa are the parents of Surge-Overload and covert spies for 
OZONE. This power couple lays claim to master’s degrees, PhD’s and hours of 
rigorous training as well as diverse experience in the field, making them a 
partnership to beat. They take their job seriously and to this day, don’t know that 
their daughter, Cindy, is actually Surge-Overload. They continue to work their 
hardest to complete any mission set before them or die trying, according to the 
OZONE creed.
Samuel Bradford is the CEO and founder of the world’s most technologically 
advanced empire, Braintech, which manufactures and creates everything from cell 
phones to rockets. Having believed there was no task too difficult for him to 
accomplish until his daughter Sky came along, his goal is to keep her safe. He is a 
genius from a long line of Jewish scientists. His skills have been commissioned by 
corporations all over the world, including OZONE, to develop equipment and 
weapons that will destroy Supernatural threats. 
Orchid Bradford is the supermodel and heiress to the largest diamond mines in 
Africa who married Samuel Bradford and bore their child, Sky. She has taught her 
daughter that etiquette, fame, style, glamour and luxury are not just gifts, but ways 
of life. Ruling her household with an iron fist, this savvy business woman loves to 
be underestimated. 
Aiel is the thirteen-year-old who, through Immaculate Conception, bore a child 
named Isja. She thinks that the baby is dead, but he has actually been given to 
another family who will raise him as their own. Aiel feels deep in her heart that 
Isja is still alive, but she doesn’t have the proof just yet. 

 
25
Isja is the only Mancer with power over Groves and sanctuaries. He is that baby 
born into a world of war time and again, yet he is always raised to be peaceful. He 
has recently been murdered by Guardian and reincarnated as Aiel’s newborn son. 

 
26
Chapter 4
YOUR LIPS TO MINE
(Pangaea: 250,010,000 B.C.E.)
Blue kissed Kaery as if she were the only girl in the world, his arms 
wrapping around her like a blanket of protection. The weather was perfect, but he 
shielded her from any cold that might have come between their bodies. His lips 
were soft yet strong, and Kaery loved every second of their touch to hers. She 
reached up and stroked Blue’s cheek while kissing him. If she stopped thinking of 
where she was and who she was, she could easily get lost in his embrace.
“I can’t do this anymore, Blue. The world knows you as Aegis, the almighty 
Mancer, but I know you as my secret lover. You kiss me like we’re the only thing 
that matters, but we both know that’s a lie.” 
Kaery made a futile attempt to pull herself away from the strong embrace of 
her lover, though she didn’t want to be away from him at all. 
“You are all that matters, Kaery. You know I would change things if I 
could.”
Blue leaned toward Kaery’s lips for a short kiss, and as his breath passed 
over them, a chill rippled down her spine. 
“It would change the world if I told them that I love you, Kaery.” 
“I don’t want you to do that for me. I’m not worth the trouble,” Kaery said 
defiantly as she pushed Blue back and turned to walk away. 
Kaery almost turned to face Blue, but knowing his eyes were always her 
weakness in the past, she decided against it.
“I wouldn’t want your perfect world to be corrupted by the truth.” 
In the corner of her eye Kaery saw Never staring at her from the edge of the 
forest. She gasped in horror. Now she knew she had hurt Never’s feelings. He was 
quiet, but always looked at her with endearment in his eyes. She simply did not 
feel the same way. He must have been told to get her for the ceremony. She had to 
see Blue one more time before telling him that she couldn’t be his mistress 
anymore. He was married to Sky, and though he claimed it was an arranged 

 
27
marriage to keep the world balanced, she wanted to be the woman at his side. Even 
worse, she also knew that he did in fact love Sky.
Kaery could only stare into the pain-stricken black eyes of her long-time 
friend, Never, and hope that he understood she didn’t mean to hurt him. For a brief 
moment that seemed to last lifetimes, his pain was reflected onto her soul. She 
wanted to reach out and say something, but before she could say a word, Blue’s 
hand was on her shoulder. She turned to face Blue, staring into his sapphire eyes so 
that he would not see who it was that had truly startled her. 
“I don’t mean to hurt you, Kaery. I just want us to be together. I know you 
want to be with me, but this is all I can do,” Blue said with sincerity as he caressed 
her soft tanned skin. His touch and his handsome face made Kaery drop her guard 
for only a brief moment before she looked back to see that Never was no longer 
there. 
“I can’t do this anymore, Blue! Maybe we’ll be together in another 
lifetime!” She pushed him away and left the shadows of the forest for the warm 
sun, crying, not looking back this time. 
* 
(Present-Day)
Never woke in his room and looked around to see posters of rock bands and 
movies. He instantly realized he had been dreaming of Kaery and Blue again. He 
was immediately aware of the present with the absence of beautifully crafted stone 
walls and perfect weather, now replaced with smelly clothes and stale air in this 
small stuffy room. Even though he was a bit of a hoarder, Never knew where 
everything was in his room. It was messy by definition, but he managed to reach 
over to find the exact black t-shirt he was thinking about wearing today. 
His motivation to go to school had nothing to do with academics. In fact, he 
only went to school to see the girl of his dreams, quite literally. Kaery had been 
going to his school for over a year now. She had transferred from New York, and 
he had been studying her every move since, thinking that her existence was a 

 
28
hallucination until he heard her laughing with her friends at their usual spot in the 
courtyard. 
Never turned to look at his cluttered room, the video games piled on top of 
fantasy books piled on top of half-eaten bowls of food. He half-heartedly wanted to 
clean the room, but thought against it in a millisecond and closed the door behind 
him as he crept through his stepfather’s apartment. His mother was asleep in the 
room, while his stepfather was half-perched on the couch, an open can of beer in 
the clutches of his fat grubby fingers.
Never did his best to walk through the creaky apartment with as much 
stealth as he could. He had enough practice dodging his new stepfather that it was 
almost a skill. He didn’t even bother to go near the kitchen because more than 
likely, there was no food. He’d make it to school for breakfast and hopefully get a 
glimpse of Kaery. He thought about quietly closing the door behind him, then 
smiled deviously and slammed it on purpose. He could hear his stepfather drop the 
can, which sounded more like he was throwing it across the room in alarm that the 
police were busting the door down again. 
This wasn’t the first morning Never ran to school with the simple 
amusement that the slob had beer spilled all over his face, which was enough to 
make him smile. It took his thoughts off of the pain he woke up with after having 
his heart broken countless times in his dreams. He thought about how silly he must 
be for feeling betrayed by a girl he had never seen before, a girl who had 
transferred to his school about the same time that violent grease monkey 
disappeared.
He wasn’t even sure Kaery had met Blue until the day he overheard her 
talking to Ahlina and Star. She was asking about the cute boy with blue eyes sitting 
in Principal Hatcher’s office. Never stopped running and gasped as he missed the 
BART train in the subway. 
“That was when the dreams started. The day I saw her get out of the car with 
her mom,” Never said to himself aloud.
He looked around, realizing he wasn’t the only person standing on the 
subway platform, and pulled his hood over his blond bed head. 
He was looking down at his skater shoes that were in dire need of a cleaning 
when the door to the train opened and a nostalgic dreamlike feeling hit him like a 
slap to the face. He looked to see Kaery’s face as if he’d dreamt her up. It really 
was her, walking off the train toward him. He didn’t know what to say or do, 

 
29
unable to keep himself from looking into her golden eyes, reveling in her AsianCaucasian features and smooth skin. 
Kaery looked at Never as if she didn’t know who he was, but somehow 
recognized him. She was in a hurry to meet her friends at the diner a few blocks 
away. She was skipping school because she had been told that one of her best 
friends, Star, would be providing them with an update on her recent and mysterious 
disappearance. 
Never didn’t board the train like the others on the platform who walked 
around him as Kaery brushed past, smiling politely. He could swear that God was 
playing a trick on him at this exact moment, so he decided to do what he would 
normally do: follow her. 
* 
Kaery was not in the frame of mind to pay attention to anything or anyone as 
she rushed past the busy morning commuters. Most of the people in San Francisco 
at this time of morning were either en route to their favorite coffee shop or just 
leaving their favorite coffee shop before a long day at work. Either way, she was 
focused on meeting with her friends, who would never in their wildest dreams 
believe her vision from last night. 
The walk was only a few blocks, but it gave Kaery time to gather her 
thoughts. Last night, she dreamt that she was out at the Forbidden City nightclub 
with Ahlina and Scape when she saw Blue and Star walk in with V.I.P. status. The 
sight wouldn’t have been so bizarre if her nerd friend Star didn’t look like a 
supermodel and if Blue was actually still alive. He had been reported dead after a 
freak accident over a year ago. To make things weirder, Kaery had been talking to 
the newly glamorous Star in the restroom when guns started firing and a martial 
arts juggernaut went ‘all action movie’ on the club’s bouncers. When they fled, 
Kaery and her friends ended up in a Japanese pagoda, running into the most 
popular girl in school, Sky, who had been reported missing on the news.

 
30
The more Kaery thought about the dream, the less she thought her friends 
would believe her, knowing they would be prone to laugh while asking what she’d 
been smoking. 
“It really happened!” Kaery exclaimed while navigating the crowded 
sidewalk. As soon as the words left her lips, she noticed a mop of messy blond hair 
in the corner of her eye. Is that the skater boy I just saw in the subway? Never 
mind, just keep walking. Everyone’s waiting at the diner. The more she thought 
about the feelings and the progression of the night, the more she began to realize, if 
it wasn’t true, then I wouldn’t be able to say it. 
Ever since she’d come to California, she’d been unable to lie. She couldn’t 
even hear lies from other people most of the time. She didn’t try to do this, and it 
had been more of a hindrance to her social life than anything else. These days, she 
only spoke to her best friends. Like some kind of crazy psychic, Kaery had been 
having dreams that kept her in a daze. It’d been harder than ever for her to 
concentrate on tasks at hand in the present, and her mother was beginning to think 
she had ADHD. 
She suddenly had the distinct feeling that someone was watching her, so she 
stopped mid-stride to turn and look. The tourists and commuters walked at their 
usual fast pace, ignoring the homeless panhandlers near the building entrances. She 
looked past the crowded street to see the skyscrapers in Union Square, gave a little 
shrug and turned in the opposite direction to continue her trek. She could see the 
diner, and the thought of being near her beloved friends brought a smile to her 
face. 
Kaery could feel it happening again. Her memories came flooding back, her 
steps growing lighter with each passing second. She was losing touch with reality, 
and she was deep in thought, even though she was only a few steps from the door 
of the diner. She was standing in the crosswalk, and her vision was beginning to 
blur. She knew this feeling of déjà vu. 
* 

 
31
Kaery opened her eyes from what seemed like the most bizarre dream ever. 
She didn’t want to move because, despite all of its craziness, she still wanted to 
remember every bit of it. This was something that had to be recorded in her journal 
as soon as possible, but first she wanted to bask in the excitement of it all. When I 
tell Ahlina about the dream, she’s going to freak out and probably make me write a 
blog about it on Surge-Overload’s hacker site she made me sign up for. There was 
the Forbidden City, Star all gorgeous, guns, escape, a pagoda and Blue. Ahlina 
will never believe this! The flashback of ancient times seemed like something out 
of a fantasy novel that had never been written, yet Kaery somehow knew it was all 
true. 
It was too early to reach over and grab her cell phone, but Kaery could hear 
it vibrating on the small table next to her bed. The room was messy, which was the 
usual indicator that Ahlina had visited and gone through her clothes again, trying 
to make sure she grabbed the most appropriately inappropriate outfit to wear. 
Kaery looked away from the clean clothes mingled with the messy ones. Her hands 
instinctively grabbed the phone and touched the screen to activate the monitor. 
Almost a dozen text messages were in her inbox and just as many voicemails. This 
was weird, considering the only people who ever messaged Kaery were her mother 
and best friends. 
She opened the first text in her inbox which, as expected, was from Ahlina: 
‘Girl where you at???’ This message wasn’t out of the ordinary and neither were 
the ones that said ‘wake up’ or ‘look we need to talk lol.’ The message that sent a 
tingle down her spine was the one that read, “Ok so I had this dream—I think 
you’re gonna want to hear about this one.’ Kaery immediately sat up in her bed. 
What almost made her scream was the tight and way-too-short silver dress 
she suddenly realized was covering her body. Even more surprising was the 
moment she jumped out of bed in stilettos she must have forgotten how to walk in, 
tripping over the pile of clothes on the floor. Who in the world made me wear these 
things? Ahlina! Kaery tore the shoes off. Now barefoot and running to her 
bathroom, she looked in the mirror at the purple bangs flowing in front of her face 
and the bumped up-do of her brown locks in the back that made her hairstyle look 
like the work of a professional, just as Scape would’ve recommended. The dress 
was way too tight and pushed things way up. Her mom would kill her if she saw 
her looking like that. She turned the handle to let water flow into the sink, glanced 
up and noticed the massive amounts of eyeliner and lip gloss on her face. 

 
32
“This couldn’t be right. That totally happened? It wasn’t a dream,” Kaery 
whispered to herself, almost horrified that she knew the truth. 
“Kaery, is that you?” 
Oh, shoot, my mom can’t see me like this. Kaery scrambled to take the tight 
dress off and even tried to rip it off. Ahlina was going to pay for making her wear 
this outfit. She couldn’t get it off in time for the inevitable invasion that her mother 
was notorious for, considering she was an only child with a control freak for a 
single parent. 
“Yes, Mom, give me a minute, please.” She knew she hadn’t bought herself 
more than three seconds, so she pulled the thin straps over her golden tanned 
shoulders and quickly wrapped a bath towel around her body while washing her 
face. 
Almost on cue, her mother burst into her bathroom with her business suit on 
and a fresh glass of lemonade in hand because she knew Kaery didn’t like orange 
juice. 
“I didn’t even hear you come in last night. And I have to say that little punk 
rocker friend of yours surprised me. I thought you were staying over at her house, 
but when she called to tell me she was going to drop you off, I simply didn’t 
believe my ears. Oh, and don’t think this gets you off the hook for skipping class, 
young lady.” She stopped in her tracks as she looked her daughter up and down.
Kaery’s heart almost stopped as her mother stared at her. She just knew she 
was caught. It would break her mother’s heart to know that her only daughter was 
out and about like some prostitute, dodging bullets at a gangster’s hangout. The 
guilt of it all almost made Kaery confess her sins to her mother, who had been 
raised in a church that condemned divorce, yet had ended the marriage to her 
Southeast Asian father. Her mom was beautiful in all of her business attire that 
Kaery could never see herself wearing. She stood and stared silently, holding the 
glass of lemonade. 
Mo, as her colleagues called her, was known for her quick assessments of 
people. Maybe she had passed that gift to her daughter, who could profile someone 
in a matter of seconds without second-guessing her judgments. She stared at her 
daughter in awe. 
Kaery could barely take the anticipation of the situation and started to tear 
up, as she wanted to spill the details of what seemed like a dream. 

 
33
“Mom, I’m sorry. We went to this club, and then these people started to 
shoot, and right as that happened, this Kung Fu lady saved us, but we were driven 
to this creepy warehouse where the doors opened to a Japanese castle, and it was 
so beautiful that I had to walk closer, except I had this dream about Pangaea, but I 
don’t know if it really existed at all because I caused the end of the world…” 
Kaery barely got to take a breath before her mother smiled. 
“Why are you smiling?” Kaery said, looking at her mother with an

“I didn’t hear a word you said young lady, but I do know that my little girl is

Mo was proud of her only daughter, and it would seem that Kaery’s

bathroom to give Kaery a hug. 
“I, um, don’t know what to say.”
Kaery hugged her proud mother, who stood considerably taller than her on a 
normal day, but towered above her when wearing heels. She was still confused. 
She knew she’d told her mother the entire truth, and on any other occasion, she 
would be riding her about everything from grades to tardiness, but this honest 
confession of last night got her a hug. She must have been dreaming, and even if 
by chance she hadn’t, Ahlina definitely wouldn’t believe this. 
“Mom, I need to take a shower.”
“Oh, of course, sweetie. Go take a shower and hurry up for breakfast. I’ll

bedroom, regaining her perfectionist’s eye for detail.
“Wash the rest of that eyeliner off, and clean this room as soon as you get 
home.”
Kaery looked down at the mess, then back at her mother and gulped.
“Ok, for sure.” 
She watched her mom close the door and ran into the bedroom, the towel 
almost slipping off as she grabbed her phone. A new text message from Star: ‘So I 
have this grungy kid here —with Ahlina and Scape at the diner on Mission—meet 
us here in an hour.’
Kaery replied as quickly as possible with a simple ‘kk’ and returned to the 
bathroom for her shower.

Chapter 5
CENTER OF THE STORM
Sky walked out of the room, not so much frustrated with Maia and her 
doomed visions as she was weary of her own fate. She walked to the courtyard 
where many of their endless training sessions had taken place. The stone garden 
was rearranged in small ways that Sky wouldn’t have noticed before she arrived at 
Gemini’s lair. Now she was able to see the entire reconstructed stone garden as if 
she had created every grain of sand and rock within it all at once. She knew 
everything that was out of place. As uncanny as this ability was, she was not able 
to fathom how Maia could see the many different versions of the future, and she 
was upset that all of the training for the last few weeks seemed to be for nothing. 
There was a beautiful mountain range in the distance. As fast as she could think of 
it, she appeared on her favorite perch high on a mountain top, happy that she was 
able to use her powers so effortlessly.
Ok, this is how Maia can do it, just like the rest of us, with a thought and a 
feeling. I still don’t understand a lot of this Soulstream stuff. It makes me mad that 
I’m forced to be this Mancer thing, but according to Maia, we are all going to die 
anyway. Why don’t I just go home and take my chances with OZONE, considering 
it doesn’t matter if they kill me. At least I’ll be able to see my friends again, and as 
much as school was an anxiety attack in a bottle…I miss that place too. 
Wow, the clouds up here are so gorgeous! It’s so peaceful, and to think, this 
is all I had to do back in Cali to clear my thoughts. All that money spent on yoga 
and meditation masters was for nothing. I miss my Mum and Daddums. They must 
be worried sick. I recorded a video, and Surge sent it to my Dad’s private files to 
let them know I’m ok. I know I must have put us at risk, but I couldn’t help it after 
she saw me trying to call him on the global phone Dad customized for me. I can’t 
just act like my parents don’t exist, as these guys seem to do. I really want to go 
shopping. I’m so tired of being here. 
“I wonder if I can sit on the clouds?” Sky asked herself out loud.

 
41
“Of course you can!” Blue said, slightly panting. He had run up the 
mountain and was climbing the last set of stones. “You know I hate heights after 
falling off that stupid mountain a couple times.” 
“You mean knocked off. And how did you know I was up here?” Sky 
laughed. She had almost forgotten how fast Blue could move.
“As much as you want to be this unpredictable trendsetter, you’re pretty easy 
to read. You’re a big softy, and you come up here and stare at the clouds when 
you’re frustrated.” Blue smiled.
“Oh, so now you know me?” Sky crossed her arms and rolled her eyes as 
she turned away from him dramatically. 
“I never claimed to know you,” Blue said, “but I have noticed you like the 
scent of Jasmine and the color green. You look at old pictures in the phone you 
think you’re hiding from Gemini to make yourself happy. You like this spring 
breeze at night, but in the morning, you like it slightly warmer after your bath. You 
make those outfits out of the dresses in your room when you can’t sleep. Oh, and 
you think I’m a loser because I don’t have a trust fund,” Blue said, chuckling.
Focusing on Blue’s profile as he looked off into the distance of the endless 
clouds, Sky blushed. A moment later, she looked away and refrained from meeting 
his gaze as she watched a storm forming over the ocean in the distance. 
“I wonder what’s in the center of the storm.”

“I think they should show us what they can do,” Sky said to Blue as they

“What do you propose?” Blue asked, smiling at his wife. 
“I want to see just how crafty the Wicasht are! I mean, their spell

The beautifully ornate armor covered the huge man’s body like Dragon scales as it

“You summoned me, Lord Aegis.” Crux kneeled in respect.
“No need for formalities, dear Crux.” Blue walked over to his friend and

Crux was one of the most powerful warriors in all of Pangaea. He was 
undefeated in the gladiator arena and had been appointed as Blue’s general after 
singlehandedly defeating a dozen Jreamers with nothing but grains of sand and his 
bare hands.
Blue didn’t consider too many people his friends, yet was proud of his 
immortal comrade. He had been raised to know only the hierarchy of the 
Supernaturals. He definitely didn’t have any human friends himself, but Crux 
seemed to take a liking to them. 
“I apologize for summoning you from your human audience, but I need to 
ask you for a favor, good friend. We are starting a new arena!” 
Crux clenched his fists and smiled a large grin that Blue knew all too well.
“Before you get too excited, please tell Hermes and the other messengers to 
gather the Wicasht covens and Mancers at once. There will be battles, and my wife 
will announce the rules.” Blue stopped himself from laughing as he saw the 
disappointed expression on his friend’s face. 
Sky saw the same look.
“I’m sorry, Crux,” she said, “but these battles will be about Magick and 
mastery over it. I grow tired of the gladiators who have only mastered the body.”
“I understand, milady.” Crux kneeled again before Sky.
“Please don’t do that. You’re a Mancer!”
“Yes, milady, I will go at once.” 
Crux stood, towering over the most powerful couple in all of Pangaea. He 
smiled, as he respected them more than they knew. 
“Thank you for trusting me with this news first, for I will not fail.”

Chapter 6
DREAMS ARE MEMORIES
When we were all in the Soulstream, we were able to create and destroy 
worlds with but a thought. These worlds still exist across the planes of existence, 
but we choose to forget them when we enter another world as an inhabitant. When 
every Shard, or ‘soul,’ of the Soulstream choose to be born human, they choose 
ignorance over the omnipotence they once had in previous lifetimes. While 
Mancers choose to hold unlimited power, Witchbreed choose to remember. 
Witchbreed are unlike other Supernaturals for the simple fact that we remember 
every lifetime we’ve lived up until this moment. We have dreams much like a 
human would during sleep or meditation, except we open the floodgate of 
remembrance. Our past reincarnations make it hard to discern this lifetime from 
others. The visions we see at night are not fantastical depictions of subjective 
experience, but true historical accounts. 
Once we are enlightened, we retrieve our memories of all past lives before 
this one. The more powerful we become, the more memories we retrieve. Wicasht 
is our true name. Witchbreed is the translation. 
Many Spellcrafts are directly linked to memories of the past. Our 
understanding of the code known as Magick makes us both dangerous and 
necessary. Even the newest Witchbreed have the potential to do things that 
Mancers can only imagine. An intimate connection to the Spellcrafts follows the 
Witchbreed in the Soulstream throughout the ages, from one life to the next. 
Some of us gain our memories from other lifetimes simply by touching a 
familiar object or seeing someone who brings a sense of nostalgia. Though some 
would say it is a curse to remember so much, others say this historical experience 
of déjá vu is a blessing.
Most of our past lifetimes were filled with moments that store themselves on 
our Shards as codes. A code written on the soul of each person is easily seen by 
other Witchbreed and explains the unique Spellcrafts and knowledge developed by 
that individual. 
Humans mistake the realms they once created or destroyed as dreams and 
nightmares. Witchbreed know these dreams as memories, parts of each person that 
are as real as the world around us. 

 
48
Underestimation of the Witchbreed’s link to these memories has been the 
downfall of countless other Supernaturals. 
~ Ssiah

Star was previously the nerdy, wannabe diva with the hunched-over walk 
and the low self-esteem to go with it. Her obsession with Sky, the school’s most 
popular girl, had resulted in a dramatic physical transformation, as she was now a 
red-headed bombshell glamazon with an attitude that rivaled those of the tabloid 
divas. 
Then there was Kaery, the sweetest girl Scape knew. She was the most 
honest person he’d ever met, a true friend who would much rather not say anything 
than hurt someone’s feelings. Scape adored her. If he wasn’t gay, he’d have a 
tough time trying to decide which one of these girls he’d try to sleep with.
Recently they’d found themselves in more of a mess than the time Ahlina 
got caught shoplifting at the mall while skipping fourth period. Thanks to the 
ranting of a little boy who claimed to be a Mancer, or the storybook equivalent of a 
God, they all seemed to be convinced of their part in some supernatural 
community. While this boy seemed to be nothing more than the God of 
hamburgers at the moment as he stuffed his face, he insisted that Witchbreed are 
supposedly Magick-wielding badasses from all throughout history, responsible for 
advising the most powerful of kings, chiefs, Gods and immortals. 
Somehow, through the immense burger scarfing, they were supposed to 
believe that the cute little kid, Crux, was not lying to them or making them look 
like fools. He really had no perceivable motive to lie to them, and his story was 
surprisingly convincing. Scape couldn’t help but think about all of the dreams he’d 
been having lately. The expressions on his friends’ faces confirmed they had been 
having similar dreams as well. 
Scape sat quietly at the table in the busy restaurant and watched as Kaery, 
Ahlina and Star listened to the boy, who continued his tale as if he weren’t in a 
restaurant full of potential eavesdroppers.
