Nina didn’t have a name for what she was, until recently. ‘Supernatural,’ 
Gemini called her. She grew up in a small town in Oregon, and she’d visited the 
big cites growing up with a father who quit working as a truck driver in favor of 
carpentry. She’d never been able to appreciate the beautiful architecture of these 
cities because whenever her father would take her on a tour, she would become 
overwhelmed by the presence of so many people in one area. At an early age, she 
became aware of the strange ability she had to influence and manipulate the 
emotions of other people in her presence. 
Now she was inside a Japanese pagoda, somewhere she’d only imagined. 
What she didn’t know was that she was a Neuromancer, one of the most powerful 
beings on the planet, who had been revered as a Goddess in other lifetimes. 
She sat in solitude wondering about her life’s sudden change, but after 
speaking to the cyber-hacking Surge-Overload, the introverted Maia, and the 
beautiful Sky, she realized her life wasn’t so much different than theirs. Of course, 
she didn’t have the religious background that Maia came from, the eccentric 
suburban life of Surge-Overload, and most definitely not the trillionaire stardom of 
Sky, but like them, she had been thrown out of her comfort zone. 
This was the first time Nina had been able to sit by herself, though she was 
supposed to be sleeping. For some reason, she couldn’t make herself doze off, too 
busy thinking of Juno, the woman who had rescued her. Juno was gorgeous in the 
‘murder-you-for-breakfast’ kind of way. She seemed to make the kids uneasy. Sky 
once said Juno had tried to kill her, but Surge argued it was a test. Sky didn’t seem 

 
54
so amused, but Nina remembered seeing Maia chuckle for the first time at that 
conversation. 
Nina could sense the emotions of the kids very easily. Blue was hard to read 
though. He was the bad boy with the attitude, but underneath his hard exterior was 
a nice spirit. He had a conscience that was as obvious as the nickname for his 
bright blue eyes. Sometimes Nina felt as if she knew Blue just from being in the 
same room with him, but she felt the same way about Sky and Juno as well. She 
felt the déjà vu, especially about Juno, as if she’d been waiting her whole life to 
see her for just one moment. 
When Nina was around Juno, she felt as if the world were stopping, as if she 
was supposed to focus on everything this woman said and did. Nina had never felt 
this focused on anything before, but it seemed Juno already knew that about her. 
She could feel Juno’s raw emotions of longing and protection all around her, even 
now as Juno was in conference with Gemini and Jayde. 
Blue advised Nina to sit tight. It didn’t seem very likely they would be 
leaving anytime soon. Juno looked slightly worried as she looked back at Nina 
while walking away with Jayde. Nina felt something she hadn’t felt from another 
woman in a long time, since her mother in fact; she felt loved. Her nerves were 
starting to get the best of her as she felt for Juno’s presence while closing her eyes. 
She felt the presence of Surge-Overload, who was wide awake, judging by the 
excitement in her emotional waves. Surge had a definite emotional signature that 
Nina would never forget. It was both human and sterile like a machine, which was 
extremely unfamiliar, but also refreshing because it wasn’t as complicated as the 
others. Surge-Overload must have been some cyber-handle because no mother in 
her right mind would name her daughter Surge-Overload. But she was so smart, it 
was almost scary. 
Earlier that night while Nina was visiting with Maia and Sky, they looked 
through hacked files on Surge-Overload’s laptop. Nina found out that Surge was 
the one who had changed her information in O.Z.O.N.E’s files, labeling her as 
‘Deceased’ to the world. As unsettling as that may have seemed for most, Nina had 
considered herself dead to the world many years ago at the moment when she 
crawled across the asphalt toward the car where her parents were burning to death. 
The gruesome sight she saw that day made her get up and run. The pain and 
suffering she knew her mother felt during the accident was enough to make Nina 

 
55
feel sick years later as a young adult. Her father had died instantly, but she still 
cried because she felt like the accident was somehow her fault. 
As Nina sat up and wiped tears from her face, she heard the door open. A 
silhouette stood on the threshold, staring at her. She didn’t have to ask who it was 
because she recognized the somewhat shelled emotional state of the popular beauty 
queen, Sky. 
“I thought you’d definitely be asleep by now, considering all you’ve been 
through today.” Sky said, concerned. “I mean seriously, a sniper in the woods, a 
sniper in Union Square, a dying girlfriend and a riot.” Sky sighed. 
“I don’t have a girlfriend,” Nina dared to say meekly as Sky sat next to her 
on the bed an arm’s length away. 
“Sure you don’t. The way that psycho—I mean—the way Juno looks at 
you,” Sky corrected, “you’d think you were in some romantic novel.” 
Sky shook her head as if to remove the mental picture she had just thought 
up. Nina felt the embarrassment slip through Sky’s shell, she herself feeling the 
same embarrassment simultaneously. 
“Where is Juno? I mean—she’s been gone for a while, you know?”
“Yeah, she’s still talking to Gemini and Jayde. Thank God we didn’t get 
caught hacking into O.Z.O.N.E’s files with Surge earlier. It was close, but I think 
Surge blocked our transmission. You do love Juno, don’t you?” Sky popped the 
question so quickly, Nina almost didn’t hear it.
Blushing, she turned to Sky and sat with her back straightened. “How can I 
love her? I don’t know her. And that’s really personal.”
“In the socialite world, that’s what we call a ‘Yes,’ my shy Nina. Oh, and by 
the way, I dreamt about you and how we used to be besties in another lifetime,” 
Sky said, attempting to lighten the mood and change directions. 
Sky had been born into a family with so much power that she the world was 
without boundaries, besides the invisible ones she created. To watch Nina squirm 
was not her intention, so she wanted to talk to this woman whom she felt a sisterlike bond to. She didn’t have any siblings, so this was as awkward a conversation 
for her as it was for Nina.
Nina turned her head to look out the window where she saw a breathtaking 
view of the moon. She didn’t know how she got to Japan, but apparently she had 
teleported to San Francisco, then to Japan with the rest of the kids after the riot. 
Nina had been so scared that night she was found by Juno. Dodging bullets 

 
56
from an OZONE helicopter in the process, Juno told her how to touch a 
‘teleportation Port Rune,’ as she called it. After pronouncing a Magickal word, 
Nina was whisked away to a cave. By the time Juno made it through the Port Rune, 
she had already sustained multiple gunshot wounds. Nina remembered the pain and 
the horror she felt that night, much like the night her parents died. 
“Ok, so maybe I like her,” Nina said, looking down at the scar from a cut on 
her hand, “but I just got out of a terrible relationship with—well never mind.” 
“So I’m guessing your ex was abusive?” Sky asked, “Not only by the pause 
in your words, but by the way you talk about him? Or maybe her?” 
“Her.”
“I thought so, but I didn’t want to offend you or assume anything, and you 
don’t have to be an empath, or whatever you are to feel that tension,” Sky 
continued, “Juno comes along and saves your life, and you feel a bond of 
protection and adrenaline you’ve never felt before?” 
“Well, yes and yes, but maybe my feelings are wrong. I tend to be extreme 
with them. When did this become ‘Profile Nina Day,’ and why do I feel like such a 
moron?” Nina asked, standing up. 
“I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to pry, but I like to see things work out the way 
they’re supposed to. You two seem pretty amazing together, and it’s sorta special, 
so I would totally hold onto it if I were you.” Sky stood and gave Nina an awkward 
hug. 
“Besides, in my dreams, you two were married.” She looked into Nina’s 
blue eyes and said, “Happily, I might add.” 
She turned on her heels and walked out of the room into the hallway of the 
giant Pagoda where she’d been a resident since the night Juno kidnapped her. 
Nina thought about the deep conversation she had with Sky. Now she knew why 
Blue was so distraught about her missing the Port Rune to this ancient city. 
“Happily?” Nina repeated to herself. She planned to hold onto the word like 
her greatest secret.

lips.
“Alright, I’m out,” Never said, noticing the awkward silence from the group. 
He picked his backpack up and ran out of the boutique. His embarrassment wasn’t 
due to the fact that he’d just made himself look like a jealous boyfriend, but rather 
that Kaery had looked at him with such a puzzled expression.
Kaery wanted to follow Never out and tell him that it was ok, that she was 
used to the jokes of the kids. Besides, she liked when they all were together having 
fun, even if it was at her expense. The only thing she could think of was the all too 
familiar energy he gave while protecting her. She knew him, somehow. 
“Crux, what did the Tarot lady say about déjà vu?” Kaery turned her 
attention from the fleeing Never to the small preteen. “Tell me.”
“Cool out,” Crux said defensively.
“Correction, its chill out. If you’re going to use slang, please use it, well—” 
Ahlina was cut off abruptly.
“Whatever!” Crux said with a wrinkled brow. “Anyway, Kaery, she said that 
Wicasht don’t have déjà vu. They only have memories and Enlightenment. Whoa, 
look at me, all Knowledge-boy, as if I paid attention to all of that crone’s lessons.” 
Noticing no one was excited about his recollection of the lesson, Crux 
straightened up his posture and stopped smiling so big. “Erm… she also said that 
when Witchbreed come together, it is no coincidence, but an…”
“An what?” Scape asked, “You can’t just leave a cliffhanger like that!”
“Yeah, this kid is starting to get on my nerves with the cryptic speech.” 
Ahlina said.
“I don’t care about the kid. How do I look, damnit?” Star did a fashion spin 
in the middle of the huddle, pushing Crux to the side. 
Crux took this opportunity to pick-pocket the glamorous teenager in her 
expensive dress. The clutch was in her hand, but it was an easy swipe, just a trick 
of the fingers if she could only get in reach. He knew the kids were focused on the 
black sequined mini-dress as Star’s red locks flew about her like a commercial on 
TV. As she got closer with her ballerina grace in heels only professional models 
wore, he lifted his arm only to feel the sudden clasp of Kaery’s hand over his wrist.
“When Witchbreed come together, it is no coincidence, but an Omen of the 
Covens!” Kaery said, with her eyes hauntingly wide, turning a fiery molten gold. 
“That’s what you were going to say, right?” 

 
62
“That’s exactly what she said. How did you know that?” Crux asked 
quizzically. For a brief moment, he forgot his mission to steal Star’s purse.

 
63
Chapter 9
RUINS OF ZALOMONI
Surge had never hit the ground harder than she did at the outskirts of the 
Forbidden City of Zalomoni. She took a moment to regain her thoughts and made 
sure she wasn’t dead. So that was the second time I teleported in one day. Stuff of 
sci-fi fantasy just got real! It sure isn’t what I expected and, oh my god, I'm 
throwing up again. ‘Stargate’ makes it look so easy. I feel like my insides were 
thrown in a blender to make smoothies for hungry zombies downtown. Wait, where 
am I? This place looks like an ancient city that an even more ancient bomb went 
off in and killed all its citizens! Oh look, there’s Maia. How come she’s walking 
around as if she didn't go through the same teleportation portal I did? I was almost 
this sick the first time I traveled with Sky and the others to the Pagoda. Wow, 
Maia's hair is bouncy!
She's coming closer, and this is embarrassing, crouched over my own lunch. 
Why are all the Mancer girls so pretty, and I'm just so plain? Why do I feel like I'm 
the only one that's ever sick? Where are we? Why can't I feel my hands? Queasy. 
Great, she's helping me up.
“Hi, Maia,” Surge muttered miserably. Her face was more pale than normal 
and had a slight hint of green undertones. She knew that she was easily prone to 
car sickness, but this was the worst feeling she'd ever felt.
“Hello, Surge-Overload,” Maia said, smiling as she helped the young 
teenager to her feet. “I have to show you something, and even though we let you 
regain your composure for a few hours, I think it's time I helped you get over your 
sickness. Gemini says it's quite normal for first-time port runers. Well, in my case I 
didn't so much feel sick as I felt disoriented from the constellation shift...”
“Ok, I get it. I'm the weakest link here. Why did they rush us in that room to 
port anyway?” Surge asked with some irritation. “Oh, wait, never mind, it’s all 
coming back to me now. The OZONE task force was coming for us. Somehow 
they tracked my laptop. My laptop! Where is it? Did anyone get it before we 
rushed into the portal?”
“You were there, Surge. Jayde showed up out of nowhere when you were 
hacking OZONE, then picked us up as if we weighed nothing and ran to the 

 
64
meditation room. We didn't have time to get anything. When we came to the other 
side, we all were in this ruined city.”
Maia looked up at the broken structures in awe. She realized this was an 
ancient civilization that even OZONE had yet to uncover. The architecture looked 
nothing like the buildings of modern times or the pyramids of cultures long past. 
These were domes of gold, metal and stone. They were gigantic in height. Most of 
them had been broken and shattered, as if explosions happened from both inside 
and out. Standing almost as high as the towers were status of champions, warriors 
and monks with ornate armor. They must have taken centuries to carve out of the 
mountains that surrounded the outer parts of the city.
The once-immaculate streets were shattered in most places by what could 
have been an earthquake. Many vines choked the life out of the temple walls, and 
huge leaves the size of cars shaded the grounds where Maia and Surge walked 
slowly toward a temple. Runes of Magick could be half-read under the vines and 
thorns that covered them like a blanket of the forgotten.
There were gashes on the ground as if huge claws had ripped at the very 
stone of this place. Maia was fascinated by the flaws of the city. The closer she 
looked, the more intriguing the story became. An epic battle had been fought here. 
Anyone could see that, but the fight had been devastating for the two sides who 
had clearly underestimated each other. Judging by the sharp wounds in the stone 
foundations, guards had been fighting off giant creatures, attempting to prevent 
destruction of the city, but to no avail. The guards themselves must have held great 
power to keep such gigantic creatures at bay. 
Surge could feel her senses regaining some sense of normalcy. As she felt 
her health being rejuvenated, she saw something miraculous. With every step she 
took with Maia, the stones in the ruined city began to reform into smooth streets, 
and the vines retracted into the ground from whence they came. The ancient 
structures were being brought back to their former glory as people began to fill the 
streets in a panic. Most were dressed in garb that Surge hadn't seen referenced in 
any book or file OZONE kept.
Surge looked at Maia. Her hands were shining light, and her eyes glowed a 
faint blue. Everywhere Maia looked, it seemed she was uncovering the truth of 
what had happened here. Surge wondered if Maia was aware of her powers. Maia's 
hand was still on Surge's waist as they walked toward the temple in front of them. 
It was the largest and most elaborate one in the city. A carving of a Dragon had 

 
65
been ornately crafted on the outside of the temple, its tail and body coiling around 
the building all the way to the top. It made the skyscrapers downtown look like a 
playpen.
Surge continued to view the chaos. Men and women, clad in armor, carrying 
dangerous looking weapons, ran past the two Mancers as if they didn't exist, 
determined to stop the horde of creatures from destroying the city. Surge's heart 
almost stopped as she saw a familiar face. He was just as handsome in this vision 
as he had been in others. Her Uncle was clad in armor that looked so fierce, she 
stepped out of his way, though she assumed this was just a realistic vision that 
couldn't hurt her. The ground rocked back and forth as an earthquake rumbled. 
Maia didn't seem to lose her footing as Surge almost did. Her Uncle ran at inhuman 
speed, a group of men and women close behind him, all holding their weapons fast.
“Maia, what's going on? What is this?” Surge asked, “How did the city go 
from desolate to raging with armor-clad characters from a good MMO game?” 
Surge knew that Maia was in a trance and would not answer her. She still hoped to 
get the answers she longed for.
“This building is huge!” Surge yelled, “What could possibly be in this thing? 
Who could have built it?” Surge looked up until her neck couldn't stretch back 
anymore. Whatever it is, these guards surrounding it lost their formation and are 
headed that way. Surge looked behind her for the first time to see a wall situated 
between two large mountains. Bordering the wall were two male champions, 
wearing distinct suits of armor, but both equally regal-looking. On the wall itself 
was a female wearing a combination of a suit of armor and a dress. The garb 
looked like that of a warrior queen. None of these statues looked remotely familiar, 
yet the place was definitely somewhere that was loved by many. The wall was 
being punished on the other side by who knows what as the dust from the warrior 
queen poured onto the city she protected. Holy crap! That warrior queen is Jayde!
Roars filled the air thicker than the dust as the wall continued to receive 
punishment. The guards that stood on top of the wall shot a myriad of arrows and 
projectiles onto the other side. The howls continued as the onslaught moved the 
very foundation of the beautiful city. Surge stopped moving the moment she saw 
hundreds of giant beasts tearing their way over and through the beautiful wall like 
a flood, destroying everything in their path. 
Surge felt a fear she'd never felt strike the core of her existence. Something 
primal was destroying this city, and it was as well organized as the chaos that 

 
66
created the planet. It ripped relentlessly through the warriors that stood in its way. 
Monsters appeared, seemingly from fairytales. These looked like werewolves, 
werecats and other beings Surge had only seen in files from OZONE. Her uncle 
was wiping out waves of them with his oversized sword as Surge grabbed Maia's 
hand and rushed her into the temple as if they were living in the moment of the 
great battle. She was lost in the vision at this point, forgetting when and where she 
was from.
“We have to get out of here, Maia,” Surge said hurriedly as she ran for 
safety in the temple. She tried not to cry as she thought of her Uncle and his army 
being overwhelmed by the sheer numbers of the creatures attacking. As they 
entered the temple holding hands, they both looked around for answers and safety. 
They noticed a set of steps that split and spiraled toward the top of this temple.
As Surge and Maia got to the base of the steps, another familiar face was 
present. Jayde was stepping down the stairs as if the roaring and earthquakes were 
as insignificant as a fly buzzing past her ear. She was adorned in armor that 
resembled the army fighting for the very life of the city. The metal looked almost 
like reflective glass with crystals and runes glowing in different areas.
Maia was used to seeing Jayde's hands bandaged in monk wraps, but now they 
were soft and deadly-looking, nothing touching them but the bracer resting on the 
top of her hand.
Jayde's hands seemed to tremble slightly as she looked back up the stairs one 
last time and said aloud, “Ahlina, may I see you next lifetime, little sister. I wish I 
was able to let you travel the world as you always wished instead of letting my 
world dictate your path. They have a fight ahead of them if they plan on getting 
through me!” She turned to walk toward the entrance to the Temple. “May Kirari 
protect us in our time of need—if we need him?” Jayde said a final prayer as the 
dress she wore seemed to move on its own behind her.
A beautifully woven silk was intricately sewn into the armor which looked 
like nothing Maia or Surge had ever seen. The perfect blend of feminine and 
masculine protective gear safeguarded the juggernaut known as Jayde. The 
metallic breastplate was adorned with golden illuminated runes that seemed to 
gleam brighter with every step Jayde took toward the entrance. Her black hair was 
in neat braids that spun in two halos behind her, the rest of her long silky locks 
hanging down to her knee caps.

 
67
Jayde began to pick up her pace, stalking like a force of nature on a warpath 
as she approached the battle. Surge couldn't help but see the charging mob of 
beasts that was about to get in the Asian woman's way. She looked as if she could 
take on OZONE armies by herself. Her enemy, however, was something more 
unimaginably terrifying than any creature Surge or an armor-clad OZONE Agent 
had ever seen.
Maia's eyes widened as she watched Jayde palm strike a Spiraar in the chest. 
The anthropomorphic beast writhed in pain, its eyes glowing with a crimson haze 
that was extinguished in a split-second as the impact from Jayde’s blow caused 
instant death and launched the carcass into the distance much like the boulders she 
struck in the courtyard during her training. She tucked and rolled on the ground, 
breaking the limbs of six more giant beasts before rising to her feet and destroying 
their skulls with her bare hands. The physical appearance of the Spiraar from one 
to the next represented every species in the animal kingdom. Some exhibited a 
clash of human and canine attributes, while others were products of more heinous 
combinations. Almost every chimera imaginable was on a rampage toward Jayde. 
Various combinations of mammal, insect, bird, reptile, amphibian and marine traits 
were seen in each macabre figure. 
Her movement was so agile and fluid, it scared Maia. For the first time, she 
saw Jayde's true skill, which surpassed the training dummy martial arts drills that 
had gained the Mancers’ attention at the Pagoda. Maia could not see a weakness in 
Jayde's technique. 
“Flawless,” she said aloud.
Surge nearly jumped as Maia spoke her first words since they had walked 
into the temple. As Jayde left a trail of carcasses on her trek to the main wall, 
Surge 
completely agreed with the descriptive word Maia used for the Asian warrior 
Goddess’ attack:
“Wave of Mutilation,” Maia said. 
The roars from Spiraars did not stop, and the more she killed, the more flew 
over the wall. Jayde walked like a panther, taking down each beast with little 
effort. She used lightning-fast kicks against twenty-foot-tall Spiraar, breaking their 
jaws as effortlessly as if she were stepping on flowers in a field. Punches that 
struck four creatures at once with an invisible force seemed to drill through 
anything in their path for a hundred feet. Jayde met with what was left of her 

 
68
guards. Her presence seemed to give them all an air of rejuvenated warrior instinct 
as they began to fight for the lives of all they loved.

Blue walked along a worn-out path alone. His short black hair was as messy 
as it normally was after all the climbing and running. Gemini had told him to 
explore when he got to Zalomoni, and he'd already seen a great deal of this ruined 
city within some canyon. It was long-abandoned, and the air was a lot cleaner than 
any he'd ever breathed. The moment he arrived at a lake with odd-shaped stones 
protruding out of its still waters, he could hear his sister's voice as if she were 
standing next to him.
“So what have you got yourself into now, Blue?” Charity asked, “This place 
is pretty in a cryptic way. Do you know who lived here?” 
“I don't know, you're right—and no clue.” Blue answered with a smirk on 
his face as he realized that if anyone might be spying on him, it would look like he 
was talking aloud to himself, but he didn't care.
“Look over there, Blue. Those rocks are beautiful. I wonder what those 
markings are? Check it out!”
He took a breath and walked so fast, he seemed to appear in front of the 
stone in a split-second. It reminded him of Sky's blinding speed of thought. I hope 
Sky is okay. His hand rubbed the old mossy stone and moved a couple vines to the 
side. The sun shone on the ageless rock as if it were waking an old friend. Blue 
noticed the Runes under the moss begin to glow in the sun rays. 
“I've seen these before. The flashbacks in Pangaea had them. They were so 
hard to read. Wait, this one says, ‘Wicasht,’ and this one says, ‘Jreamers.’ ” 
Blue pointed at a three-dimensional hieroglyphic eyeball and the Runes 
spinning around it. He tilted his head slightly and took a step back slowly.
“What, Blue? What do you see?” Charity asked impatiently. “You Mancers 
tend to zone out if you haven't noticed.”
Blue pondered this idea of ‘zoning out.’ I have lost track of time lately. How 
long has it even been since I killed my family?

 
69
“This place...It’s a little over a million years old, protected by 
Supernaturals,” Blue said, “very old and powerful ones who mastered the 
Soulstream. If I'm supposed to master my powers, it's going to be here!”

Jayde said a silent prayer for the fallen warriors at her feet as she fought with

dark shadow was looming over the city, threatening to block out the sun. She 
looked high in the sky to where she knew the one being dwelled who could save 
her city. The clouds covering the mountains cleared for only a brief second, and 
Jayde took in a deep breath as she stared up at one of the most magnificent 
spectacles she'd ever seen in this lifetime.
“Kirari has awakened!”

made for this moment. He was only twenty-five years old, but he had fought 
thousands of battles with Supernaturals and creatures from beyond the walls of 
Zalomoni. His heart was racing with every swing of his heavy sword. Sweat 
blended with blood, dripping into his weary eyes. He was born to fight, and with 
all of his power, that was what he was going to do.
“Fall back, Eight Heavens strategy!” Guardian screamed out to the men and 
women of his elite squadron. Jayde was behind them fighting her own battle of 

 
70
sixty or so Spiraar, and she was doing quite well as expected. Guardian knew that

Guardian remembered the old tales about the first war called the Great War,

Ignorance. Needless to say, before humans were changed back into simple mortals,

Gaia was on the verge of death when she created her own warriors of justice 
to fight off the Earth’s impending doom. With every earthquake and storm, 
millions of Spiraar were born from the soil and pure mineral landscapes all over 
the world. With nothing but murder on their minds, the beasts were bewitched by 
Magickal instincts to destroy everything in their path until Gaia was pure again. 
These enchanted beasts cared not for their own lives, nor did they fear pain. They 
were gifted with accelerated regeneration, super strength, uncanny flexibility and 
heightened senses among other abilities by the Mancer that is the spirit of the 
planet, Gaia.
Guardian had known this story his whole life, yet he had only encountered a 
few dozen Spiraar up until this moment. He knew them as primitive, but it was 
expected. They usually ran in small packs, not more than the four to twelve he’d 
usually seen. They protected the groves which they held sacred, and they hunted 
small game much like the other carnivores in the area. The Spiraar were able to 
shape shift into any of the indigenous creatures, which made it hard to hunt them

Spiraar mated with less volatile wildlife. Spiraloupe, also known as werewolves, 
now ran wild, hunting throughout the plains. Spirafel, also known as werecats, 
were now stalking prey in the shadows of the forests. Many other new breeds of 
Spiraar cubs had emerged these last few decades, but either way, they were never 
organized enough to launch a full-scale attack. As head of the Elite Royal Guard,

71

glowing eyes of these creatures proved there was no reasoning or peace to be made

The members of the Elite Royal Guard had been hand-picked by Guardian

Being proficient with many weapons was a prime requisite, but complete

“We are being overrun, sir!” Melviel said to Guardian as he used his twin

Guardian knew what Melviel meant as he sliced through the Spiraar. He

If I don't try, then my life was for nothing. I have fought too hard to let it end this 

 
72
way. Please, Gods and Goddesses, grant me the strength to purge these lands of

you, most honorable warrior. She turned in a whirlwind form and sent Guardian a

Jayde had unparalleled control over her body as a Metamancer. She was able to

Yet her power was limited, and she knew better. She had complete control

Ahlina looked at the Magickal binding on her bandaged hands and asked for 
forgiveness, knowing that she had to leave this place in order to find her coven. 
She took the bandages off slowly, even though she knew it was forbidden, and 
stood in the dusty room that held the pride of her hometown, Zalomoni, the Dark 
Prism Dragon, Kirari. Her short black hair covered her green eyes that were the 
only similarity she had to her older sister, the Goddess of these lands, Jayde. Jayde 

 
73
never wanted her to leave because she said the world was dangerous and full of too

very few left on the planet, and one of them was but a few feet from her touch. 
Ahlina took a step closer, knowing that Jayde would kill her for disrupting Kirari's 
slumber. Her whole life, she'd been taught that most things, especially Magick, 
were forbidden. To awaken the Dragon was forbidden. To leave the City was 
forbidden. Hell, the name ‘Zalomoni’ meant ‘the Forbidden City.’ Ahlina hated it. 
This was her time to defy everything forbidden and to do what she wanted.
After a long pause, Ahlina reached her palm toward the sleeping majestic 
beast. The sweat from her brow wouldn't stop, even though she was telling herself 
that she wasn't nervous. This was the moment she'd prayed for, but she just 
couldn't force her muscles to touch the Dragon. Ahlina was a Witchbreed able to 
see not only the Runes, but also the very code that makes up the Magick present in 
all creation.
With the bandages removed, she could see the Runes spinning around her 
palm and wrist. The words ‘chaos’ and ‘disruption’ glowed brightly. She knew the 
warnings Jayde berated her with, and she knew that everything she touched 
somehow changed, sometimes for the better, but most times for the terribly worse. 
She didn't want to hurt Kirari like she had hurt so many other people in Zalomoni, 
so she hesitated and stood there shaking.
At that moment, a loud blast came from outside, and Ahlina snapped to her 
senses. She turned to run toward the large door to see what exactly could be 
causing the alarm. Ahlina was horrified to see large Spiraar eating people alive, 
destroying everything they touched. They were making their way deep into the 
city, and the Elite Royal guard was not going to be able to hold them for very long. 
Ahlina ran back to the Dragon, and this time, with tears in her eyes, touched the 
scales. “Wake up! We need you!”

Chapter 10
EXPENDABLE
Narles lay in bed hooked up to a machine that had just awoken him from his 
coma as he quietly tried to understand his current predicament. His pain was not 
unbearable, so he must have had some interestingly strong prescription medicine in 
those IV’s. He didn’t move as he controlled his heart rate to stop the panic and 
looked at all of his limbs in casts. The last thing he remembered before waking up 
in the bed was shooting Surge-Overload in the head. 
He knew that he was in bad shape. If he could see himself in the mirror, he’d 
probably cry, and he wasn’t much of a crybaby. OZONE was his employer, and he 
was willing to die for the organization. Apparently he was good at a lot of things, 
and dying wasn’t one of them. He remembered that he had shot the wrong guy, but 
his memory was hazy, and he couldn’t get his thoughts to stop jumping from his 
childhood to exploding battlefields, from trials for excessive force to the moment 
he tried to commit suicide surrounded by beer cans and bullets. 
Sharp pains surged through Narles’ eyes like pricking needles as he tried to 
look in one direction too fast. He couldn’t stop a tear from falling as he slowly 
closed his eyes while a fierce headache formed. He wanted to go to sleep and 
possibly never awake. He couldn’t think of anything more painful than the 
thumping behind his eyelids and under his swollen temples. The incessant beeping 
of the machine sounded like a jack hammer piercing his skull every second. Narles 
could no longer control his heart rate. He would’ve screamed if it weren’t for the 
oxygen mask attached to his mouth and the tube shoved down his throat. 
Dear Lord, what have I done to deserve this? I went to church. I voted for 
the guy who supported state religion and opposed women’s rights. I hate the gays 
for you, Lord. More than that, I hate the Supernatural scum who disease the planet 
and prey upon your children like animals. What am I talking about? I don’t have a 
fucking clue what’s going on. I can’t even tell where I am or how I got here. I’m 
supposed to be dead. I’m supposed to suffer for what I did to those people. No, I 
was doing your will, right?
I was all for this new job after I got discharged. I knew it was a sign from 
you, dear Lord, as I received that call with the barrel of my Glock in my mouth. 
I’ve done all I can to serve and protect people from terrorists. They threaten 

 
76
America and our freedom. They know I’m weak, and they try to take advantage of 
me, Lord, but I still fight. I’m your mortal instrument. Do with me as you will, but 
PLEASE stop this fucking pain. Please, Lord, make them give me more morphine 
or some shit because my head is killing me, and all of my limbs sting like a bitch!
I’m sorry I’m swearing, dear Lord, but I can’t help it. I’m a Soldier, and it’s all 
I’ve ever wanted to be. I don’t know what to do, and I don’t know what to say, but I 
am here to serve you in any way I can. I promise if get me off this bed and put a
gun back in my hand, I’ll shoot all those liberal godless bastards. 
I’m sorry, Lord, for being sexually confused when that freak, Agent Zeer, 
was interrogating me. He has delicate hands, dear Lord, and I didn’t know what he 
did to my prostate, but I sure didn’t mean to feel that way when he whipped me for 
information. I’ll go to church even more than I did last week.
I’ve always heard that people get religious when they are in these situations, 
but I didn’t know I’d be one of them. I always prayed for a swift death by a gunshot 
or plane crash—or maybe something cool like being cut in half by a Katana. I 
never knew an explosion felt like this. 
That’s right, some little fairy boy with a girl’s haircut is the reason I’m in 
here. He took my C-4 and detonated it on the mob. Why did the mob attack me? I 
was dressed like a cop. Even undercover, that was pretty fucking strange. 
Whatever, I still killed Surge-Overload. I’m a national hero. No, an 
INTERNATIONAL hero! They’ll decorate me with all kinds of awards. Damn, this 
headache is killing me. Why do I feel like I’m on fire?
*
“Agent Zeer, checking in to the Receptionist.”
“Proceed, Agent Zeer.”
“I have information on the condition of Soldier Narles.”
“Continue with your report,” the Receptionist said coldly.
“He’s still in the induced coma. His memories are still muddled at best, but 
he truly believes he killed Surge-Overload. His thoughts are a chaotic prayer, and 
his self-loathing is…”
“I don’t care about that. Stick to the facts.” The Receptionist cut him off 
abruptly.

 
77
Agent Zeer had jet black hair that he subconsciously slicked back with his 
pale fingers. He knew that the Receptionists were not interested in feelings or 
intangibles, but in cold hard facts. 
“We don’t have any more information about Surge-Overload due to his 
memory confusion, but we Psiel are making progress on the reconstruction of his 
limbs. The neurocybersynthetic implants are adjusting well to his body as if he 
were born with them.”
“When will he be fit for discharge back into the field?”
“Within a month.”
“If he does not give you the results you need, kill him.”
“Roger that.”
“Over and out.”
“10-4”

Chapter 11
BUILDING A MYSTERY
Witchbreed, also known as Wicasht, are the ‘Keepers of Mystery.’ Having 
taken an Oath to uphold the balance, we all keep our secrets close to our 
Spellcrafts. Each Coven does their due diligence to keep this Oath intact. We must 
not break the Veil of Ignorance! Those that breach the covenant are subject to 
harsh Judgment from the Great Circle. 
Most Covens hold their Spellcrafts in the highest regard, which are therefore 
the most mysterious of all secrets on Earth. Since mystery and secrets are the very 
backbone and foundation of Wicasht society, it is no wonder there are hidden 
groves around the world. Some of these sacred groves are so secret that even other 
Witchbreed have lost the maps and all recollection of their existence. 
I am from a sacred Grove far from the likes of humanity. It is filled with 
creatures long forgotten by the outside world. The beasts are my friends, and the 
Fae are my family. I was born able to wield the powers of the Fae and am 
surrounded by their graces with every song and dance. Though this Lost Continent, 
named after our matriarch and founder, Ara of the Ballade, is my home, I feel 
alone and lost. 
There are many Wicasht, Spiraar, prehistoric monsters and mythological 
beasts on this Lost Continent, yet they look at me as though I am an outsider. It is 
not like the Monoceros Coven is a malicious branch of the Wicasht; it is not in our 
nature to be so. However they look at me differently. Glancing at me with everjudging eyes, they keep many secrets from me. The Queen mother has taught me 
what it is to be a Wicasht. 
Queen Mother, Amarantine taught me that there was a Great War at the 
beginning of the planet’s early life. This war almost destroyed the fabrics of reality 
and tore Pangaea, a once peaceful continent, into pieces. The Monoceros Coven 
was nicknamed the Fairy Kin because the other covens believed we abandoned the 
war and choose to evacuate with the Fae. The Queen Mother did not deny the 
claims, for our first ancestor, Ara, was the twin of a self-obsessed Glamora. While 
Glamora stayed in the lime light of the other covens with her league of superficial 
Glamori, Ara used her powers to manipulate the environment and shroud the 
eighth continent in potent Magicks, protecting it forever from harm. 

 
79
The Lost Continent and its founder disappeared from the rest of the world 
that day. It would seem the place I call home would be lost forever, only found by 
accident or by summon. Some sailors have crashed to their deaths while listening 
to my sisters, and some have never left, dying a blissful death but thought lost at 
sea. While some dream of a mystical place such as this and how they would never 
want to leave, I dream of the day I can be free of it. 
Ever since I was a small girl, I was enlightened by the Queen Mother who 
told me to always stay true to my heart and never be ashamed of my purple hair, 
for it is the mark of the Fae’s favored. I love my hair, and I am ready to fulfill my 
destiny, to leave the Lost Continent and reunite with my true coven, the Coven of 
Unity.
~ Heaven ~
(Written in hieroglyphs on the walls of the Holy Temple of Ballade)

HAVE A LITTLE CHAT
Crux stood in front of his target with a new-found resolution. He looked the 
Shandor directly in his cold unblinking eyes and breathed deep. In the next 
moment, it was going to be either the Shandor's unlife or his own immortal shell, 
but either way, someone was going down. His hand shook slightly as he 
remembered the last time he had to fight the Vampyl on the docks.
“So you've found me, huh?” Crux whispered defiantly, “I hope you didn't 
hurt the girl because she's innocent. Oh yeah, you don't care about that, do you? 
You'll drink Star dry and move on like the monsters you are!”
The Shandor stood perfectly still and stared, smiling without a word.
“You know it doesn't have to be like this. You can leave now, and I'll act 
like you never saw me. That way you don't have to die here and now.” In the palm 
of his hand, Crux unfolded a paperclip into as straight a line as he could finger. “I 
won't just pierce your heart—I'll make it explode!”
He knew that eventually he'd have to fight the Shandor, so he was going to 
practice. If there was one Vampyl, then there were definitely more on the horizon. 
Crux closed his eyes and did a spin. He heard Star in the hallway singing and knew 
she'd be opening the door in a matter of seconds. He tried to concentrate on the 
paperclip weighing 30 pounds with the probability that it would strike the target's 
heart with the speed of an airplane soaring through the sky. As the door turned and 
Star’s terrible singing got louder, his heart skipped a beat, and his projections were 
skewed slightly. He threw the paperclip with all his might and watched it fly 
through the air.
With nearly flawless aim, the paperclip pierced the target's heart, and the 
loud boom of an explosion came after, followed by a scream from next door. The 
weight might have been off by a few hundred pounds.
“Crux, you little mental patient!” Star shrieked, “You have to stop this. It's 
the third time this week!” Star yelled at the twelve-year-old as she looked at the 
collage Crux had built on her wall of a hot model that looked like he could be a 
vampire. “Either you have something against really hot male models, or I'm 
missing something. You keep making these pieced-together men out of my 
magazines and using them as target practice. It's getting old.”

 
81

can't just hide here in your room and not be practicing how I'm going to take them 
down.”
She lifted the sheet of paper where the heart was, looked underneath and 
gasped. “It went all the way through the wall and through the neighbors’ houses 
too! If you don't stop throwing stuff in my room, I'm gonna take you down!” Star 
straightened her dress and regained her composure. “Look kid, we need to have a 
little chat.” She patted the bed next to where she sat.
Crux skipped over to her smiling and sat down. “But you saw my aim. It's

“Ok, kid, here's the deal. If a girl like me is running from a guy like that—” 
She pointed to the wall where Crux’s collage of supermodels-turned-Shandortargets was hanging. “—Then I'm the one with the problem, not him.”
“But you don't understand. They're not good guys like Blue and Gemini; 
they're killers, and so what if they look like that? They can rip you to pieces with

“Don't threaten me with a good time,” Star said as she licked her lips and

“I don't get it,” Crux said, looking thoroughly confused.
“You wouldn't!” Star said with a quick rebuttal. “Anyway, we need to find

“Nine of us?”

once, becoming her own choir. The Fae nicknamed her ‘exalted,’ as she was gifted 
with a heavenly voice that could summon higher levels of Fae if taught properly; 
her voice could bring about chaos and destruction if used improperly.
The Fae nicknamed her ‘exalted,’ as she was gifted with a heavenly voice at birth. 
This meant that her voice could summon higher levels of Fae if trained properly, 
yet it could bring about chaos and destruction if trained improperly. 
As Heaven floated along the dark seas, watching the whales and sea 
creatures in a blur beneath her, she began to remember that last conversation she 
had with her mother, Queen Amarantine.
“Mother, I must go!” Heaven pleaded.
“I cannot stop your heart once it's made up, Heaven, but know that you are 
walking a path to doom. You are meant to mate with a Fae and continue the

and peaceful. The Queen looked at her rebellious daughter with all the love a

“I know, Mother, and I don't want you to risk all that you have worked hard

84

“I may not be able to stop them, but I can delay them, my daughter. Here,

“Yes, dear, after dusk you must stand in the moonlight and put a bit in your

“You have a gift that they cannot take away nor control. I know you have

“The last Fae who harnessed all nine voices summoned an army that saved

The Queen took her necklace off and handed it to her daughter.
A sharp pain brought Heaven back to the present. She found herself

“Show yourself!” The three voices spoke as if harmonizing the threat. 
“It is your cousins, the Cetus. You didn't think you'd escape, did you?” Two

85
like a cross between a green dragon and a giant black squid, his scaly tentacles

“Don't do this, cousin. I don't want to hurt you.” Heaven spoke calmly as she

A shriek and roar of a laugh belted throughout the sea for miles in all 
directions. The Shark circled so fast, a whirlpool began to form, and the DragonSquid made himself invisible with a cloud of ink.
Heaven knew there was definitely no turning back as she sang three songs

“Your lullabies won't work on us, Witchbreed. You're nowhere near as

“Nowhere to run, and your blood is filling the water, cousin. Oh, it smells so

Through the clouds, a thunderous squeal parted the silver linings as an

Melviel smiled to the porcelain soldier of a Vampyl next to him. She reminded him

Chapter 13
FIRST DAY BACK
Her anxiety was all but gone; however, Sky screamed inside 
because she felt like she was missing something. She had forgotten the 
last few weeks of her life, and she didn't know how that was possible. 
The therapist told her it was post-traumatic stress disorder and 
retrograde amnesia, due to all the shock of her recent events. The 
doctors tested her for all kinds of diseases and mental ailments because 
of the kidnapping. Braintech labs gave her special devices to make her 
feel more safe, and they even secured her room with the latest 
technologically-advanced security and cameras.
After a few restless nights, she was finally able to go to sleep. Last 
night she dreamed of flying and floating in a bathtub; nothing a normal 
teenager wouldn't dream of. She shrugged. Her sleep was where she 
found the most peace these days. It was like she was in her own world 
within her head where no one could violate her. The doctors said it was 
normal for people to withdraw the way she did. Though she seemed to 
be back to herself for the most part, deep down she just wanted to 
remember what happened while she was gone.
Why would a kidnapper leave me without so much as a bruise and 
give me such nice clothing? Thank God my Daddums was there to find 
me! The reporters said he was on a mission to get me back with bulletins 
and developing technology that could search for me, the same 
technology the government just passed as the Eye in the Sky law.
It’s all some satellites and artificially intelligent birds that travel 
across the world tracking people. I guess my absence brought about 
something good. Mum seems to be doing okay. She only remembers 
leaving me in the house as she rushed to Dr. Hill’s the night I was 

 
90
kidnapped. I tried to tell her it's not her fault, and she almost burst into 
tears! How would she know some random guys looking for my dad's 
secrets would come into the house? Makes me wonder what, or why 
Daddums has to hide...
“Sky, it's time to get ready for school,” Sky's mother said as she 
spoke through a monitor on the flat screen next to Sky's bed. Her 
beautiful dark skin looked exceptionally comforting to Sky as she 
opened her caramel eyes fully. Sky subconsciously reached up to close 
the blinds above her bed. She looked at her mother for a moment longer 
before her mother continued, “We have talked about this day for the past 
few weeks. Are you well-rested?”
“Yes Mum, I'm well-rested and ready to get out of the house and 
back to my life.” Sky smiled and looked at her closet for a long time.
“Is everything alright, Sky?” Her mother sounded concerned as she 
stared at her daughter's countenance.
“I'm just tired...of all the tests and questions,” Sky whispered. “I'm 
great. I'm excited to get dressed is all.”
“Alright, love, I'll leave you to your closet,” her mother said in a 
sweet tone as she turned the monitor off.
“Since when did she call me from the kitchen and have a full 
conversation over a monitor?” Sky mumbled to herself. She thought 
everything seemed to be back to normal, except for little details like that 
and the camera in the corner. Though virtually unnoticeable, Sky knew it 
was there, somehow.
It feels like someone is always watching me. Meh. I guess it's for 
the best, considering all that's happened. Ok, so here is the fun part, 
clothes!
Sky opened her closet, and for the first time since she'd been back, 
she smiled with pure happiness.

 
91
*
Dear Diary,
It's Kaery, again. Today will be my first day back from visiting my 
dad in New York. Things were a little strange, and I feel like I don't 
know him anymore. He wants to move on with his new family. But that's 
not what I wanted to talk to you about. I couldn't stop thinking about 
Blue. Ever since the night at the Forbidden City where we almost got 
shot by the Triad, the cops or the Mafia, I'm still not sure. I saw Blue 
protecting a boy named Crux and his best friend Tommy was dead. We 
somehow got to Gemini's Pagoda; I can't take my mind off what 
happened. But I wrote about all of this months ago. Today I just want to 
write about that moment between Blue and I in the hallway of Gemini's 
Pagoda.
To most people it was simply a glance, but we shared a moment 
that made my heart stop. Everything he said to me was in his indigo 
eyes. He looked at me as if for the first time and the last time, but I didn't 
say a word with my mouth, however my eyes said it all. I wanted to be 
with him. I wanted to kiss him. He was a pure light in the strange scene 
that made me feel like this was home.
Blue said to me with his glance that he recognized me, and though 
we don't know each other, he knows me. He wants to know me better. He 
wants me to kiss him and hold him to share my warmth. He's lonely and 
tired, but he will fight for what he believes in. He trusts Crux, and he 
won't leave the boy’s side. Blue wants me. He thinks I'm beautiful. He 
wants to be my lover.
I can't stop smiling, and sometimes I cry when I think of that 
moment. I can't stop thinking about it when I'm alone.

 
92
Something else happened that day. My friends, Star, who was 
transformed into a glamazon using a power she calls Glamour, Scape, 
who is now wondering if he's schizophrenic because he can speak to 
plants and animals as well as Ahlina, who has to wrap her wrist because 
it's constantly bothering her, were all placed safely in their beds but had 
the same dream. We know now that it wasn't a dream but an actual 
event, as crazy as it all sounds. My issue with only hearing and speaking 
the truth is getting tolerable, but I don't know what I'm supposed to do 
beyond this.
I know, Diary, this is a strange letter to you, but you have to 
believe me. I think we're all going slightly insane, but I think it's a good 
thing. I tried to confess to my mom, and she didn't seem to understand a 
word I said, as if I spoke another language.
So I was told there was a rumor that Sky was going to be back in 
school. I can't fathom how she is going to return to her life as the most 
popular girl in school while dealing with all the rumors. Maybe she can 
handle it, considering she has been dealing with public appearances her 
whole life. I heard her father paid for meditation specialists and yoga 
masters...
That's it! Thank you, Diary, for listening to me. As usual you've 
made me see things from another perspective. I am grateful to have you.
Till next time with love,
Kaery
*

Sky's closet had vastly improved since the last time she saw it. It 
was a lot larger and there were amenities within the large room which 
could accommodate the spoiled teen for weeks, like a safe room. She 
noticed the clothes were very similar to the ones she told her doctors 
about in the hospital while being rehabilitated. The French haute couture 
that she so loved blended with the finest fabrics money could buy, but 
also black and pink cashmere sweaters, t-shirts and leather pieces that 
she didn't have in her previous selections were found in different parts of 
the large closet.
The room was separated in sections based on her aesthetics, and 
she could easily find anything with the touch of a button on the new 
holo-screen that replaced the flat screen she once had near the door. The 
holo-screen could appear in mid-air anywhere in the closet with a hand 
gesture. Being one of the prototypes in new technologically advanced 
software/hardware, it ran on a platform that Sky wasn’t familiar with, 
but she picked up the functions as if it were catered to her very own 
whims. She loved her father for such extravagant gifts and felt so lucky 
that she was a Bradford.
As Sky pranced around her closet with wide-eyed anticipation of 
what to wear and touch, she felt a sense of nostalgia that plagued her like 
a family member's burden spying on her from heaven. She knew in her 
heart that something was missing. She wanted none of this fashion more 
than she wanted her memories. 
With all the money her family made, her father being the CEO of 
Braintech, the most influential and successful software/hardware 
company on the planet, and her mother being the heiress to the largest 
chain of diamond mines in Africa, Sky only wanted them to retrieve her 
memories of the few weeks she was abducted.
The holo-screen popped up and told Sky that she had a message. 
She approved with a simple nod as she kept browsing her very important 

 
94
debut outfit. It was Sky's father cheerfully saying in a prerecording, 
“Now remember, Bunny, you don't have to go through with this. The 
first day back was your mother's idea, but don't tell her that I said that. 
She still has the notion that you'll need to be around all those people in 
order to feel 'normal,' but baby, I know and you know that you're not 
normal!” Sky turned to look at the hologram intensely, breathing hard 
for no apparent reason.
What does he mean by that? Sky thought.
“I mean that you're my Indigo child, and no one can change that. 
I've always known you were slightly more intelligent than most, and no 
one ever had to tell me that you were going to be this beautiful, not to 
mention your taste for fashion resulting in your own fashion line by the 
time you were in middle school. There doesn't seem to be anything my 
beloved Sky can't do,” her dad said with the most proud smile she'd ever 
seen.
Sky sighed with relief from her father's confession, but felt an 
undertone in his words that she couldn't put her fingers on, as if she 
knew he was hiding something. She shrugged the feeling off as soon as 
it had appeared and turned back to the selections chosen by some perfect 
stylist she had yet to meet.
“By the way, Sky, in your new holo-screen is a program called 
Distinctive Ideatum Veritas Assimilation—for short, DIVA. Simply ask 
for Diva, and she will appear to you. Her choices are based on all the 
information stored on your selections in the past eighteen years, 
including seasonal wear, music, art and school subjects you've 
researched. She can be quite useful to you. You can speak to her like a 
normal person without the entire invasion of privacy issue. It is a 
program that only you and I have access to, so feel free to adapt your 
DIVA as you see fit,” her father said with a loving smile as he stood 
silent for a moment.

 
95
Sky looked over her shoulder to see the recording still staring at 
her. She could do nothing but smile back at him even though she knew 
he couldn't see her. “I love you, Daddums,” she said in a whisper.
“Just know that I love you, Bunny, and that if you want to reach 
me, all you have to do is the swipe hand motion that I taught you, and a 
hologram will appear as long as your phone is near you, say, if you were 
in this closet. Eventually I will release some similar technology to the 
public, but in the meantime, you're my special child who deserves the

“Bye,” Sky said smiling. “Okay, so how is this gonna work? I just

“You called, Sky.” A hologram of a beautiful brunette slightly

“Yes that is correct, milady,” Diva responded in a British accent.
“Don't be so formal,” Sky commanded.
“You got it,” Diva responded in an American accent.

“Michelle Obama.”
Sky's brow lifted as she smirked. “Who is a close second?”
“Marie Antoinette.”
“What's my favorite fabric?”

 
96
“Egyptian cotton sheets.”

and put blonde highlights. Now change those heels to blood-red, and the

“Is this to your liking?” Diva responded as Sky smiled at her very

The trip to school was definitely different from her usual routine 
after her yoga and bath. Instead of driving the electric car that she had 
gotten for her birthday, Sky was being escorted by bodyguards that her 
dad had hired. She was confused by the entourage, considering they all 
looked like they had stepped right out of an action movie set. They were 
nice enough to her, even though they didn't speak much.
Sky sat in the back seat of the large SUV and wondered what the 
first day back would be like. Her anxiety was starting to rise with 
anticipation. She would normally listen to her favorite music when this 
happened and breathe like her meditation coach told her. These guys 
were playing some music, but she didn't bother telling them it was all 
wrong. She reached into her purse and grabbed what looked like a seethrough crystal but was actually the cell phone her father had created 
especially for her.
When Sky's thumb touched the phone, it scanned her clothes, and 
the back of the phone camouflaged to match the same color as her blue 

 
102
dress. The screen's menu showed her playlists. Sky chose a nice relaxing 
dance mix and put on her head set so she could listen in peace. She 
noticed the driver glancing at her to make sure she was okay. She gave a 
cordial smile and looked out the window. The music was soothing to 
Sky as she watched the city’s buildings pass by and the locals walking as 
if life were so simple. It must be nice to not be known. They can do 
anything they want; it's not fair. Sky tried to smile but couldn't force 
herself to.
The security guard in the front seat began to whisper to the other, 
“How long do you think we have to babysit? I mean we're highly 
decorated Agents. Don't you think this is a little remedial?”
The other Agent smiled a grimacing yellow flash of broken teeth 
and said telepathically, “Watch your mouth, Agent Stone, the young lady 
can hear you.”
Agent Stone looked into the rear view mirror as he drove to see 
that Sky was still listening to her music and looking outside the SUV. 
He whispered, “Are you sure? She seems deep in thought.”
“Trust me, you know what I specialize in, the thoughts of others.”
The Agent reached for his pack of cigarettes but stopped. He knew Sky 
despised cigarette smoke. He covered his fingers in a gob of sticky spit 
and ran them through his greasy hair.
Sky was already subconsciously removing the cigarette smoke that 
had been hanging in the car when she got in, focusing her energy to push 
it out of the windows. One of the Agents reeked of nicotine and bad 
breath, but none of that attacked her senses anymore. She could hear 
them whispering and wondered when she could talk to her father about 
dismissing them. Finally we're here at school. Sky took her headset off 
and fixed her beautiful locks of hair. She took a deep breath to prepare 
for her exit from this car.

 
103

Her father looked at her for a brief moment and said calmly, 
“Bunny, this is for your own good.” After seeing the look of

Chapter 14
SPELLCRAFTS
Heaven sat in the dark room alone and heard the dripping of water. Her 
senses were on overdrive, and she awoke in a place other than her homeland. It 
smelled of death and fear all around her. She wouldn't allow herself to think about 
how she had assisted her cousins in meeting their deaths. Heaven had never killed 
a bug, let alone an animal, or any creature for that matter. Her strict moral code 
that all life had a right to be had not been compromised, but she was in survival 
mode and knew that many of her cousins did not carry the same ethics. She 
doubted she could even physically kill anyone, and whoever owned the dungeon 
she found herself in most definitely did not agree with her code.
She tried to stand up and realized she was walking through water. There was 
not enough to submerge herself in, but enough to glide across the top with her 
mermaid's fin. Heaven studied her surroundings. There were scratch marks and 
what looked like teeth marks on the rusted stone walls. The random chains hanging 
throughout the room no doubt had held captives at some point.
I can only do so much with movement and muscle memory. I must use my 
Spellcrafts in order to see the room for what it truly is and find a way out. With a 
soft hum, Heaven remembered that she had minor control over her environment by 
simply moving within it and singing. She cleared her thoughts and imagined the 
mermaid's fin falling from her legs. Each scale that fell from her flesh became a 
glowing blue flower in the ankle-deep water. Her hum swelled to a full wave of 
harmony as her voice mimicked instruments and drum beats.
The flowers glowed brighter as Heaven belly danced across the dimly lit 
dungeon. Hundreds of flowers floated over the top of the slowly draining water 
like a happy algae that belonged far below the ocean's depths. Heaven's touch of 
the once-putrid water had now purified it enough to be drinkable. Her smooth 
dance moves were not yet up to full potential, and when Heaven bent backward, 
she felt the wound on her ribs so acutely that she almost toppled backward into the 
water.
She didn't stop dancing because she wanted to complete her environmental 
spell. She knew that Spellcrafts allowed Magick users to weave reality as they saw 

 
107
fit. There was not much time for her to create an environment that would let her 
heal herself. The plants had not fully bloomed, but when they did, they would 
spread more spores in the darkness that reacted to her music. She glided so 
gracefully across the room that you'd think she was in a dance studio and not a cell 
used for unspeakable crimes against humanity.
I'm not dumb; this is a torture chamber of some sort. I have to keep dancing 
if I hope to summon any more energy that will heal me. That's right, a well-placed 
garden of Arcane light specter blossoms, and this room will never see darkness 
again. With a little more light and a lot of love, I should be able to grow Elixir 
berries that will heal these wounds. Just keep dancing and singing, Heaven. This 
will have to do as a makeshift grove for now.
*
“So, you're saying that we need a grove,” Kaery asked inquiringly to Crux, 
“in order to practice magick because it will help us learn Spellcrafts faster?”
“I'm not sure how all of it works,” Crux answered uncomfortably as the kids 
stared at him, waiting for answers, “But that's what I heard from the card handlers, 
and my mom is one of the greatest. If you find a grove, it's a purified place where 
Mother Gaia has allowed Magick to flow freely with no blockers. Such Magick 
will fill the senses and allow for creative energies to rejuvenate. Or something like 
that. Whatever.”
Kaery's long purple bangs and light brown locks in the back were very nice 
distractions, and Crux chose to focus on her hair as he answered. She was very 
easy to talk to. He hadn't stayed anywhere for very long, but for some reason he 
liked these teenagers. They didn't have any of the answers, even though technically 
they were supposed to as Witchbreed.
Crux ate the fruit snack that Scape gave him with a smile as he spoke to 
Kaery, “I think Witchbreed use Spellcrafts. It just means each of you will learn to
use Magick in a different way, even if you all get the same results.” Crux combed 
his fingers through his freshly-cut mohawk that Ahlina and Scape had styled for 
him. He looked around the room at Star, Scape and Ahlina. I bet they know I'm 
winging it. I wish I'd spent more time actually listening to my occult teacher. This 

 
108
stuff was always just so boring! I mean, Mancers do what we feel, and Witchbreed 
read and rewrite the code. That's it!
“Okay, I think I may have an exercise that will help.” Crux scrambled 
around the room like a mad man.
Star shrugged silently as she shook her head at the obnoxious boy. She really 
hated kids, but this one was growing on her. He had an air of confidence that was a 
cover up for all the pain, and she felt that. At least he refused to be a pity party. 
Star respected his ridiculous version of wisdom, as he was clearly winging the 
whole thing, but it was adorable that he at least cared enough to teach them 
anything about Supernaturals at all.
Scape was as handsome as ever in his cool demeanor as he sat in a yoga 
position and watched Crux run around Star's room. He'd grown to think of the boy 
as a spirit journey host, an honor that was given to the highest of Shaman in 
Scape's Blackfoot Native American culture. He didn't understand where this 
journey would lead, and he didn't care at this moment, but he was excited to know 
what the future had in store for them all. He also wondered about the other 
soulmates.
Ahlina tried to act as if she were annoyed by sighing aloud, pushing her 
curly bangs out of her face and into a head band. Her Asian features were smooth, 
thin and tom-boyish. She was very happy with her appearance but wasn't happy 
with the current uncertainty. She looked around the room to see what the boy could 
possibly be scrounging for. “What are you looking for?” she asked impatiently.
Crux smiled as he found what he was looking for. It was a glass of water he 
had been drinking a couple days before. He turned, and with a flourish, presented 
the unimpressive object to his audience. His excitement raised the intrigue of the 
four Witchbreed in the room. “This is it. Half a glass of water.”
Star pouted, “If this is some stupid half empty, half full philosophy...”
“Let's hear him out, Star,” Kaery interrupted as Scape smiled, silently 
agreeing.
“Thanks, Kaery. So the trick is to first read the code. The next step is 
recreate the code,” Crux said as he looked at the puzzled expression on each of the 
teenagers’ faces. “Really take a deep breath and focus on the water. You should be 
able to see the code that makes it water in the first place.” Crux was definitely 
winging it, considering he had always failed this assignment in the occult class. 
But he knew the basics behind it. Witchbreed should be able to see the code easily.

 
109
Kaery stared at the water in the glass. She saw the clear fluid and thought 
about how thirsty she was. She took a deep breath as instructed, and just as she 
opened her mouth to ask for more instructions, she began to see small blue runes 
floating in the cup. Her mouth gaped further in wonder as she read, ‘Hydrogen,’ 
‘Oxygen,’ ‘Life,’ ‘Liquid Essence’ and ‘Water’ runes floating harmoniously 
together in the glass. She looked over at Scape, who seemed to be focusing hard on 
the glass, and Ahlina, who looked extremely bored. Star was leaning close to the 
glass, then yawned, flipped her red hair and leaned back into her seat.
“Okay, so I see the code or whatever,” Star said aloud. “What do you mean 
recreate it?”
Kaery looked at Star, amazed. Is she really able to see the code like me, or is 
she just trying to speed the lesson along? This looks like a dream. How come we 
haven't been able to see the code before this point?
Crux smiled and sighed in relief, not knowing beforehand if his little 
experiment would work. “So, who all can see the code?”
Kaery raised her hand as she looked at the glass one more time. Star raised 
one hand as she looked at her nails on the other. Scape slowly raised his tanned 
hand as he concentrated on the glass in awe.
“So I'm the only one who can't see some random virus in the glass?” Ahlina 
asked, more frustrated than anything because she really couldn't see the code at all. 
She didn't even understand the lesson. “This sucks.”
Crux smiled. “Trust me, that's a good ratio. I wasn't able to see the code for 
years, and you all can see it in seconds. I'm a really good teacher, I guess.” Crux 
sighed, and his smile grew. “Ok, now for the recreation part. Don't feel bad if 
neither of you can do this. Basically, you see the code, and you can make the code 
in your own way using your Spellcrafts.” Crux realized he had lost his audience 
and began to back-peddle. “Like, for example, if I asked either of you to go get me 
water. One might go get a glass from the faucet, another might bring me a bottle of 
ice cold water and another might turn on the shower. All water, but different 
interpretations. Witchbreed can remake any code they've seen if it's up to their 
power levels, or something like that.”
Star placed her hand on the small oak table where the glass was sitting, then 
raised it, and a glass of water identical to the other was conjured into being.
“Whoa!” Scape and Ahlina yelped simultaneously as Kaery reached to grab 
the glass Star had made.

 
110
Kaery grabbed the glass and crushed it in her hands. “It's not the same. It's 
fake. The code was wrong. It lacked the rune Life.”
“That was awesome though! Star used Glamour to recreate an identical glass 
visually, but not in reality. I almost forgot that she was in the Glamouri coven at 
one point and is able to conjure such perfect illusions. Kaery was somehow able to 
tell the real cup from the Glamoured one quite easily.” Crux clapped happily. 
“Kaery, you try to recreate the code. Remember it probably won't be the same way 
Star did it. Maybe you need to snap it, or blink or say it out loud.”
Kaery looked at the glass and snapped, but nothing happened. She looked at 
the kids as they stared back at her with wide eyes. She blinked really hard, thinking 
of the water filling the cup, but nothing happened. “Focus, Kaery,” she told herself 
as she read the runes aloud. Every rune she read began to glow, then multiply, and 
the water started to fill the cup until it reached the brim. Kaery sat back and stared 
at the glass in disbelief, then amazement, and started laughing uncontrollably. 
“You've got to be kidding me? It's that easy.”
Ahlina didn't know what to do, but she did stand up and look at the glass as 
if it were an alien thing. “So we can all do that?”
“In one way or another,” Crux answered simply.
Scape got close to the glass and put his finger in the water. He stared for a 
long while.
“Are you trying to pee or something? ‘Cause I heard that if you...” Star was 
interrupted by a shush from everyone in the room as they stared at the glass with 
Scape.
The water began to slowly leave the cup as if his finger were absorbing 
every drop. Scape smiled as he concentrated harder until there was no water left. 
He pulled his finger out of the glass and looked at his friends with a gleeful grin on 
his face.
“That was awesome, Scape,” Kaery said as she clapped.
Scape looked up at the ceiling, and everyone followed his gaze. They saw 
nothing but the lumpy paint and more pictures of models pinned to the walls. Then 
all of a sudden, the room began to rain. Literally, water dropped from nowhere as 
if they were all standing outside in an afternoon April shower.
Star screamed as the water flowed into her room. She desperately tried to 
cover her clothes, and then she stopped and looked up again, wondering why it was 
raining in her room.

 
111
Ahlina stood up with a haunted expression, then she started laughing aloud 
for the first time. “OMG! Now that is epic!” She spun around in the rain and 
laughed.
Kaery sat in her seat and smiled at what was happening. At that moment, she 
realized they were able to perform miracles. The dreams weren't just fantasy, but 
their real memories. They all had been extremely powerful at one point or another, 
and this was just a taste. Now they needed to actually learn how to master these 
Spellcrafts, and life would be so much more interesting.
“Get out of my room! My place is officially off limits,” Star screamed as the 
rain stopped abruptly, leaving the room soaked.
Crux giggled until a smack came to the back of his head from the heavyhanded Star. “That was for all of your stupid throwing.” She then smacked the 
laughing Scape on the back of his head. “That's for the rain!”
“She's right. We do have to find somewhere else to practice this,” Kaery said 
in a haunting voice that made everyone stand at attention, drawn into her glowing 
golden eyes. “There is a grove nearby guarded by Spiraar. We have to find it, then 
use it to become stronger.”
Crux smiled then said aloud, “Cool!”

Chapter 15
FALL OF OLYMPUS
(Mount Olympus: 391 B.C.E.) 
We will always be young and beautiful, but is it enough? We’ve done

“Shut up, Ahlina.” Star glared at her friend, then looked back to Kaery. “Ok, 
fine, you won’t ask.” Star pouted and looked out the window to see the clouds 
below the great city of Olympus. 
Kaery was about to give in and ask what was bothering Star, but she didn’t 
have to proceed as Star impatiently broke down and confessed her qualms. 
“So, the reason why I’m so down is because today is very important.” Star 
was interrupted by Ahlina. 
“Yes! It’s the day when Nina decides which Supernaturals are to have 
children in the new season!” Ahlina said, excitedly. 
“She wants to be called Aphrodite, and eww, no, that’s not what I was 
talking about, but it does have to do with her decision,” Star said. 
“You mean the decision of who is to ride with her to Sky’s Kingdom in 
Egypt?” Kaery said, taking the wind out of Star’s intriguing conversation.
“Way to use your foresight to tell what I was going to say,” Star pouted. 
“I’m sorry Star, I did see this happening, but I didn’t have to use my powers 
to do so,” Kaery said, walking over to her tall friend and placed a hand on her 
milky arm.
“Bam! I knew you were predictable, Star,” Ahlina said, chuckling. 

 
113
