parade. We shall give the humans love potions.” 
“I shall have the servants bring the perfumes to the steps of your castle,” Star 
said as she walked in long strides out the room. “I’ll meet you at the baths to 
prepare your makeup and wardrobe.”
Nina did not reply. She knew Star was the best in the kingdom at such a task 
as Glamour. Deep in thought, she could not help but to think: Am I too lost to be 
saved? Will they hate me for the next thousand lifetimes? This is going to be the 
hardest decision I’ve ever had to make, and for some reason, now I know what 
Zeus must feel with all his laws and courts that he governs. My Magick is not 

 
120
strong enough to shield me from the storm that is definitely going to need to be 
cleaned up.
Kaery guided Nina into the large room that was full of water. With a silent 
prayer, Kaery walked ahead of Nina saying, “May the warm waters cleanse as the 
cool wind guides my true breath.” In a second, the water began to steam, and 
Kaery’s breath was visible as she walked across the water like it was stone. 
Nina stood in awe of the Witchbreed who effortlessly warmed an entire pool of 
water just for her comfort. She’d seen Kaery do such a task thousands of times, but 
she noticed today that Kaery smiled the entire spell. Though Kaery was immune to 
Nina’s ability to control emotions, this was an obvious sign that the maiden was 
pleased with her work. “Why do you smile so gleefully?” 
“I am honored to be part of such a court. You respect and love me and my 
sisters as your own. We are not worshipped as Goddesses, yet you treat us as such. 
I would not ask to be in anyone else’s court.” Kaery smiled and spoke truthfully as 
she walked across the water and back to the marble floor where she reached her 
hand to Nina to guide her into the pool.
Nina’s hand was warm, and she loved her baths, the water blessed and 
enriched with herbs, minerals and crystals on the floor that glowed once her foot 
was submerged in the water. With every step, she felt relaxed and rejuvenated, 
wiping away the anxiety she felt just moments before. “Are you sure of that 
statement, Kaery?” Nina said, smiling mischievously as her naked body sank into 
the pool, only allowing her breast and upper body to be touched by the cool but 
steamy air.
“Whatever do you mean, Nina? I am in the court that I love the most.” Kaery 
spoke, slightly confused. She knew that Nina was a jealous Mancer, yet she’d 
never shown such a side to Kaery until today.
“I mean, I am no fool. I see the way you look at Aegis—I’m sorry—Zeus.” 
Nina made sure she was facing Kaery when she spoke his name so that she could 
witness the expression for full effect. 
Had I been sloppy? How did she know that I was seeing Blue? This is 
embarrassing. “I adore the Mancers. I still would not want to be part of his direct 
court.” This is only because he’s married to Hera, and she’s a wrathful Mancer.
“I understand you love him, though. Not as your master, but as your husband.” 
Nina smiled as she turned to lower more of her body into the water. “This is a 
secret I will hold until I die, so stop worrying so much, Kaery.” 

 
121
Kaery sighed softly, but knew she couldn’t hide much from the mistress of 
subterfuge herself, who not only read emotions Magickally, but also interpreted 
them through any and all expression. Kaery blushed as she spoke, “Thank you, 
Nina.”
“Not to be mentioned again. But I have a secret I would like for you to 
keep.” Nina sank into the tranquil waters slowly and then emerged after a long 
breath. Her eyes were the same color as the pale blue crystals underwater. She 
spoke in a voice that was so sure of itself, it made Kaery shiver a little. Nina was 
notorious for changing her mind and being indecisive. “Today at the ceremony, I 
will announce that there are to be no more Supernatural births this cycle.”
With a wide-eyed stare and shocked expression, as if she’d heard the most horrible 
news in her life, Kaery could do nothing but listen for a hint of a joke. She knew 
otherwise as her senses told her this was the absolute truth. Nina had made her 
decision and was planning on sticking to it. 
Nina noticed Kaery’s predictable response and replied with a confident 
rationale. “Before you think this is an attack on you or anyone specific, it isn’t. I 
know how much you and your Coven of Unity have done, not just for my court and 
the courts of all the gods, but for the courts of Mancers and of other nations, 
equally.”
“Yes, but if you know how loyal we’ve been, and with all that you’ve asked, 
we’ve answered your every request, why would you make such a decision that will 
undoubtedly make many suffer?” 
Kaery dropped to her knees as if pleading with the bathing Mancer. 
“I do not intend to make anyone suffer anymore. That is exactly the reason 
for this. I must put an end to the wars. We should love one another, not fight,” 
Nina said with a peaceful sparkle in her eyes. 
“Not all Supernaturals fight. Most of us live in peace among the humans or 
in castles, such as this one in the sky. Our Magick aids both cultures without 
prejudice. We have created pools such as the one you bathe in, weapons that make 
the Mancers of Blue’s court exceptionally powerful—channeled the very elements 
and asked for their divine blessings to carry the castles on the clouds and even 
crafted mirrors and garb for your vanity.” Kaery spoke so passionately that her 
voice was starting to become louder, deeper, even booming and rocking the 
foundation as her eyes began to glow. 

 
122
“Kaery, calm yourself!” Nina said to her, subconsciously using her powers, 
even though she knew they had no effect on this individual. “This is my choice, 
and it is done. You will carry on as if you know nothing and as if everything is 
perfectly fine with my decision. The other Supernaturals look to you for guidance, 
and I am counting on you to calm the waves.” 
Kaery’s eyes continued to glow a molten gold as the Runes of Magick spun 
in all directions within her pupils. She could not fathom how this Mancer had 
grown so egotistical that she could stop the progression of the Supernaturals for 
something as inevitable as war. It was unacceptable, and Kaery knew that in a 
single word she could reduce this castle to rubble. She shook as she clenched her
open hands into fists. It took every ounce of willpower for her not to say another 
word but she succeeded. Her eyes stopped glowing as she realized she was floating 
high in the room. She willed herself to settle down on the ground. 
Nina smiled valiantly as she said, “I knew you’d see things my way. There is too 
much violence in the world, and we can put a stop to it. The Supernaturals of the 
most powerful kingdom in the entire world shall feel my real power, and they shall 
understand that I won’t condone war.” 
Kaery could not smile or bring herself to speak without some sort of 
judgmental comment, so she stood quietly as Nina spoke. In a single decision, this 
woman had broken the trust that Kaery had instilled in her. Kaery had countless 
conversations with her coven, telling them all not to worry; this would be the 
thousand-year cycle when Nina would finally bless them with children. She would 
look like an idiot to her coven, as well as a hypocrite, if she gave her blessings to 
such a fool. Kaery was livid but held her composure close to her heart. 
“I see you are struggling with this, Kaery, but I trust you will say nothing to 
anyone, not even your Coven of Unity. I have an announcement to make soon.” 
Nina knew the world would never be the same. She spoke aloud, mostly to herself 
than to Kaery as she walked past the edge of the baths toward her quarters. “I have 
warned the Mancers of this kingdom that I will not participate in their warring 
games. I told them they will not overlook my power ever again.”
This is something she had planned for many moons then, making us do these 
ridiculous tasks and wait on her hand and foot like slaves. We were never her 
equals. Not in her mind. She was using our kindness and loyalty to her benefit. 
Now that she’s the most revered goddess in the court, she spits in our faces. What 
will I ever say to my coven? Kaery’s thoughts were so sporadic that she barely saw 

 
123
Nina’s hand approach her. Flinching away out of reflex more than anger or disgust, 
she looked into Nina’s blue eyes. 
“I promise this pains me more than you’ll ever know, but it must be done,” 
Nina said, retracting her hand from Kaery, knowing her feelings were hurt. Kaery 
wasn’t very good at subterfuge, and her emotions were written all over her face 
and divulged through her body language. “You’ll do as I have requested, right?”
After a silent stare and a conscious decision to do something even worse, 
Kaery answered while bowing. “I will not tell anyone of your decision before the 
announcement.”
Nina’s large smile was a sign of her true happiness as she hugged the 
Wicasht lovingly, though no hug was returned. “You will not be sorry.” I will still 
help her and her coven have children, just not now. She has to trust me; I want to 
see who in the court has underestimated my power. “Trust me,” Nina said before 
walking her naked body down the long marble hall adorned with artistic replicas of 
her own face along with paintings of her many exploits in the myths and scriptures 
of diverse cultures. 
Kaery could not bring herself to move after recognizing the monster she had 
helped create. She was still holding her clenched fists behind her back, and they 
lowered slowly to her thighs as she thought. This pains me more than you’ll ever 
know, but Olympus must fall!
*
Blue stood in the center of his courtyard speaking to ten of the most 
powerful members of his court. His golden armor was handcrafted by the Coven of 
Unity, and his love, Kaery, had personally blessed it with her scent so that when he 
wore it he’d always know she was near. Of course this was something no one 
knew, and it was subtle enough to give him hope that he’d be with her someday. 
He’d married Hera to appease her followers many decades ago, but he was not in 
love with her. His power over the people was unchallenged, yet he seemed to think 
it was necessary to exercise his might every so often. “What do you mean they will 
not pray to my altars anymore? I am Zeus! Do the humans no longer fear me?”
“It is not that, my King,” Hera said coldly. “They have not seen your 
presence in so long, they have lost some of their hope.” 

 
124
“I shall renew their hope, and they shall replenish their fear and respect for 
my great kingdom,” Blue said as he slammed his spear on the ground, cracking the 
foundation and the marble in five different ways. “We are the Immortals of 
Olympus, and they will know how displeased I truly am.”
“What do you plan on doing?” Hera’s intricately curled hair hung in loops 
that made her look more regal than she anticipated. Her silky skin was untouched 
by blemish, and she wore a revealing goddess dress that draped her curves. Her 
plum lips showed no expression as she asked her question. 
“After today’s fertility parade I’ll let them make love and sleep well into the 
morning in a Dionysus drunken stupor as they praise Aphrodite’s power. They will 
remember my power when I release the Kraken!” Blue’s eyes did not flinch, and 
his expression was daring someone to question his judgment. 
The entire court gasped dramatically as they whispered to one another. The 
Mancers of this court had a healthy fear of the God of Gods. He had the ability to 
send any of them back to the Soulstream with but a thought. The painfully 
beautiful hum of the Soulstream meant that he was channeling his unmatched 
power. He could open a portal and send raw Magick to disintegrate any mortal or 
Immortal alike just as easily. 
“My apologies, but don’t you think that is a little harsh…” Hera couldn’t 
complete her words that she would dare only to utter. 
“If any of you challenge my word, you’ll see the Soulstream up close and 
personal!” Blue said, provoked. “The humans may not be blessed with the sight to 
see my power the way you all do, and thanks to the Veil of Ignorance, they have 
already forgotten that I reign supreme. They will no longer forget this after tonight. 
I suggest you gather your favorites, mistresses and demigods, then bring them to 
Olympus because tomorrow will be nothing but my wrath.”
His court fell silent. Every member knew he was going to unleash one of the 
world’s greatest terrors on his own kingdom to teach them a lesson. This was not 
going to end well, no matter what anyone said or did. Zeus was given a lot of gifts 
in his many parades and shrines; one of them was the artifact, which allowed him 
to control a Dehmn that came through the ocean from another realm like the 
Supernaturals used Port Runes. 
The Kraken was a beast that was so destructive, it was banished by the Great 
Council to a dark realm where it could destroy to its endless tormented content. To 
have such a creature released upon earth was chaos incarnate. Only the most 

 
125
powerful could stop it, and it wouldn’t be until the creature touched their kingdoms 
far from Greece or Zeus stopped the violence with the artifact. 
In a booming voice, Blue asked, “Where is Aphrodite?”
Hermes was as handsome as his intimidating father. He dared not interrupt 
such a speech and awaited his moment to speak: “She is preparing for her parade 
of fertility, Father. Would you like me to deliver a message?” 
“Tell her to hurry and make sure to use her powers of love and persuasion so 
the entire kingdom is under her spell.” Blue waited for his son to leave in a rush 
before continuing, “I expect even the Supernaturals she blesses with children will 
be busy mating tonight, only to be hit with a rude awakening. None of you shall 
say a word. Save your own, and the rest I will handle.” I’m tired of these warring 
kingdoms getting more respect than me. No longer will they forget my wrath. The 
Titan shall be released upon this world, and they will remember who put him in the 
Dehmn realms in the first place.

HISTORY
In the beginning of creation, Aenjelhn and Dehmn, also known as Angels 
and Demons, were created by the Soulstream. Their missions were polar opposites. 
Aenjelhn were the bringers of order and light while they traveled across time and 
space. Dehmn were the pioneers of chaos and darkness as they ventured 
throughout the cracks of reality. When both forces inevitably met, there was a 
clash of true power that lasted anywhere from a flash to millions of years. In the 
end of their battles, a large Grey area of new matter, a Grey area that neither 
faction wanted to venture into, was created. This Grey area is known by humans as 
the big bang theory, the means by which a universe is born and with it, potentially 
life.
By the power of the collective souls within the Soulstream, this grand plan 
has destroyed and created countless universes and multiverses. No one knows what 
the point of the plan is unless they are within the Soulstream themselves. 
Those few who are given a glimpse of the grand plan are called Wicasht or 
Witchbreed. With this enlightenment comes the most responsibility a mortal shell 
can obtain. The Witchbreed are meant to teach parts of the plan and maintain the 
balance that is the foundation for all reality.
Essentially, anywhere there is life there will always be a Supernatural 
hierarchy to maintain a balance. The Mancer or Gods were created to rule, as they 
chose to be leaders. The Witchbreed were created to teach Mancer, Supernaturals 
and humankind alike how to maintain the balance through Magick. The thin line 
between chaos and order is Magick, which is the foundation of all energy. Some 
Witchbreed have simplified the code of Magick for humans as science, while 
others have enlightened the Supernaturals with the highest levels of specialized 
Spellcrafts.
There were twelve main covens on the planet Earth, and with each coven 
came a specialized and secretive mastery of Spellcrafts that were overprotected and 
highly dangerous in their own rights. The separate covens made sure to keep their 
secrets so that too much power wasn't held in one place. They remain neutral to 
one another but maintain prejudices and stereotypes of each other’s covens, as each 
one doesn't fully understand the complexities of the others. Each coven is so 

 
127
extremely powerful that they can break down the very fibers of reality in one form 
or another. This has led them to a governing system called the Great Council. All 
of the leaders of each coven make a presence for all to hear the direction of their 
individual coven, and new information which can assist the others in maintaining 
the balance.
One coven was formed from nine members of the other great covens. Their 
allegiance to and love for each other was formed in a Magickal bond that could not 
be denied as well as an oath to maintain the balance, thus giving them the 
thirteenth seat on the Great Council. Calling themselves the Coven of Unity, they 
have become one of the most renowned, feared, loved and hated covens of the 
Great Council. The reason why there was so much mistrust and interest in this 
coven was because of their blend of traditional Spellcrafts never seen before by the 
other more conservative covens. Though they are by far the smallest coven, they 
are downright formidable as they do what was once forbidden: master and crosstrain each other in their uncanny Spellcrafts.
Kaery was appointed as the leader of the Coven of Unity after leaving a coven of 
oracles called the Anu that mastered the oracular truth of the code. Their point of 
view was quite literal and so were most of their Spellcrafts. By reading the runes of 
the code and using her Spellcraft called Veritas, she was able to recreate reality as 
she saw it. She was also able to see future outcomes of any adjustment to the code 
as well as heal others by rearranging the runes of the code and restoring the order. 
Because people are fearful of the truth, the Anu were nicknamed the Banshees, and
the masses fled from them when they arrived in council meetings. Kaery's gift was 
that she was born with Avatar's Armor, a natural immunity to others’ Spellcrafts 
and anything Magickal. However, thanks to her only flaw, the Soulstream’s 
Beckon, the more she was within her trance-like state, the more she lost touch with 
reality and became more of the true form of the Soulstream.
Scape left his coven of beings called the Gaian that studied, worshiped, respected 
and summoned elements and animals from Gaia herself, the first Mancer on earth 
and true spirit of the planet. This coven was nicknamed the Beasts for their obvious 
love of animals. Scape was able to reach within the instinctual side of the psyche 
and communicate with Gaia and the elementals who form the surface of the planet. 
His mastery of each elemental resulted in the birth of a living tattoo on his skin, 

 
128
which allowed him to summon their energy at any moment for his own unique 
Spellcraft called Acat. His Gift was also his flaw, called Totem, which allowed him 
to take the form of the Elements he had mastered. The longer he was in the form of

Ahlina was linked to a coven called the Children of Daeva, also known as the

Magicks without the lack of control that other focused Witchbreed had spent

Star was part of a coven known as the Glamouri. Their leader was a beautiful

Bitches for their attitudes and roles as Supernaturals. They were meant to be the

create and wield items that were powerful and normally unobtainable. Her flaw 
was called the Void Trap: the more powerful she became, the more enemies she 
would attract. This flaw was marked by her eyes, which were two different colors.
Never is one of the last descendants of the Wyrm, a coven of Dragon-riding and 
Dragon-Blooded Magick users. Most of them decided to leave earth along with the 
Dragons that once inhabited the planet after the Great War. He was able to use the

ice. He could also change his form to that of the Wyrm. His gift Dragon Form

 
129
allowed him to teach others the abilities of the Dragons while granting him near 
invulnerability and great strength. His flaw was Disconnected from the hive. Once

way through trial and error, acquiring a Spellcraft called Rage casting, which

Heaven was from the Monoceros coven, also known as the Faery kin, whose

other mystical realms of Gray called Fae Magick. Instead of being self-obsessed

was also able to permanently change her physical features to match those of the

Her unique Spellcraft was called Multicasting. Using her multiple sets of vocal 
cords, she was essentially her own choir and could cast more than one spell at once 
while singing and dancing. She could change her very environment as if she were 
using Glamour, but on a dreamlike and sometimes permanent scale. Her flaw was 
Elven aura. No matter what she tried to do, she would always stand out in a crowd, 
never able to adopt the plain features of her peers.
Khenebu was a prodigal master of the Necromantech Spellcraft that is unique to 
the coven known as the Fen Ying, or the Gorehounds, as they have been 
nicknamed throughout the Great Council. They reached deep within dark matter, 
opening portals to realms mainly controlled and/or inhabited by the Dehmn. They 
brought back alien technology and codes that gave them mastery over the use of 
dead flesh and souls. His gift was Humanity’s Touch, which allowed him to 
somehow maintain his humanity while practicing his dangerous Spellcraft, as 
others from his coven resembled Liches or undead sorcerers after mastering the 
dark arts. His flaw was that he was a Dark Conduit for higher levels of Dehmn, 
which made his soul an easy target for possession by these beings who longed to 
enter his plane of existence. 

 
130
Ewm was from the esteemed Clandestine or Warlocks, a coven of males who 
harbored knowledge and secrets as intelligently as they mastered high levels of 
offensive Magick and acted on their ambition for power. They held considerable 
power and influence over the Great Council after the banishment of the Sin 
Chimes. Ewm broke away from the Warlocks with a curse that forbid him to enter 
the Coven’s Grand library, the Celirdor, no matter what lifetime he was reborn in. 
The young Witchbreed didn't seem to be bothered by the curse, as he had a gift of 
Magickal eidetic memory, which prevented him from losing all codes he ever read 
and/or mastered. He also had access to the Spellcrafts of eight other covens that the 
Warlocks could only wish they had information on. His Spellcraft that he mastered 
was called Battle Casting, which allowed him to cast spells and curses without 
much concentration, even while fighting under extreme duress. His flaw was that 
he was not proficient at defensive Spellcrafts at all, as if the universe would not 
allow someone so gifted in offensive Magick to master both.
Ssiah was the coven of Unity's beacon of Aenjelhic light. She was from a coven 
called the Llabrat who later deemed themselves the Orphans because they were 
abandoned by their Forefathers, the Aenjelhn, after the Great War. Despite this 
disconnection, they continued to remain noble and ever-present in their search for 
order and all things light. Being able to see Aenjelhn and Dehmn alike, Ssiah was 
able to learn potent Magick from beings that are older than our universe. She could 
summon Aenjelhn to do her bidding as well as use a collection of celestial 
Spellcrafts known as Enochian Magick. Her spells were so strong that she was 
thought to be otherworldly. In truth, her gift was Binding of Enochian Magick, 
which allowed her to cast spells very much like a Mancer casts Magick: by pure 
thought and feeling. Her flaw was that she was Warded, which meant that the 
Aenjelhn were overprotective of her spiritual vessel and wouldn't allow her to learn 
certain types of Magick, even from her own coven.
It is not certain how so much diversity and power bonded, but the Coven of 
Unity has been under my watch per the request of the Great Council. Each lifetime 
we meet, they seek my services to teach them how to use their Magick. Every
lifetime, I've known them all to have nothing but the purest love for each other. 
Our paths have crossed many times, and it is my spell that has shielded them from 
the ever-watching eyes of OZONE and much older enemies. There is no such thing 

 
131
as coincidence, and these kids will soon find this lesson as important as any 
mistake they may have made in past lives.
The Coven of Unity is responsible for some of the most powerful spells that 
have ever been cast, the Spell of Enlightenment and the Veil of Ignorance. They

the Supernaturals; the Spell of Enlightenment would have been impossible for any 
other coven to complete. The results were so devastating that the Earth erupted 
with its first war called the Great War. The war almost tore the world apart, 
literally, which forced the Coven of Unity to cast a reversal spell that would 
forever make humans Magickally blind, the Veil of Ignorance. For with ignorance, 
the soul remains humble and innocent, unable to see Magick and therefore unable 
to change the code in any way. Of course, no spell is perfect, and a lot of new 
Supernaturals were born on earth. I have to make sure that nothing like that 
happens again.
This is the brief history of the Coven of Unity as I have witnessed it, for I 
was one of their most prized mentors and Wicasht elders, Gauntlets the Avatar of

Chapter 17
DEJA VU
Okay, I can do this, Scape told himself in the bathroom mirror, I have 
maintained a great GPA, I have sick friends, I'm the head of the new student 
greeting program and I'm a fairly attractive guy. So why can't I find time to get a 
boyfriend?
Scape pointlessly messed with his sculpted hairdo. He knew there was just 
enough product to look like a rocker, but not too much, so he still kept his 
approachability. His tanned skin was a compliment to his athletic physique that fit 
his earthy and comfortable clothes, which had only taken him a few minutes to 
choose.
Maybe I spend too much time putting my look together, or maybe I'm just not 
looking in the right places, but I swear I would be a good boyfriend. I spend so 
much time making sure everyone else is happy that I have been neglecting my own 
spirit. Of course, I've never had a boyfriend, so this will be tricky. I don't know 
what spirit guide to pray to, but here goes nothing. Just send me a cute boy that I 
can take care of who has great hair. Ok, I don't mind his hair so much if he is 
really cool. Thanks, spirits of the land.
Scape took a deep breath and closed his eyes while the water from the sink 
flowed over his fingers. He didn’t notice the sudden transparency of his hands as 
they assumed the properties of the stream that flowed from the faucet. His mind 
kept wandering, but he had to go back to the admissions office to show a new 
student from Egypt around the school. He had chosen to help new kids become 
more comfortable with the school shortly before his friend Kaery had transferred 
from New York. It served as a credit on his transcript, and he would get a killer 
letter of recommendation. It'd been almost two years, and he was excited about the 
position, even though Principal Hatcher was tough and always on his case. He 
opened his eyes when the restroom door slammed. Scape instantly stared at the 
pale face of the strange loner, Nevin, who looked shocked to see someone 
meditating in the restroom.
“Umm… hi,” Scape said, taking a gulp, “I was just…umm. Well, hi Nevin. 
Oh wait, you like people to call you Never, right?”

“Yea, that's right.” He lifted his hand to shake Scape's. “Since I didn't 
officially introduce myself to you, I'm Never, and you're Kaery's friend Scape, 
right?”
Oh, yeah, he is the punk rocker kid who stares at Kaery a lot. But he's so 
cute. I didn't really pay attention to his hair before—it’s so blond it’s almost white! 
And he may be shorter than me, but he has skater boy features. Cute! “I know I 
haven't spoken to you much since the day at the diner, but I wanted to tell you I'm 
sorry I never took the time to get to know you.” Scape raised his hand up to shake 
Never's hand. “I'm Moon Scape Mahkan, but everyone just calls me Scape.”
“Oh, ok, Scape,” Never said as he touched the warm hand. Never looked 
into Scape's familiar brown eyes, and a sharp gasp filled the air along with a 
desert-dry heat.
Scape gasped simultaneously as he held onto Never's hand. The hot air filled 
the room and invaded their lungs as they were brought to another time.
*
“Don't fall, Never! Just hold on,” Scape screamed desperately as he held 
onto Never's hand while the pale teen hung over the side of a cliff. “Just let me get 
my footing and I'll pull you up!”
“Don't tell me, just get me up!” Never cried, looking down at the rocks 
hundreds of feet below. “This is what I get for trying to run from those pirates. I 
knew they'd find us.” He felt his hand slipping from sweat.
“Shut up and hold on,” Scape said, kicking his simple leather boot off his 
left foot and touching the rock with his toes. The rock fused with his flesh, and his 
leg became as solid as the stone. Now that he knew he wouldn't fall, he pulled 
Never up to safety. His long hair blew in the wind, and he ignored the dust as it 
swirled around the cliffside and into his eyes. Instead he used all of his strength to 
pull Never up over the cliff where they collapsed onto each other and lay there for 
a long while, breathing heavily and staring up into the sky.
“You know we have to stop meeting like this,” Scape said as he laughed 
while his heart tried to stop racing.

 
134
Never stopped looking at the sky and stared into Scape's brown eyes, “It 
would take all the adventure out of it.” He smiled and rolled slowly off Scape's 
muscular chest and arm.
*
“Whoa, what was that?” Scape said, lying on the floor of the restroom with 
Never next to him, staring into the void created by the boy’s dark eyes.
“I don't know, but I don't think I have to pee anymore.” Never got up 
quickly to his feet and looked down at Scape who was smiling peculiarly at him. 
“I… umm, gotta go.” Never practically ran out of the restroom.
Scape lay back onto the cold tile, smiling as he looked up at the ceiling. “So 
that's what Deja vu feels like.” I wonder what lifetime that was ‘cause we looked 
like pirates, or worse. He looked down at his hands and smiled. “I saved him?” 
Scape continued smiling until the bell rang, and he remembered he was supposed 
to be in the office helping the new kid get to classes. He jumped to his feet, dusted 
his pants off and barely looked at himself in the mirror before he ran to the office.
*
“Your guide should be here any moment,” Ms. Hatcher said impatiently, 
looking down at the impressive transcript from the exchange student. “Khenebu 
Anwar, is it?”
“Please, call me Ken,” he smiled graciously.
The charming white smile almost caught Ms. Hatcher off guard, considering 
this teen had not made many facial expressions since entering her office.
“Well, Ken, you will not be given any special treatment here at Drakehawk 
Academy. Do I make myself clear?”
“Yes, of course, Madame.” Ken spoke smoothly.
“It's just Ms. Hatcher,” she responded quickly. “As I've told you before, we 
have a large student body of well-to-dos and wannabes of the high society and elite 
class. Even with this exceptional transcript, I still can't believe the admissions 
board let you in so late in the school year, especially with all of this Sky business.” 

 
135
The last part Ms. Hatcher was mostly saying to herself, but realized Khenebu was 
lost when she saw the expression on his face change. “So no funny business. I'll be 
watching you.”
Running into the office panting was Scape, who ignored the secretary and 
barged into Ms. Hatcher's office. “I'm so sorry, Ms. Hatchet; I was talking to a 
student and...”
“Silence!” Ms. Hatcher interrupted rudely. “I don't have time for your 
insubordination. And if you want to be replaced, Mr. Mahkan, then trust me, I can 
make it happen.” She knew Scape hated being called by his last name.
“My apologies. It won't happen again, Ms. Hatchet— erm, I mean, Ms. 
Hatcher.” Scape cringed at the mishap of calling her the teasing name. She 
reminded the students and teachers alike of a serial killer the way she cut people 
down in her school.
Khenebu smiled as he looked at Ms. Hatcher's face becoming slightly red. 
He stood up and spoke, “You are my guide, I assume?”
Scape barely looked at Khenebu for the first time, but grinned automatically. 
“Yes, I sure am. Let's get you to your classes right away.” Scape tried to avoid the 
glare from his principal, but couldn't take his eyes off her for some reason.
Khenebu walked out of the office politely and into the hall where Scape 
hurriedly paced behind him.
“Thanks for that,” Scape said, “I owe you.” He finally got to observe the 
new student properly. “Wow, you're attractive! Oh my gosh, I'm becoming Kaery. 
I'm sorry, I'm Moon Scape. You know what? Just call me Scape. I'll be your guide 
in the harsh social jungle known as Drakehawk Academy.” Scape looked into the 
light brown eyes of the strange new student.
“Please, don't be sorry. Thank you, Scape. You're handsome yourself,” 
Khenebu replied politely.
Like the earlier run-in with Never, Scape felt a familiarity that he couldn't 
shake. There was also a slight chill to this handsome newcomer though. He had 
neatly-twisted dread locks that cascaded down his back. His smooth dark-brown 
skin revealed strong, masculine and modelesque bone structure. His walk was that 
of a noble aristocrat with etiquette, though he smiled as if he were happy to be free 
of some unknown burden.

 
136
Scape watched him closely as he described the history of the halls and 
paintings while also offering small tips on not getting bullied and surviving the 
classes. “The eldest prep school in California, Drakehawk Academy is housed in a 
historic castle with origins dating back to the Sixteenth Century. After hundreds of 
rooms and passages were restored in the late Nineteenth Century, the grounds 
became an epicenter of academic achievement and have continued to produce 
visionary leaders ever since. We pride ourselves in giving our students freedom of 
expression while maintaining high standards in curriculum. Okay, this speech is 
too boring for me to continue without asking you about yourself.” Scape smiled as
Khenebu turned to him, interested.
“What would you like to know?” Khenebu asked, taking a step closer to 
Scape.
Scape became slightly nervous and uncharacteristically shy all of a sudden, 
so he looked down at the documents he had grabbed on the way out of the office. 
“Well, Khenebu, I see here on your transfer papers that you're basically an 
academic guru. Trigonometry, AP Physics, Excel English, Latin, Excel History and 
Excel Engineering. But you look like, well, a stud.” Scape looked down.
“I assume you calling me a horse is a compliment, so I will take it as such. I 
like to study is all. And you can call me Ken if Khenebu is too much for you, 
though you said my name perfectly.” His warm smile was comforting. He raised a 
hand to shake Scape's. “This is your custom, right?”
Scape slightly lost track of what he was talking about as he looked at the tall 
dark teenager smiling like a prince. He looked down at the hand in front of him and 
said, “Oh, yes, of course it is. A… umm, custom to shake hands.” Scape smiled 
genuinely back up at the tall Khenebu. He reached his hand out to shake as they 
stood in front of the trophy case in the East hall.
The moment the two shook hands, the lights flickered in the hallway, and the 
trophy case exploded around them like a wave from the ocean. A salty breeze filled 
the air, and both of them were taken to a different time where they sat side by side 
in what looked like the bow of a slave ship.
*

 
137
“Tonight Ahlina will get us out of here, and we'll be free, Khenebu,” Scape 
said to the dark muscular man next to him as their arms were clasped.
“I hope she doesn't take it as an opportunity to run for her own life,” 
Khenebu said to the Native American next to him. “You know I trust you, but she's 
too...”
“The spirits tell me that tonight is the night we will be freed,” Scape said to 
comfort his fellow slave.
They stared at each other for a long time as they held hands. The shackles 
clanked in the dark pit of the stinky ship. They could feel the water shaking the 
vessel side to side as it splashed through the cracks of the putrid wood. Other 
slaves continued to row. The overseer looked in the direction where Khenebu and 
Scape were whispering and twirled his whip to strike the backs of both slaves, “Get 
back to work!,” making them release the grip of each other. 
*
“You're a Witchbreed!” Khenebu said aloud to Scape as they stood among 
the shards of glass from the shattered trophy case, alone in the silent hallway of the 
school.

single creature or Supernatural. Yes, I just called you the most powerful beings on

understand and respect the Code.”

why he wasn't trying to come up with a plan to save Sky and teach her this lesson.

them, but he stopped himself, and for the first time, decided to not be a rebel. Nina 
stood close to Surge, who seemed to find comfort in being near her. Juno was 
perched in her OZONE-issued armor on a stone nearby, watching and listening 
carefully as Gemini spoke, even though she was not one of Gemini's students.

learned.”

There was a river which didn't seem to end as well as some of the most

140

Blue walked toward the stones as if he weren't being followed. His short

Maia stepped into the sunlight that was near her foot. She felt the warmth of

142

Jayde nodded as she looked at the kids take a step closer to selfenlightenment.
Nina felt the excitement that everyone was charged with around her. She

Kaery looked at the food on her tray, puzzled, then back up to her concerned

stuff.”

 
144

“Ok, so I don't know why, but for as long as I can remember, I have been 
dreaming of this city that was under attack by these huge monsters. I know you're

werecat-looking monsters, and I saw that Asian lady, Jayde kicking major ass, but

than the monsters, and a lot of people died.” Ahlina was waiting for Kaery to 
laugh, but it never came so she continued, “Ok, so I couldn't—or more like didn't 
want to sleep, and that's when I got into MMORPG's, you know, the massive 
multiplayer online role playing games where I can escape into random dungeons 
and country sides, blowing stuff up.”
Kaery nodded and said, “I remember that was about four or five years ago.” 
When Ahlina nodded, Kaery whispered, “that's when you met that guy Surge 
what's-his-face, right?”
“Overload,” Ahlina said, “and I think it’s the computer science teacher to be

“Wait, you think your teacher is the cybergod hacker online?” Kaery asked, 
almost laughing. Then she noticed Ahlina pulling away and said, “Ok, so what

Ahlina looked around suspiciously, “Well I started seeing these weird 
symbols like hieroglyphics all over the online world, and I started seeing them on 
the computers.” Ahlina raised her right hand with chewed fingernails. “When I

“What the?” Kaery gasped, looking at Ahlina's hands as she knew she was

“That’s why I wear a glove on this hand most days and/or some other cute

“I touched my cell phone, and it literally melted. Kaery, I'm scared.”
Kaery focused on her friend's right hand so intently, she thought she was

Kaery grabbed Star's arm and ran down the hall saying, “I'll explain later,

Chapter 19
STOW AWAY
(Somewhere in the Atlantic: 1513 A.D.)
En route to the colonies of South America, she found a way onto the slave 
ship as a deckhand. Ahlina fled from her home and made it to Portugal. She cut her 
hair even shorter than it was and wore a hat and baggy clothes like the local 
vagabond sailors. This was no game, as she knew these were the scum of the earth, 
using people as cattle for their own profits. If they found out she was a woman, she 
knew worse things could happen to her, so she resorted to no eye contact, always 
keeping her head low and following orders the best she could. The ships were easy 
enough to maintain, but the trip with so many people below was the issue. There 
were dozens of slaves who did not last the entire trip due to starvation, disease, 
neglect, or some other inhumane act. 
Ahlina was enlightened already and was forbidden to use her powers, but if 
worse came to worst, she’d blow them all up and put them out of their misery. She 
used her martial arts training from other lifetimes to carry loads that were three 
times her body weight, which made her an asset to the captain, who thought the 
skinny boy was more useful than half the deckhands. This attention was not 
overlooked by an overseer named Ratkin. Ahlina couldn’t imagine why anyone’s 
mother would name her child after a rodent, but after watching him for a while, she 
noticed the twitch in his nose and the jagged overbite reminiscent of the rats in the 
ship. She giggled to herself when he wasn’t looking, and she realized the Captain 
was the one who named these people like property. 
She wore bandages on her right hand up to the elbow, and no one knew why, 
so they nicknamed Ahlina ‘Wounded.’ She didn’t particularly hate the name, 
considering her past, so she kept her mouth shut and answered to it. There were 
worse jobs on the large ship by far, like the peg boy, barely a teenager, who sat on 
a wooden peg while the ship sailed, so that the men could have their way with him 
when the seas were calm. Ahlina would have much rather killed herself than be 
subject to such a fate. There was a keeper below named Retchmaw the Lasher, or 
just Lasher. He was an especially vile and disgusting creature of a man from the 
desert who had been hired to keep the slaves below in line. Ahlina tried to stay 
away from him because she wanted to spit anytime he was near. Today just wasn’t 

 
147
her day. She was called by the Captain Emilian, a noble from a family in Portugal 
called the Shandor, who supplied most of the ports with weapons and rare 
commodities, making them rich and powerful. For centuries the Shandor family 
had changed their trades to match the demand; lately their trade had been people.
“Wounded, come here,” Captain Emilian said with an air of arrogance and 
contempt that meant he never wanted to repeat himself. 
Ahlina lowered her hat on her head and hid her face as she bowed to the 
captain. “Aye, Captain,” she said in the lowest voice she could muster.
“I need you to assist Retchmaw the Lasher today when his assistant Narles 
needs to change shift. I heard you have a soft spot for these slaves, and I want to 
teach you a lesson that they are nothing more than animals. These are good men 
doing their job as you do yours with the deck every day.” The Captain looked at 
her bandaged hand and asked, “What happened to your hands, Wounded?” 
Ahlina was so scared of the day the Captain would ask about her hand that she 
simply hid the bandaged arm behind her back and said one word, “Burn.”
“Very well then, off with you,” Captain Emilian said with an undertone of 
pity. 
* 
“We’ve been here for four days, and I don’t think half of these men are 
going to make it. They haven’t given us food in two moons,” Scape said to the 
dark slave next to him. “What’s your name? Look, you can talk to me; I’m not 
going to hurt you. I may not be African, but I was captured in Persia and sold, just 
like you. Oh that’s why you’re not speaking; we don’t speak the same language.” 
Scape rolled his eyes at his own ignorance and dumb luck.
“He’s not speaking because he’ll get whipped to death,” a man with smooth 
dark-brown skin said to Scape as his light-brown eyes watched Narles the Snitch 
leave his station. “He’s changing shifts, so I can say something to you. Every time 
you get these people to speak, you get them whipped, if you haven’t noticed. And 
they don’t understand a word you’re saying. I’m Khenebu by the way.”
“Nice to meet you, Khenebu, I’m Scape,” the muscular young man said 
smiling, as he couldn’t move very far with the shackles on his ankles. “Are you 
from Persia?”

 
148
“No, I’m from a small village on the Ivory coast,” Khenebu answered almost 
matter-of-factly. “Why do you ask?”
“Because the only language I speak is Persian,” Scape said. “And, well, you 
speak to me quite perfectly.”
“Now that I think about it, I don’t know how I understand you,” Khenebu 
said confused. “Shhh, here comes another overseer.”
*
Ewm was a pale Romanian boy who had been adopted by a poor medicine 
man. He watched as his adopted father was robbed and brutally murdered by 
bandits who stole the few coins that were in his purse. They laughed as the small 
boy cried over his father’s lifeless body. “I curse you for what you’ve done!” Ewm 
spat through his sobs as lightning struck in the background. The bandits smiled and 
shrugged at the creepy orphan.
After the bandits left, Ewm followed them to a nearby camp where they all 
slept near the dying fire. He used some of the poisonous tree frog serum his father 
had told him about to taint their water supply. Slowly but surely, the thieves’ 
health seemed to deteriorate as they made it back to their lair. Some even began to 
believe in the boy’s curse. Ewm waited for his time to strike. He walked into the 
lair as they all lie paralyzed with tears rolling down their cheeks. 
The ten-year-old stood over their bodies with a smile they could never 
forget. A sinister gaze came over his moist eyes that bordered on insanity. Ewm 
straightened his already-neat black hair and rummaged through their spoils. He’d 
always been a scholar, as his adopted father had called him, preferring to gain 
knowledge rather than play with the other kids. He had memorized his father’s 
small library, including all of the rare books on medicine and linguistics, within 
weeks. He saw treasure chests of gold and jewels in the bandits’ lair that were 
worthless to him; nothing interested the young boy until he came across the book 
that would change his entire life. 
“My name is E-w-m, pronounced ‘Oh mm,’ and you’ll have to remember 
that name when you give the River Man his coin and tell him who sent you. I’d 
like you all to know that a simple frog was the catalyst for your curse, and his 
simple neurotoxin has paralyzed you so that a small child like me can seek revenge 

 
149
for his innocent father.” Ewm put the large book in a leather sack as he took a 
warm hooded scarf from one of the smaller men, then grabbed a coin for each of
the thieves from the many chests that littered the room. 
After placing a coin into each of their hands, he said a prayer and set an 
antidote in the center of the room among their treasures, which they had thought 
were more valuable than human lives. “This antidote is your true treasure and 
salvation. If you don’t ingest it in time, your bodies will permanently shut down, 
and you’ll need to pay the River Man. The world is better off without your kind 
killing innocents, but I’m not like you. The paralysis should slightly wear off in a 
few hours. The problem is there’s only enough of the antidote for maybe two of 
you.” The young boy sat in the room full of paralyzed thieves whose eyes stared at 
him silently, though their souls were screaming in agony. 
Opening the ancient book, Ewm gasped as he somehow knew how to read 
the strange text: “This is the Language of Magick.” The boy sat for hours, 
enthralled by the words of the book, learning what he could before realizing that he 
had to run for his life. He would never let anyone take the book away from him. 
When he stood up and looked into a mirror, he saw an image that terrified him; his 
eyes were no longer dark brown but had changed to a blood-red. He saw glowing 
runes spinning around the pupils, but he didn’t look very long before grabbing a 
small amount of supplies and a bag of coins, heading to the port city, Porto. 
After three days of hiking and reading the book he knew was sacred, Ewm 
found out about a war between Supernaturals that no one had ever talked about 
before. He learned that Magick truly exists and that he was a Wicasht, also known 
as a Witchbreed. Ewm became obsessed with the book, intent on learning as much 
as he could about what the Soulstream was. The book was never out of his reach, 
and he had the growing suspicion that someone was following him. The young boy 
kept running without looking back. When he reached the fishy-smelling port city 
of Porto, he soon saw the same types of low-lives who had killed his father and 
countless others, all hired by nobles to carry out terrible crimes against humanity. 
He didn’t want to speak to any of them, let alone ask for safe passage to another 
country. He saw boys his age taken upon these large ships and raped as if it were 
an everyday occurrence. Ewm stuck to the shadows for days and slept only for 
minutes at a given time, trying to move often so no one would find him. 
The ship he chose to stowaway on was very similar to the others, nothing 
really special in particular, except it was named Relίquia, which translated in 

 
150
Portuguese to ‘Relic.’ He took this as a sign and snuck aboard the wooden vessel 
the night it was going to set sail. As he climbed aboard the ship, he looked back to 
see hooded figures moving through the city toward him. Ewm felt as if they were 
after him, but then dismissed the thought as simply paranoia when he found a 
shadowy part of the ship to hide in. He could barely sleep as he heard screams and 
cracking whips from the deck below. He hugged the leather sack and tried to stay 
out of sight, hiding in any crevice that would support his small frame. His hunger 
led him to a half-full barrel of stale hardened bread. Ewm was so hungry that he 
slid into the barrel and shared the bread with the rat that was already inside. 
*
Ahlina walked down the stairs to the pit of the ship. The smell almost 
knocked her off her feet as she slowly grabbed the rail and held her mouth 
instinctively with her gloved left hand. She got to the bottom of the slimy stairs to 
see at least thirty men and ten or so women staring at her intently. Their white eyes 
screamed for hope in the darkness, but she could not deliver, as she was trying her 
best to stay off the radar. In this moment, I know that we’ll never be Royals. We are 
all destined for darkness in a world that can allow this kind of injustice.
“Wounded!” A loud man snarled her name from within the shadows. He 
walked toward her wearing a filthy tunic that had blood spattered on it and grime 
from who knows what on the sleeve. His teeth were hideously discolored and 
disgusting as he smiled a foul grimace. 
Ahlina put her head down and simply said, “Aye.” 
“So you’re taking over Narles’ shift? Good, ‘cause I was getting tired of that 
slacker anyway.” All of a sudden the Lasher pulled out a whip that had small 
hooks you might miss if you weren’t examining it closely. 
Ahlina was afraid he had found out who she was and was going to attack 
her. She looked around for safety when she saw two men whispering with each 
other. One looked Persian and the other obviously African. She also saw a man 
next to them who wasn’t rowing because he was nearly dead. There was nowhere 
to run, as the staircase was behind the foul beast of a man whom Ahlina was 
prepared to kill if she had to. She reached down toward her bandaged hands when 
she saw the Lasher spin the whip in the air. Ahlina dodged the whip and lowered 

 
151
her body into a fighting stance on the warped wooden plank that she was standing 
on. 
A quick snap of his wrist, and the whip sliced through the air and past 
Ahlina’s head as fast as a snake bite. The sting was just as deadly as it hit the 
whispering man from Persia across the chest. His smooth tattooed skin was now 
bleeding as he yelped aloud. Another crack slashed the man next to him with the 
neat dreadlocks. His head whipped backwards in a painful grimace as his arm was 
raked by the barbed whip. 
As she looked back at the now bleeding men, Ahlina did everything in her 
power to prevent herself from running over to their aid. This was not the way to 
treat anyone, and she despised everything about this cursed ship the Relίquia. 
“No one talks, they row! Ok, Wounded,” Lasher started speaking as if he 
never harmed anyone, “So your duties are gonna be to make sure they aren’t dead. 
If they are dead, they become shark bait. If they are almost dead, let me know. Got 
it? Good!” He scowled at the slaves as the deckhand stood up. “Quick reflexes. 
You’re gonna need them if you plan on keeping these animals in check.” 
An uncontrollable cringe came over Ahlina’s body as a gasp escaped her
mouth in the undeniable sound of disgust. 
“They disgust me too, Wounded,” Lasher said with a phlegmy cackle. “I just 
had to get used to their smell. Once you get past the black skin, they almost look 
like something.”
He has no idea how wretched I believe his existence truly is. I’m not 
disgusted with any of them. I’m disgusted that you call yourself a human. Ahlina, 
just keep your head down and do your job. You only have a couple more days 
before the ship arrives at the colony, and you’ll never have to see his filthy face 
again. Don’t take off the bandage and melt him. Don’t destroy the ship. Just nod 
and do what he asks. “Aye,” she said simply through gritted teeth in her imitation 
of a male’s voice.
“That’s what I like about you, Wounded. You know what to say. Narles 
talks too damned much. Anyway, go check on them, starting with that one over 
there. He looks half-dead.” Lasher pointed at the man next to the Persian who 
looked like he had fainted.
“Aye.”
Lasher turned around and walked into the darkness where there seemed to be 
a cabin Ahlina had never noticed before. In the darkest part of the ship, the slaves 

 
152
crept away from the shadows as if they were alive. Lasher flinched at the slaves as 
if to hurt them and took great pleasure in their fear as he disappeared into the cabin 
and shut the door behind him. 
Ahlina tried to keep her balance as the ship swayed for a moment. Her green 
eyes adjusted to the darkness quickly as she walked toward the injured man. She 
stepped over the African, then the Persian, apologizing by habit as she took her 
glove off and examined the man. She felt his sweaty skin and found there was a 
faint pulse. She spoke Portuguese, “When was the last time you all ate?” No one 
responded. Then she said aloud in an ancient Chinese dialect, “Great, they don’t 
understand what I’m saying. For someone who’s enlightened, how the hell do you 
get yourself in these situations, Ahlina?” 
“Who is Ahlina?” Scape said, smiling shyly up at her.
Khenebu elbowed Scape, causing his shackles to rattle.
Ahlina looked extremely shocked, knowing she was speaking a dialect no 
one here was supposed to understand, not even the noble captain. “How did you 
understand me?” 
“I don’t know, but me and this guy can understand you, right, Khenebu?” 
Scape nudged the slave next to him, who didn’t say a word or even look up. 
“I won’t hurt you,” Ahlina said as she took her ungloved hand off the dying 
man. 
The cabin door slammed open at that instant. “Who’s out there talkin?!” 
Lasher screamed. 
All of the slaves cowered in fear at the loud voice that pierced the darkness. 
Not knowing what to do or say, they all lowered their heads as to not draw any 
attention to themselves. 
“It was me.” Ahlina stood back up on the warped wooden plank in the 
middle of the ship so Lasher could see her. 
“Wounded, why are you talking to the beasts?” he spat. 
“I wanted to know when they ate,” Ahlina said in response to the ridiculous 
question. She looked back at the now very familiar faces of the black slave and the 
Persian slave, then whispered to herself, “Khenebu and Scape.”
“Go get them bread. There is a barrel on the deck. You may have to scoop 
out a couple rats, but it’s a good enough meal for them to eat. And what of that one 
over there?” Lasher pointed a grubby finger at the man with the faint pulse. 
Ahlina looked at the man, not knowing what to say. “He’s dying.”

 
153
“Good, bring him here,” Lasher said with a sinister expression, as if he 
wanted the man to die terribly.
The man was twice Ahlina’s size, but with her martial arts skills, she lifted 
him and his heavy shackles, balancing on the beam and walked toward Lasher. The 
man was breathing faintly, but he was suffering from internal bleeding based on 
what Ahlina knew of anatomy. He was dehydrated and also whipped into a stupor. 
She felt sorry for the man and didn’t know how to ease his pain as she carefully 
walked him to the villainous Lasher.
“I’ll take it from here. You go get the rest of ‘em bread,” Lasher commanded 
as he grabbed the young man and threw him roughly into the dark cabin behind 
him. He looked at Ahlina until she started toward the stairs. The door closed, and 
Ahlina heard a gut-wrenching scream followed by the tearing of human flesh. No 
doubt it was the young man. Ahlina looked over to the Persian and the African, 
who somehow seemed more familiar than anyone she’d ever met, then she turned 
back to the stairs. She simply could not process any more of this horror. 
Ahlina ran as fast as she could to the upper deck and took in a deep breath of 
salty sea breeze. For the first time ever, she found the fishy air refreshing and 
rejoiced at the fact that she was no longer in the hellish pit of this ship. 
“Seems I was right, about you being a little soft on them down there,” 
Captain Emilian said smugly, “didn’t take long before you came runnin’ topside.” 
His handsome face was slightly more charismatic than those of the crew, and his 
style of dress was definitely more fashion-forward, but it didn’t change the eerie 
feeling that touched every man he spoke to. 
His pirate-like speech pattern reminded Ahlina of the vile crew when she’d 
pretended to be a boy just to get passage to the New World. She stood up straight, 
but kept her head low as she spoke in her deepest voice while trying to maintain 
her composure and take the pity out of her tone. “I was simply ordered to get them 
bread, Captain.”
“Is that so? Well, off with you then.”
“Aye, Captain.” Ahlina turned on her heels, and as she did, her senses told 
her to dodge an attack, but it was too late. The Captain was fast, and he moved 
behind her so closely that she could smell the wine on his breath as he spoke into 
the back of her neck. 

 
154
“There’s something different about you, Wounded. I could use someone 
like you in my entourage. Remind me to introduce you to my family when we get 
back to Portugal.” 
Did the Captain just sniff me? Oh, no, I need to get out of here as soon as 
possible. Ahlina simply nodded as she took off toward the poop deck to find the 
bread she was supposed to pick rats from and feed to the slaves. When she turned 
the corner, she overheard a sailor yelling down to the Captain from the crow’s nest.
“Captain, there is a ship following us about a league East. It has been for 
some time now. I thought it was en route to a different destination until it started 
back our way, sir.”
“What kind of ship is it?” Emilian looked into the horizon. “Already less 
than three nautical miles away and traveling at thirty-five knots? It’s moving faster 
than the wind would allow.” 
Ahlina gazed in awe at the Captain’s strange ability to track the movement 
of the ship as he whispered to himself, “Magick.” 
Emilian’s realization caused him to stand at attention and call for the men to 
prepare for combat. “All hands on board! We have a pirate ship headed straight at
us!”
Ahlina was shocked that pirates would attack slave ships, considering most 
had little cargo besides the captives and some fine gifts for the colonies. Usually 
there was nothing of much value to pirates. “But then again, those guys would sell 
their own mothers for profit,” Ahlina whispered as she found the barrel of bread 
and all of the men scrambled past her toward their stations in the front of the ship. I 
have to help Scape and Khenebu get free. We have to get off this ship. As she 
opened the barrel, a bright light came soaring over the sun-setting ocean sky and 
dropped quickly toward the ship. Ahlina wasn’t even noticing the figure hunched 
over in the barrel as she stared upwards to see what the light was. 
A small boy’s voice said in gasping awe, “Fireball!” 
*
Ewm sat in the dark barrel as quiet as he could. He didn’t want to get the 
attention of any of the deckhands because he knew they especially didn’t like 
children. In the bottom of his stomach, he could feel that something was incredibly 

 
155
wrong. He tried to take his mind off his situation and crack open the barrel for just 
enough light to read his new obsession, the old book. As soon as he opened the 
book, he saw Runes of Magick forming on the pages as if they had been waiting 
for him specifically to unlock their mysteries. 
The Runes translated themselves with no command from Ewm, telling the 
tale of a Great War. Sky, the Mancer and Goddess of Magick, along with her 
husband, Aegis, the blue-eyed God of Gods, forced a coven of Wicasht to perform 
a dangerous spell of epic proportions. The Spell of Enlightenment, which was 
meant to make innocent humans into Supernaturals, almost tore the world apart in 
a single night, breaking Pangaea into pieces and causing blood to rain from the 
skies. The Coven of Unity was then forced to reverse the spell and cast another on 
the once peaceful humans. They cast a spell called the Veil of Ignorance on every 
human who would be born from that day forth so they wouldn’t be able to see or 
comprehend Magick ever again. 
This is the truth. Somehow I know this for a fact. My father never told me of 
anything like this, but I can feel it’s the truth. Ewm flipped page after page, 
memorizing every Rune until he came upon a list of spells. “Wow,” he whispered 
to himself as he watched a drawing on the page become animated, showing the 
effect of a spell. The flames were easy enough to conjure, as he only had to say a 
few words. Ewm dared not speak, remembering where he was. Yelling from 
outside interrupted his read as the deckhands scrambled to complete the commands 
of the smug nobleman, Captain Emilian. 
“We are definitely under attack!” Captain Emilian said with certainty. “All 
hands on deck!” The attractive Captain looked for his commanding officers. He 
saw Yanoro tucking his tunic into his britches and wiping his face disgustedly as 
he walked through the doors of the slave pits and stood next to Emilian. “So she 
needed your ‘expertise of the tongue’ again, I see?” Emilian smirked. 
Yanoro blushed and looked out to sea, ignoring the question and obvious 
slap to his ego. His shoulder-length dark brown hair blew in the wind as he 
realized what he was looking at. “Emilian, erm, I mean Captain Emilian, is that 
what I think it is, a pirate ship?!” 
“That’s not a normal ship, if it’s even a ship at all,” Emilian said aloud, more 
to himself than to Yanoro. His mouth opened in awe at the dark smoke that 
surrounded only the black ship as it sailed far too fast for these winds. There 
seemed to be thunder and lightning booming and striking within the dark clouds. 

 
156
Thinking his level of belief couldn’t have been any more disrupted, Emilian saw a 
large fireball catapulted into the slowly darkening sky. The only ball of fire larger 
was the sun itself, which seemed to be sinking into the sea to hide from the 
inevitable battle. 
Emilian saw his men scramble back and forth, trying to decide what course 
of action to take. He hadn’t been one for losing his cool, and today would 
definitely not change that. Besides, he was more afraid of what was in the bowels 
of the ship than he was of any pirates at sea. Emilian breathed in deeply, looked 
around at the panic that was striking the souls of the crew and returned his gaze to 
the sky before saying, “Damage control, prepare to put out the fire. It’s too late to 
steer the ship. Get the cannons ready for return fire. Take cover!” 
Ahlina saw how brave Emilian was in the face of impending doom. She also 
saw the face of an obvious stowaway. The child was no older than nine years of 
age and was staring into the sunset sky at the enormous meteor that was 
descending upon the ship. She heard him announce there was a fireball, so she 
wasn’t afraid of what was behind her, but she did take a step back as she saw the 
boy’s eyes begin to glow. His already dark-red eyes were now a true reflection of 
the flames in the sky. He was holding a book almost the same size as his torso, and 
she could have sworn she saw the pages of the book begin to flip themselves as he 
stood up in the barrel with the salty air blowing through his dark hair. 
“Kid, this isn’t a place for you,” Ahlina said to the seemingly possessed little 
boy. 
“My name is Ewm, and that’s a fireball. Not just any fireball, but a Greek 
flame. It’s meant to keep burning even after it explodes its oily flames all over this 
ship.” He did not smile, but spoke as if her were reading a passage on the fiery 
globe itself. 
Ahlina turned her attention upward and saw what the boy was looking at. 
The spinning Runes of Magick were on this weapon of destruction. She’d been 
almost fully enlightened before she deceived the Captain and made her way aboard 
the ship. Magick was a thing that she had to learn through trial and error. She had 
learned most of her skills from a Monk named Jonuss, who told her that in every 
lifetime she would have difficulty controlling her powers. He taught her a very 
useful form of spellcraft called Mimic Casting. All Ahlina had to do was watch a 
spell being cast and raise her right hand, which was bandaged for this very reason, 
then apply pressure to her palm or forearm. By doing this, she could repeat a spell 

 
157
she’d seen in an instant. She didn’t know how this was going to help her now, 
considering she hadn’t seen the spell of the fireball being cast. 
Ewm released the book, but somehow it floated in front of his small frame. 
The pages stopped at a spell called ‘Greek Meteor’ where a picture of a flaming 
orb began to form, similar to the one in the sky. The Runes of Magick on the page 
manifested themselves in reality, and Ewm started to speak the words, “Miscreo 
Ignemitus.” His hands lit up like the sun, and a blinding light shot forth from his 
left palm toward the sky in the direction of the fireball. A searing heat blasted into 
the air and was gone as fast as it had been conjured. 
Ahlina watched in amazement while this small boy cast one of the most 
powerful spells she’d ever seen. Her palm began to light up as Runes of Magick 
began to spin violently up her fingertips, wrist and forearm, then back down again. 
From underneath her bandages, the Runes kept repeating a shivering vibration and 
light show that she could see clearly. Ahlina looked at her glowing arm, and then at 
the boy who looked like he was going to pass out from exhaustion. She turned her 
focus toward the sky and saw what looked like two suns about to collide. The 
fireball from the boy was a crimson hue that seemed to make the ocean glow like 
boiling blood. It was larger than the one coming from the dark ship, which was a 
blinding yellow. 
Without losing any momentum, Ewm’s red fireball smashed into the yellow 
flames and engulfed them with explosive force that rocked the sea underneath, 
along with both ships. Ahlina was in awe as their lives were saved, but she quickly 
felt the burning Runes from underneath her bandages as they seared through the 
cloth and outward around her palm and forearm. Scared to cause unanticipated 
effects, Ahlina pointed her palm out at sea toward the other ship. Without saying 
the words Ewm had recited, Ahlina just wanted the burning to stop and thought 
about the red fireball that was still in the sky, making its way toward the black 
ship. In that thought, a large pink ball of flames ignited the very air, and instead of 
flying in a catapulted volley, shot in a straight line across the water toward the 
black ship at blinding speed. Ahlina fell backwards into the barrel with Ewm. 
The barrel tipped over, but not before both Ewm and Ahlina watched as two 
huge explosions nearly destroyed the ship across the water from them. The 
resulting force rocked the Relίquia almost completely sideways. Ahlina 
instinctively turned her back to the blast and wrapped her arms around the boy. She 
could feel his shallow breathing on her collarbone, but the heat did no damage to 

 
158
her back. She sat up while the ship was trying to rebalance itself on the chaotic 
waters, her arms still wrapped around the boy. 
“Boy, are you alright?” she said, still not looking at him, but rather staring at 
the flaming ship. The clouds looming on the horizon seemed to appear angry, 
growing with terrifying speed. 
“It’s Ewm, pronounced ‘Oh mm,’ and I’m fine.” He noticed the fading 
Runes on Ahlina’s forearm and reached to touch them. 
With martial arts reflexes, Ahlina parried Ewm’s hand and pulled her aching 
wrist away from him. “Don’t touch me.” She breathed heavily, staring at her flesh 
as it slowly lost the pink glowing Runes of Magick. She then looked back at the 
boy and said, “Either you’re Witchbreed or Broken, but either way, you’re really 
lucky we pulled that off?!” 
Ewm sat up and said, “Wicasht, and I only recently found that out through 
reading this book.” He gripped the large leather bound book with a large eye on the 
cover with extreme intensity as if to never let it go. He gripped the large leatherbound book with extreme intensity as if to never let it go, its blue eye shining in 
the sunlight. “They aren’t finished fighting, and I believe I’m the one they’re

Of course you are, Ahlina thought to herself. I can’t even get across the 
damned ocean without some Supernatural hook up along the way. This is starting

definitely Supernaturals. It’s the only way we could’ve understood each other. Ok,

Whatever it is, it doesn’t look like it’s going to stop chasing this boy. The clouds

“Ewm, can you stand?” Ahlina said calmly. 
“Yes. I think so,” Ewm said, now whining like a small child in contrast to 
the assertive tone he took when casting his Magick. His eyes even changed back to 
a red that was softer than the glowing dark blood color they once were. 
“Good. You’re going to have to hide, okay?” Ahlina looked around at the 
other barrels that must have exploded in the pandemonium. “Try to find 

 
159
somewhere close to here so I can find you when I come back.” She pointed at the 
door to the crew’s food storage room.
Hopping gracefully to her feet, Ahlina walked over to the door. She didn’t 
want to touch the door with her right hand, so she reached down with her left, 
which was still gloved. The door easily opened, and inside was a dark room that 
was small compared to any of the other rooms on the ship. It was covered in debris 
and shattered glass from crates, barrels and jars that had tipped over and broken. 
The smell of pickles and something sweet filled her nostrils. The gunk on the floor 
was sticky, but at least there were burlap sacks Ewm could hide behind while she 
proceeded on her suicide mission to save strangers. 
Ewm ran over to the corner with no instruction needed. He clung to his 
book, and before she knew it, he had disappeared into the shadows. 
“I’ll be back, kid.” Ahlina fixed her hat and closed the door behind her, but 
when she turned around, Narles the Snitch was standing in front of her.
“Why are you coming from the food pantry at a time like this, Wounded?” 
Narles’ suspicious tone seemed to hiss at her.
“I’m following the Captain’s orders to feed the slaves! Do you want to take 
it up with Captain Emilian or should I tell him you had something more important 
for me to do?” Ahlina watched as the deviant’s expression jumped from curiosity 
to fear in an instant. Apparently this large rat was no different than the slaves he 
was helping to transport and was trained to be subservient. He said nothing in 
reply. “I didn’t think so,” she continued, pointing to the food pantry door, “I was in 
there; I heard a loud explosion, and the ship rocked sideways, so everything is 
broken.” Ahlina was proud of herself for thinking on her feet. She had always been 
quick-witted, but this was the first time she had used her wits to save someone 
else’s life. “Don’t you have something better to do than question my orders from 
the Captain?” 
Narles would have been a ruggedly handsome brunette if it weren’t for his 
terrible hygiene and black-toothed smile. He sneered at Ahlina, but the story made 
sense, so he scrambled past her without further inquiry and took off running 
toward the crow’s nest where the Captain asked him to retrieve a report. He was 
suspicious of almost everyone, but Wounded seemed to bring about a sort of 
paranoia. 
Trying to catch her breath as soon as Narles was out of sight, Ahlina ran 
toward the slave quarters entrance. She saw the other crew members running 

 
160
around in a panic, but all glad to be alive. Emilian was screaming at everyone to

She took a few steps backward at the sight of a woman in beautifully crafted

had ever seen before. The woman’s hair was a dark brown, and her lips were

She saw both handsome men still chained next to each other. Scape was

“I have to help you both get out of here,” Ahlina said, trying to figure out

“Why did you come back?” Scape asked, surprised to see her.
“I heard explosions,” Khenebu said before Ahlina could answer the

Ahlina looked truly perplexed and answered Scape, “Because I don’t believe

“I don’t think you’re strong enough to break them. We’ve tried for days

161
have the key and had come down here unprepared. Then he gasped as he saw her

Scape’s legs were finally free, but he couldn’t believe it. He felt the bloody

“So you have a soft spot for these slaves, huh? Well I think it’s about time I

162

163
“You piece of shit. You won’t inflict any more pain on anyone else. You’ll

164

“I do,” Khenebu said, stepping next to Scape. He noticed the look on

165
That means you’ll be paralyzed until I say so. I can see why these people are

Sasha’s dark brown hair was frozen almost as stiff as her bones. Suddenly

Ahlina stood up and stretched until she heard a crack of relief in her neck.

Scape walked over to Sasha but kept a safe distance. “You’re right, Ahlina.

166
reality-bending illusions. Until now I didn’t believe these things existed, but I

Khenebu almost laughed at how easily he was able to control Sasha. “I’ve 
only encountered a couple of your kind, but unfortunately, they did not survive.” 
He moved his hand like a puppeteer, and Sasha’s foot lifted off Ewm. 
“Thank you,” Scape said as he helped Ewm to his feet while carefully

Khenebu made a gesture with his lips, and she copied it identically.
“I’ll kill you all for this--” Sasha’s sentence was interrupted as her mouth snapped

“No, I don’t think you’ll be killing any of us today,” Khenebu corrected her,

Ahlina went to the rowboats and told everyone to jump in as she filled them 
with sacks of food. Once the four of them boarded the small boat, Ahlina realized

Images were flooding back to them so rapidly that it seemed as if they were in a

167
Sasha leered over the edge of the ship with hatred only the darkest tales 
could conjure. She did not order them to attack the small boat, but Narles took it

“You’ll die today, he growled. “You won’t get away from my master,

Ahlina saw the harpoon coming toward the slowly drifting boat, about to

Sasha saw the glare with her heightened Vampyl senses. Upset that Narles

America.
***
(San Francisco: Present Day)
“So that’s who the new guy really is!” Ahlina said to Scape as they watched 
Khenebu walk smoothly down the hallway. Ahlina pulled out a cigarette and struck 
her Zippo lighter. 

 
168
Scape didn’t even look at her as he grabbed the cigarette and smashed it 
under his boot heel. He continued to stare at Khenebu. So we met on a slave ship in 
another past life, and now we meet again.
