Chapter 20
FIGURE IT OUT
Jayde sat up from her meditation. Her piercing green eyes opened slowly as 
she absorbed the surroundings of the ancient city she once called home. She wasn’t 
a sentimental person by any means, but the feeling of nostalgia could prove her 
wrong. If anything, she had never wanted to return to the Forbidden City, 
Zalomoni, but according to her mentor, Gemini, this was the only option.
The walls are as cold as the bones that are now dust in the very valleys that 
make up this forgotten place. I personally trained so many of our kind to fight as 
proud warriors, priestesses and monks for what they believed in, and look where 
that got them. I’m not one for second-guessing myself, but this was one of my 
greatest failures. Gemini would disagree and say it was meant to be, but I can’t see 
how watching Spiraar devour thousands of Supernaturals, nobles and warriors I’d 
grown to love has any relevance to the current situation. I’m sure Gemini would 
say something like, ‘Jayde, in the big picture, if it weren’t for their great sacrifice, 
the Coven of Unity would not be united, nor would the Mancer kingdoms of human 
lore have ever existed.’ Jayde sat up with a frustrated expression, and in her clarity 
said aloud, “He’d be right too.” 
Though this is a grove of extreme power and secrecy, I told him it’s the last 
place on Earth I’d actually want to be. He knows this place makes me feel 
uncomfortable, and I can feel Kirari stirring restlessly in my chest, awaiting the 
chance to burst forth and sleep within the temple once again. I need to take my 
mind off this subject and find the Mancers that will turn the tables on the extinction 
of our kind and the Magick that must be preserved. Let me sense where they are. 
Breathing deeply, I should be able to feel the Ki that makes up the few living 
beings in this dead city. Of course, they are about to start mayhem in the 
Blacksmith’s temple. 
Jayde stretched her firm muscles and stalked out of the prayer circle within 
the dusty temple that had not seen life since before the Ice Age. Before leaving the 
once immaculate hall, she turned and bowed in respect. Her long black hair almost 
touched the floor in her deep bow, and she lifted her head in remembrance of the 
souls that used to clean this very place daily. A tear nearly fell from her eye before 
she caught it and looked at it, its transparent sheen alien-like on her finger tip. She 

 
170
smiled for the first time in a long while and dropped the tear on the ground as if it 
were dew on a fresh blade of grass in the morning. Leaving the tear and the hall, 
she now had the conviction she needed to continue her mission and prevent her 
people’s lives and deaths from being in vain. 
*
Blue walked into the dark hall as if to guard Surge-Overload from any 
impending doom. Though he didn’t know exactly how he was going to protect her, 
he decided that the best choice would be to walk in front of her. 
Surge was so excited about finding this secret passageway into a dark cavern 
with Blue, that she failed to analyze the kind of danger she may have truly been in. 
She looked at the walls and the wonderful hieroglyphics. “Wow, look at the star 
charts! I bet Maia would totally get a kick out of this. And o-m-g, look at the 
paintings, Blue! This place was some sort of blacksmithing forge. I thought you 
were going to lead me to technology?”
“Shhh.” Blue tried to quiet the ranting teenager. “Did you hear that? It 
sounds like something is in here with us.”
“You’re just saying that to scare me,” Surge said, half-hoping Blue was 
joking for once. After realizing he was serious, she hushed and listened for the 
sound that alarmed her handsome body guard. “This isn’t funny, Blue.”
Blue gave Surge a grave look that silenced her. He focused on the dark halls that 
seemed to be only dimly lit where there were Runes of Magick. The blue aura they 
cast on the cave walls allowed them to be read, though few were actually 
understood. The silence was both comforting and slightly creepy. Blue knew they 
were being watched. He acted as if he weren’t paying attention to the stalker that 
must have been lurking in the shadows above them. 
Both Mancers walked slowly as Blue tested booby traps throughout the 
cave. He sustained injuries from sharp arrows and crushing stones that retreated 
back into the walls. Surge was both afraid to move without Blue and intrigued as 
she read the Runes on the walls, confirming his suspicion that this place led to a 
Temple of not just forging, but technology. 

 
171
Surge couldn’t believe how much pain Blue could withstand. She thought he 
was going to die every time he triggered another trap. If she could make an 
educated guess, she would swear he was triggering them on purpose to protect her. 
Surge tried to tell him that each one was a puzzle, but Blue, too busy looking into 
the shadows, absentmindedly triggered yet another well-devised death sentence for 
any mortal. Each trap he walked through was one more demonstration of his 
immortality. 
The Runes became increasingly mesmerizing as they told a tale of the 
Forbidden City of Zalomoni. There were thousands of Supernaturals that once 
lived here. The center of their civilization and culture was a dragon named Kirari 
that slept within the largest temple in the city. A blacksmithing Mancer by the 
name of Nadir had lived in this specific temple like a hermit. He didn’t particularly 
like visitors, so he had designed creatures known as Golems to watch over his 
sacred space. 
“Blue, this is some really cool stuff,” Surge said as she read the Runes, not 
paying attention to where she was stepping. A stone was pressed delicately by her 
small combat boots, and before she could react, a long spear shot from the walls 
toward her head. Surge didn’t even have time to scream before Blue dashed from 
his location in the large hall, grabbing her protectively as he ran as fast as he could 
deeper within the cave. When Blue slowed down, Surge looked back to where she 
had been standing to see hundreds of metal spears crossed over each other as if 
creating a deadly design. Upon further inspection, she noticed they formed the 
elaborate shape of an eye. 
Blue asked Surge if she was ok. She caught her breath and said, “Thanks to 
you, I am.” She looked intently at the spears and pointed at them as they slowly 
started to retreat back into the wall. “What is that?” Surge hugged Blue as she 
noticed the body of a creature impaled by the booby trap. 
Blue followed Surge’s gaze to the bloody mass. With its iron embrace, the 
eye that was once formed by the spears now held its prey, which looked like some 
kind of beast. 
Unrecognizable as the thing was to Blue from this distance, he was quite 
sure it was what had been following them in the shadows. “That’s our stalker! I 
knew we weren’t alone here. I wonder if Jayde and Gemini know about this?”
“I’m sure if they don’t, they’ll figure it out soon enough. What is that thing? 
Looks like some anthropomorphic creature with human and beastlike features from 

 
172
a fairytale, only way more scary ‘cause it’s huge!” Surge said, horrified, as the 
spears slowly slid out of the creature and back into the wall. It let out a loud 
screeching howl that echoed throughout the darkness as the final spear left its 
body. Surge jumped yet again, hugging Blue’s hips as he stood in front of her 
instinctively. 
The creature began writhing in pain on the ground, and after a few spasms, 
stopped moving until it began to shapeshift right in front of them, becoming a 
large, lifeless insect. The blood on the ground pooled around the shrinking carcass 
like a pond with a dead fish floating belly-up, the prey remaining still in the center.
“It’s dead now, that’s for sure.” Blue said, slowly petting Surge’s back to 
calm her. “See, you triggering the booby trap accomplished my goal. Flush out the 
stalker.”
“I knew it! You were purposely setting off those traps.” 
“Of course I was. I could easily dodge them, but I wondered if they would 
expose themselves,” Blue said, proud of himself, placing his hands over the 
wounds that had healed minutes ago, but were still sore as if someone had punched 
him. 
“You’re stupid,” Surge said as if she couldn’t believe Blue would put his 
own life in danger to save hers. “And I didn’t mean to almost get impaled by 
hundreds of spikes, thank you very much.” She released his chiseled waist and 
took a step back. “What I really mean to say is, thank you.” She smiled up at him 
with her eyes closed. 
Blue half-smiled back as he began to scan the room behind Surge, but before 
he could answer, he said “What in the F?”
Surge quickly turned her attention to what Blue was looking at. “I know I 
say this a lot, but O-M-G!” Surge gasped. “Looks like we found the machines the 
Runes were talking about, and they’re nothing like anything I’ve ever seen 
before!”
Blue and Surge stood still as they inspected the room full of large Golems 
staring coldly back at them. The stone and metal monoliths looked as if they hadn’t 
moved in centuries, but were definitely instruments of war and destruction as the 
Runes of Magick on their shells clearly stated. They were two-legged tanks with 
hulking arms and Runes of Magick written all over them. Surge recognized them 
immediately as the constructs Nadir had written about on the walls. “It never said 

 
173
Nadir completed them though, or maybe I didn’t get that far before you picked me 
up.”
“Whatever you do, don’t move,” Blue said to the now-fascinated Surge 
quietly, as if the Golems would be triggered to kill them by sound.
Surge didn’t move a muscle as she translated a word from one of the Runes 
aloud: “Femtotechnology.”

(OZONE Base, Colorado Springs, CO: Present Day)
“Someone get me a report of Surge-Overload’s damage right now!” Deme 
screamed at the Receptionists without discrimination. “I want to know what that 
little bitch did to our mainframes and intranet! I want to know all the lost files that 
were compromised and the data that was corrupted!” Deme didn’t stop looking at 
the monitor as he barked his orders. 
He had been getting chewed out since the death of the most powerful hacker 
the world had ever known, Surge-Overload. He thought that a recon mission to kill 
the threat would get him kudos, and yes, he received congratulations from his 
Commander and Chief, but in the same breath he got a mouthful of slander due to 
the damage that was already assessed. Deme hadn’t failed at anything in his short 
but decorated career as an OZONE elite, and he didn’t plan on making a habit of 
being ridiculed. 
When the files reported an anonymous girl was responsible for the most 
notorious quantum slider hacks of all time, outsmarting even Deme’s most 
intelligent Technomages, Deme almost lost his sanity from disbelief at what he 
was hearing. Of course the only person who got a good look at the girl and was 
still alive happened to be the most epic fail Agent, Narles. 
“Do we have any progress on Narles’ condition?” Deme stopped looking at 
his laser monitor with an obvious expression of annoyance. Before he could speak 
again, a neatly cut brunette Technomage by the name of Shadow Harvester 
announced, “Surge-Overload has stolen and deleted over three thousand files. They 

 
174
include twelve Braintech files on the Nanotechnology of the Zenith Program, a top 
secret project meant to increase agent performance, which was supposed to—”
“Was supposed to turn even the weakest Soldier into an elite Supernatural 
hunter like Guardian,” Deme retorted, “Each Nano, theoretically, was a 
supercomputer with terabytes of information essentially replacing any and all cells. 
Granting our forces hyper-regeneration, cellular repair, weather resistance, and 
contagion immunity was the next step in OZONE evolution and new world order. 
That was the greatest invention Braintech had produced since its inception.” 
Shadow Harvester’s gaze sunk to the floor in embarrassment. She frantically 
pulled up the field agents’ reports, knowing she needed to find some way to deliver 
good news. “Surge-Overload is dead, sir. Agent TJ identified a child’s remains at 
the site and verified that they were supernatural.”
“You’re confirming the elimination of a high-priority target without a full 
DNA scan and a coroner’s report?” Deme asked. 
Shadow Harvester’s head sunk lower than ever. 
“I didn’t think so,” he said, “now get back to your desk before you insult my 
intelligence a third time and end up like Narles, or better yet, like one of Agent 
Zeer’s sex puppets. Which sounds more amusing to you?”
The mortified agent had already returned to her desk before she could reply.
Deme stared at the screen before him as if trying to crush it with pure
willpower. There is no way to reproduce that nanotechnology without the specs 
and none of us can figure it out in this lifetime without the blue prints. At least the 
secrets died with her...hopefully. 
“God damn you, Surge-Overload!” Deme slammed his hands on the desk 
and looked down at his balled fists, knowing in some way or other he’d be blamed 
for the loss of such delicate intel. His young face twisted into a hateful grimace as 
he spoke again. “Give me the full damage report! And tell me when Narles is 
awake!”

Chapter 21
OLD ENEMIES
“It has come to our attention that an old foe has arisen.” The booming voice 
demanded attention from the coterie of allies gathered in a dark hall within the 
French catacombs, their ethereal forms seated around a sacred blood circle after 
traveling through the spirit realm, Oneira. “I, Prince Lord Ka will do anything in 
my power to assure you they will fall once more.”
A regal-looking female Witchbreed stood up to address the congregation. 
“With all due respect, Ka, your Vampyl can’t be sure it was Aegis they saw. I 
mean seriously—” 
“With all due respect, Ophiuchus, the blood running through the corpse of 
one of my Generals was not human nor Witchbreed, it was the rarest of 
Supernaturals, Mancer!” Ka hissed at the Witchbreed heretic who dared challenge 
his words. He knew she was powerful, but he had an army of darkness that was all 
his own, and she’d be in for a hell of a fight if she mocked him again. “My 
General, Chass, reported who and what he saw. Not only was Aegis alive and well, 
but so was Isis.”
“Call me Ophi,” the blonde said nicely, with as much demand as the 
commanding voice of the Vampyl lord. “I have consorted with my coven, and we 
have not heard any news of this.” 
“As much as I hate to agree with Prince Lord Ka, he’s right.” Nef appeared 
to be the youngest in the company, yet he was the oldest immortal in the room. “I 
was graced with the presence of one of our old friends, Gemini.” He smiled as if 
remembering their conversation. “He has no clue that we are indeed the ones who 
have created OZONE. Ophiuchus, he doesn’t even know you are still on this plane 
of existence, let alone alive. Zenith, he doesn’t know that you faked your death in 
Zalomoni. Osiris is still nowhere to be found, but his cults, ruled by Prince Lord 
Ka, have been tormenting humanity for the greater part of five thousand years. I, 
Neflym, am the only one he remotely knows about besides the one he killed, 
Enoch, my fellow brother of the First Immortals.” Nef stopped speaking, 
understanding that he was delivering information that the members of his coterie 
already knew. 

 
176
“So we haven’t met in centuries, and all of a sudden I have been awakened 
to sit in a room full of Immortals who are so full of themselves, they let these 
Mancers slip right through their fingers,” Ophi said impatiently. Her blonde hair 
fell down to her waist in a cascade of gold around the mask she was wearing to 
hide her face. The vermillion mask was one of many she was known to wear, each 
of which had a serpent fashioned into its design in one way or another. She was 
looking at her allies who were more like liabilities than equals. 
Prince Lord Ka was growing weary of these old wind bags. They were 
arguably the most powerful alliance in the world, and yet they could barely agree 
on the sky being blue, let alone what to do with the Immortal enemies that had 
recently been reborn and would inevitably challenge their power as per their 
birthright. “My legion did not let them slip through their hands. They were spared 
by the Mancers.”
“I do not doubt this is what happened, Prince Lord Ka,” Neflym said 
respectfully. “What we do next will decide the fate of OZONE.” 
“Kill them!” Prince Lord Ka said abruptly.
“I second that motion.” Ophi raised her milky hand from under her elaborate 
robes embroidered with diamonds and sapphires. 
For the first time since the formal introduction, Zenith spoke. She was a 
dark-skinned woman with hair that could be mistaken for a void in the time-space 
continuum. Her voice was quiet, and she always refused to repeat herself. Her 
white lips were a direct contrast to the rest of her dark physique and ominous 
essence, but forced all to pay attention to their movement. “I think it is a mistake to 
kill them now.”
Neflym could not hide his smile as he spoke. “I have thought long nights 
about this, and I agree with Zenith, we should wait for the right moment. Since 
Enoch is dead, and we are awaiting his replacement, it would seem we have a 
stalemate.”
“So I am not allowed to kill the Mancers?” Prince Lord Ka asked, 
unamused.
“That is correct,” Nef said.
“What about the Coven of Unity?” The dark lord’s rebuttal was that of 
someone who was withholding information.

 
177
“What do you mean?” Ophi turned her attention to Ka. “Speak, vampire. 
And I thought we were to refrain from using the name of that insolent coven in my 
presence?” 
“My humblest apologies, Ophiuchus,” Ka said unapologetically. “It has also 
been brought to my attention that the Coven of Unity has been reborn.”
“Lies!” Ophi cried, stirring restlessly on her throne. To the onlookers it was 
the simple hatred she had for this coven that caused her outburst. Ophi however 
knew what this meant. The Coven of Unity and the Mancers equal the end of 
OZONE, if properly directed. If Neflym is correct, then Gemini is playing a 
dangerous game of Chess. 
“If this is true, then we are in danger. All of us.”
Zenith spoke once more, “We must not allow the Witchbreed to return, for 
they will be the downfall of OZONE, as they were the downfall of Olympus.”
“Spoken like the wise woman you are, Zenith,” Neflym added. “Where is 
this proof, Prince Lord Ka?” Neflym smiled curiously at the Vampyl prince, 
awaiting his answer.
“This is the proof.” Prince Lord Ka raised his hand, palm facing the sky as a 
purple light began to swirl in the air above. This powerful sorcerer was ancient by 
all means of the word and had strong reality-shifting powers that made him 
formidable. He was currently using his powers of scrying to show the coterie what 
he wanted them to see. “This is a dungeon in one of my castles.” The dark light 
burst forth and surrounded the company, projecting the live image of a beautiful 
girl pacing about a dungeon cell. Her hair, as violet as the flowers on a spring day, 
danced around her, as if every follicle were alive. She was singing an enchanting 
song that caused small glowing flowers to bloom in this dark place where no life 
could exist for long, though they seemed to thrive in her presence and illuminate 
the torture chamber in which she was being held captive.
“Heaven!” Ophi shrieked. “How are your Vampyl coming across so many of 
these villains?” 
“One could say luck,” Ka smiled without looking at the masked witch. 
“Luck, bah! A fairytale we sold to humans,” Ophi hissed. “You will tell us 
soon enough. In the meantime, we need to kill her. Now!”
“If the Coven of Unity and the Mancers are reborn, then this can also turn 
out to be a perfect opportunity to turn them against Gemini and get them to work 
for us,” Neflym said. “Currently, most of our agents think these kids are dead. I 

 
178
don’t believe that for one second. Gemini is more cunning than to let them all 
explode in a Pagoda in Japan. The slap in our faces was the fact that he didn’t have 
them posted safely in a Grove.” Neflym paused with a gasp. “That’s exactly where 
they are! We need to round up the Coven of Unity and keep them alive for now 
until we decide what to do with them.”
“We need to find those Mancers, if what you say is true, and they are still 
alive,” Ophi said. 
“I have one of them so I will have my blood lines find the others. Better yet, 
I’ll have them come to me,” Prince Lord Ka said triumphantly. 
“How do you plan on doing that?” Ophi asked.
“I already have one of them. That’s all the bait I need.” A sinister smile 
formed on Ka’s face as he began to craft a master plan in his head.
“Not if I don’t find them first,” Ophi said, smiling under her mask. “I have 
waited over five hundred years to repay them for what they did to my face and to 
the rest of my coven.”
“Vengeance won’t be the path we take, Ophi,” Nef said, nonchalant. “We 
tried countless times before. If we kill them, they’ll only be reborn. If we have 
control of them while they are alive, we win.”
“I can still have a little fun with them. Alive is all we need,” Ophi spat 
hatefully. 
“It would seem the race is back on to see how the world will end, this time.” 
Nef laughed aloud. “May the best leader win!”
At his final words, all four entities disappeared, returning whence they came. 
The silence was deafening, but speaking to himself, Neflym said aloud, “Your 
move, Gemini.”

Chapter 22
THE OTHER WOMAN
(Pangaea: 250,001,100 B.C.)
“Blue, you know how much I love you?” Kaery asked as they lay on the 
grassy field coiled together lovingly. The spring day was so beautiful that the 
words came out of Kaery’s mouth effortlessly. So distracted by the clouds in the 
open sky overhead, she continued without listening to his answer, “So much so, it 
hurts me inside to be in this situation.”
“Kaery, please don’t be like this,” Blue said so gently it made her heart 
flutter. He knew he loved her, and this was the conversation he had dreaded for the 
greater part of a thousand years, but he was willing to have it for the sake of the 
golden-eyed beauty in his arms. 
“I know you have to be the God of Gods, or in our case the Mancer of 
Supernaturals, but I don’t understand my role. I just sit in the background, listening 
and watching as if I were a shadow.” Kaery almost came to tears. 
Blue knew she was hurt, and it pained him more than battle to see her this 
way. “I don’t want you to feel like you don’t matter. You are my love. Nothing can 
change that.”
“I know you speak the truth, but it doesn’t change the fact that you’re 
married to Sky.”
“What would you have me do? Leave her? And go to some other unknown 
world where we can escape together and live by ourselves?” Blue asked 
sarcastically, but realized the error of his ways when Kaery let go of his grip and 
sat up. 
“I don’t think this is funny.” Kaery was pouting as she looked off into the 
horizon at the large and majestic sun. “Use your powers and send me back to the 
Soulstream, please!”
“You know that even if my powers worked on you, I’d never use them to 
harm you!” 
“I’d rather die than feel like this.” Kaery began to sob. 

 
180
Blue sat up to comfort her. “You know that my wife is the will of the council 
and the will of the world.”
“But you love her.” Kaery looked into Blue’s sapphire eyes. 
“Yes, I do. I mean, I have learned to love her, but I am in love with you.” 
His words were so genuine that he neither flinched nor stuttered. This only made 
Kaery’s eyes tear up even more. 
She didn’t try to wipe the tears from her eyes. She only laid back into his 
loving embrace. He’d taken his armor off, and his bare chest was warm to the 
touch. In the embrace of the most powerful Mancer, Kaery should have felt like the 
most protected woman in the world, but she believed she was insignificant and 
vulnerable. She couldn’t turn her head to face him because she knew how 
emotional she was becoming over this situation. 
“Why can’t we just run away?” Kaery asked the question knowing it was 
rhetorical. Blue was a Mancer who was meant to lead. His specific task was to rule 
the other leaders of this new planet earth. Blue was paired with a Mancer named 
Sky, who was actually beautiful and courageous, as well as cunning and generous. 
Kaery had already felt bad the first time she slept with Sky’s husband, but he 
continued to pursue her and had proclaimed his love for her many times. Sky had 
the power to see everything and control everything within a sphere around her. 
Currently the Sphere covered most of the planet, so there was technically nowhere 
the two could hide from her. 
“The only reason we can even have our love affair is that Sky can’t use her 
powers on you, nor can anyone for that matter,” Blue said to Kaery, “Your Avatar 
armor that serves as your skin is the only thing that shields us from Sky’s everscrying eyes. I would be with only you if my marriage to Sky did not unify 
Pangaea.”
“Enough!” Kaery’s voice involuntarily rose to the sound of thunder crushing 
a mountain, causing every bird on the horizon to fly from location. It took her a 
moment to regain some semblance of composure as her voice returned to its 
normal pitch. “I’m sorry, but I just can’t think of any other option than for me to 
stop seeing you.”
“Please, Kaery, don’t do this to me! I love you! I always have. I’ve proven 
my love for you. You wouldn’t want me to break the world apart for the sake of 
our love, would you? If so, then let me know, and I will make it so.”

 
181
Kaery looked at Blue for the first time with a selfish glint in her eyes. Her 
pain was somehow overshadowed with something more rich and intoxicating, 
hope. “You would do that for me?”
“Without a second thought, I’d do anything for you,” Blue said as he pulled 
Kaery’s sun-touched skin towards his pale muscles. His black hair blew in the 
wind, contrasting her long brown mane. He was prepared for the inevitable 
destruction of the world to keep her at his side. 
Kaery knew he was telling the truth, and the thought of her selfish act 
sickened her. She laid her head on his hard chest, listening to his steady heartbeat. 
It was a sound she would take to the grave with her before she allowed the world to 
be destroyed because of her mistake. She kissed his skin, and he moved to her 
touch as expected, in bliss. 
Blue closed his eyes as Kaery kissed his chest and moved her way up to his 
neck. He held her head still, staring into her eyes that burned through him like the 
sunrise defeating the darkness. He rolled over on top of her, still looking into the 
golden orbs that helped him forget about the pressures of the outside world. He 
somehow knew that she was contemplating leaving him even after his confession. 
Before his heart broke, he kissed her. When their lips touched, it was as if it were 
the first time he’d taken a breath. Her sweet lips were so soft, he couldn’t think 
straight. He didn’t want this emotionally deafening moment to end, and as he said

Kaery knew without a doubt that Blue loved her. 
I can’t allow him to do what I know he is thinking, just for me. Sky had him 
first, and she will keep him. I will always love him, but from the shadows. His lips 
make me want to cry. His smell is musky and like the river. His black hair I can fall

As much as he takes my breath away, with every second he touches my lips,

182
“Kaery, if you’d only trust me enough to know that I’d give up everything

Her skin makes me want nothing more in this world. I want to be the only

The way she moans in my ears with every thrust and the pain I feel from her

I’d happily take for her. 

 
183

Blue’s hands continued to rub the soft flesh of Kaery’s lower back and the

life, his immortal life, tears of anguish fell from his eyes as he finally understood

Sky smiled to herself as she looked all over the planet at once. She noticed

“Is that so? He has eluded me all day, which is abnormal. He rarely does 
this. Well, if you could tell him that I’m looking for him if he shows up to your

“Please stop bowing,” Sky lifted Scape’s head up noticing his slight shiver,

“Yes. I am. I just feel like the Grove isn’t presentable to such a distinguished

“May I look around your beautiful home?” Sky asked politely. 
“Of course you may,” Scape said, knowing that Kaery and Blue were

“As you know, Sky, we are still in the midst of retrieving the items we need

187
“You are ever so humble, dear Druid Scape.” Sky stopped walking and

Scape did not know what to say. He felt his skin light up in the moonlight. 
He looked around and as she uttered the words, he was able to see something he

188

Kaery screamed in tantric ecstasy for what seemed the thirteenth time in a

Sky had just flown from the Coven of Unity’s Grove after blessing Scape for

189
Sky put her hands over her mouth as if she would be heard from a distance. She

For Sky, Blue’s delicate and loving words to Kaery were like a spell that had

Blue stroked Kaery’s hair and looked over his shoulder at the thunderous

“Blue, we can no longer do this,” Kaery whispered into his ear. She stood up 
and started walking toward her Grove. She didn’t even look back to see the 
anguish on his face that was as harmful as the storm in the distance. Her heart 
broke, and she wept with every harsh and inevitably painful step. As she crossed 
the threshold of the Grove’s Magick, the storm was no longer the music that 
overpowered her sobs. She fell to her knees, and with every bone-wrenching 
convulsion, cried, “Why?”

Chapter 23
TEACH ME HOW TO FIGHT
“How many times do I have to tell you, lead in with your left foot like this?” 
Crux said impatiently.
“Shut up, you little brat. I’m trying to get this,” Star said. “You’re a crappy

don’t know why you insist on wearing those… stilts.”

teach me the stupid kick.”
Crux stood back and got into a fighting stance. He lowered his body, then 
leapt off the ground and did a spin kick as high as Star’s face. The twelve-year-old 
landed gracefully like a cat. “Like that.”
Star had seen the kick a dozen times before this and had only agreed to the 
lesson because Crux was right, she didn’t know how to fight, though she’d never 
admit it. She tried to mimic his stance and dropped as low as she could drop in her 
mini dress and six-inch stilettos. She jumped up and did a high kick with her long 
legs, surprising even herself. But as she landed, her heel turned to the side, and she 
crashed hard on her butt. 
Crux cringed at the terrible looking fall, even though he was proud of her for 
trying. The kick looked legit, but before he could say anything, a female voice 
came from behind him.
“Whoa, that was painful looking, as in what the hell are you thinking? 
‘Cause I’m uploading that feed to my site right now. #whatthehellwasstarthinking, 
like right now.” Ahlina laughed so hard, she could barely press the buttons on her 
hacked smart phone. 
Star looked up at her from the dirty patch where she had landed. The dust 
still in the air, she managed to pop up onto her feet with no visible dirt on her dress 
then said, “If you do, I’ll kill you, then post that video of you kissing that old guy 
when you were drunk. #ahlinaisaslut, now what?”
When Ahlina lunged toward her phone, Star did a pirouette. 

 
191
“Okay, guys stop,” Kaery said, “What is going on here? Star, why are you

“Fine, we delete at the same time.”
“Fine!”
Both girls showed each other their phones as the files were erased. 
Kaery shook her head and asked, “Why is she doing jump kicks, Crux?
Crux shrugged and replied, “If that’s what you call that. I’m trying to teach

be prepared, or you’re going to get slaughtered, literally.” 
For a moment, Kaery’s eyes glowed without the help of the sun. She knew

Kaery smiled and turned her attention to the preteen who had a smug look

Crux focused his attention on the seventeen-year-old ‘girl next door.’ He

(Zalomoni: Present-Day)
Maia had been walking through the Ruins for hours, which could have easily 
become days had she not been aware of the Mancer time paradox. She knew that 
all Mancers were born with two definite things. Immortality prevented death from 

 
194
time itself, though most could be destroyed by other means. Its companion, time 
paradox, caused them to lose track of time and, by extension, reality if they didn’t 
meditate regularly, focusing on the here and now.
She had been wandering around the Ruins, taking in her surroundings 
through her Oracular Sight. The dusty walls somehow cleared up and showed 
themselves as they once were before the Ice Age. This city was beautiful and 
absolutely one of a kind. It was built in a valley deep within a Cliffside, 
overlooking a river running through a lush forest. 
The main fortress where they had all been staying was behind a stone wall 
that must have been crafted by gods themselves, for it was the largest wall she’d 
ever seen. It had gigantic statues, now broken. With her Oracular sight, she could 
see the way the statues used to look. 
There were four large statues that surrounded the city. The first that caught 
Maia’s attention was one of an Emperor. He was handsome, a large crown of 
precious metals atop his head. In his eyes were Runes that read ‘Human’ and 
‘Emperor,’ and in his hand was a spear adorned with large jewels sparkling in the 
sun light. 
About a mile within the city was a second statue. Carved directly into the 
cliffside was a gigantic soldier with a large sword pointed to the ground. His bulky 
and ornate armor was marked with a Rune that read ‘Jreamer.’ The statue of a 
stunning woman directly across from the Jreamer was built into the mountain 
itself, her long dress flowing, her palms out with an orb suspended directly above 
them. On her tiara was a Rune that read ‘Wicasht,’ and on the orb were Runes of 
Magick that read ‘Sunlight,’ ‘Moonlight,’ ‘Glow,’ and ‘Power Source.’
The most amazing of the four statues that guarded this city was the one of 
Jayde built directly into the wall. Her sword in her right hand and shield in her left 
symbolized the protector of this great city, her carved figure flying out of the stone 
itself. Her robes and armor terrified onlookers of the Forbidden City. She was 
ready to attack even a giant if it dared come near her home. A second statue of 
Jayde on the inside of the wall showed her praying for the safety of her people, an 
image that was as intimidating to enemies as it was calming to friends. 
Maia’s blond hair blew in the cool wind, the lonesome valley seeming like 
the only place in the world. The fresh air was like nothing she’d ever inhaled, but 
even in the tranquility of this majestic city, she couldn’t shake the events that she’d 
seen with her own eyes. This place was both a wonder and a nightmare. Maia knew 

 
195
she was standing on the remains of fallen Supernaturals whose spirits might never 
be at rest. She shivered at the thought and continued her trek, mapping the city as 
she tried to subdue her inherent fear of the unknown.

now more interested in these two ladies than she’d ever been before. Now I’m

Pagoda! I wonder what Juno is stopping me from finding out about Nina. Why are

Juno was taller than Nina by a couple inches, partly because she was

more she thought about the technological innovation she was wearing, the more

As she walked into the dusty temple, this one much smaller and intimate

“You guys are impossible!” Crux cried impatiently, annoyed beyond his

“You figure it out. I’m done for the day.”

Star struggled to stand. Her feet felt like they were on fire. “You’d think

Ahlina had thought she was a better fighter than both of her best friends. 
However, after the training with Crux, she knew she was just as out of shape and in

riding her bike on Market Street once. The bruise lasted for days. This was worse.

Kaery didn’t have to ask for help as Ahlina and Star both pulled her up to 
her feet slowly and groggily. “We’re pretty sad huh?”

the appearance of her short dress to that of a small top and micro mini-skirt, the 
kind she had seen Sky in once. “We looked depressing. No, worse, like freshmen 
fashion.” 
Ahlina giggled, knowing how badly most freshmen looked. They had those 
fish-out-of-water expressions on their faces and looked as if they were still dressed

200
years ago, and it downright made her laugh. Star had a way with words that were

Star stood quietly looking at Kaery, shaking her head at the bad joke. 
Obviously her friend didn’t get the sarcasm, but it made Kaery look even more

This revelation that Kaery didn’t get the reference wasn’t so easily passed up

Ahlina shook her head without looking in the direction of the approaching

Four males exited the inappropriately parked car. The passenger was

201

guard with no help in sight, Kaery tried to keep the peace. “Please just let us go.

By the time the warmth from the young man’s touch reached Ahlina’s skin,

Kaery did the splits, crushing the right foot of each of the two nearest men

The other flew through the window of a home, breaking through the glass as if he

Jake looked into the eyes of the green-eyed girl he had just assaulted, which

Star, Kaery and Ahlina were all low to the ground in battle stances, stone

Chapter 24
KIRARI’S WRATH
Juno took a defensive stance and looked at her two students as if they were 
from another time. She could see Nina as she had once looked before the Ice Age, 
dressed in custom-made light armor intertwined with a robe of the monks. Her hair 
back then was a long golden braid that touched her knee if it wasn’t intricately 
twisted upward. Juno stopped herself from being drawn into a memory as she 
looked at the stark contrast to Nina’s present appearance, an outdoorsy tomboy 
with short hair she cut herself with a wood carving knife. This young woman was 
wandering in the woods for years without as much as a blip on OZONE’s radar. 
This was a feat and Juno was dying to find out how she did it. 
Juno could appreciate Nina’s fit frame underneath the dirty clothes as she 
stood still, confused. She must have been hiking for miles every day, climbing 
mountains and hunting for food on her own, so she can’t be completely helpless.
Juno looked over to Maia and saw her in many other forms as well. In other 
lifetimes, this Goddess would have been surrounded by Witchbreed or other 
powerful council members. She usually wore elaborate headdresses with nothing 
but a loincloth and an embroidered cape, a far cry from the pink and black-layered 
emo girl she was in the present. These two were like all other Supernaturals who 
weren’t Witchbreed, clueless until they were enlightened once again. 
Knowing her work was cut out for her, she finally spoke, “I know that we’ve 
been at this for days, but you both are barely catching onto the basics of my 
attacks.”
Nina was completely out of breath or she would have stopped Juno right 
there. Days? There’s no way we’ve been at this for days, it only felt like hours at 
most. Nina’s muscles however, were very sore and she knew why, because she had 
been hitting the stone floor more than Maia. She didn’t understand what all this 
fighting was for in the first place. “Why are we fighting?”
“Remember those Soldiers in the forest that almost shot your head off?” 
Juno snapped unapologetically. “They are the reason.”

 
207
Nina lowered her head in embarrassment. She almost forgot that Juno had 
saved her life in the woods from soldiers of some secret society she knew next to 
nothing about. Her shyness caused her to sink within herself slightly and remember 
the fear she felt that day when she had to jump into a waterfall, teleport to another 
city, find Gemini and save Juno. She ended up finding a group of kids who knew 
Gemini, causing a riot to attack the soldiers who were trying to kill them. Then she 
remembered Sky lifting everyone off the ground as if gravity didn’t exist, then 
flying through the forest to save Juno. It all sounded like a trip someone would get 
from some good mushrooms or something, but now it was all real. 
“Nina, pay attention,” Maia whispered softly. Her long curly blond hair with 
black strips flowed around her in the soft breeze like a warm halo. She was 
intrigued by how similar Juno’s training was to Jayde’s. Juno had been scolding 
Nina, but the tomboy seemed to have spaced out for an uncomfortable moment 
while Juno stared at her patiently. Almost too patiently. 
Juno nodded toward Maia for the interruption in Nina’s withdrawal, then 
turned away from both students and lifted her hands to the sky. “I think I have a 
better lesson for the two of you to learn.” She kneeled and closed her eyes with one 
hand still aimed upward toward a dusty wall of the room. In the language of 
Magick, she uttered softly, “By the Palm of the Prophets, show my past.”
Nina and Maia gave each other quick glances and watched as the wall in 
front of Juno changed, losing its dust to reveal the ancient hieroglyphs underneath 
in pristine condition, and the longer they looked at it, the faster the wall seemed to 
reverse millenniums, the rest of the room around them also reflecting the shift in 
time. 
Nina was beginning to panic when she felt the reassuring calm from a hand 
touching her own. She looked down to see Maia’s hand holding hers. 
“I’ve seen something similar with Surge,” Maia said, “It’s a glimpse of the 
past. Don’t be scared. It’s all just to show us what happened to this place. It’s quite 
fascinating. Wow, Sky would totally grill me for saying that if she were here!”
“Silence,” Juno’s voice rang out, and though Nina could no longer see her, 
she felt her presence. Maia could see Juno walking out of the temple, beckoning 
for them to follow, but she was more intrigued by the use of Glamour Magick that 
created this astonishing illusion in the first place.

*
(Ancient Zalomoni: Last Night of the Spiraar Attack)
Jayde was a sight to see. Her long black mane spun around her like the 
shadow on the dark side of the moon as she sliced another twenty-foot Spiraar in 
half. The shapeshifters were relentless, and she matched their onslaught with a 
myriad of attacks and parries. Her grace would have captivated her warriors’

slashing any creature unfortunate enough to get within her reach. 
“Juno, take your flank and secure Emperor Loiza’s escape!” Jayde didn’t 
bother to make sure Juno heard as she continued to seamlessly attack the wave, 
killing enemies by the dozens. She noticed a spin that Juno used to fight her way 
toward the Emperor’s tower, four of her most trained Jreamers and Monks 
following closely behind. 
“Guardian, Requiem for a Tower!” Jayde called once Juno was out of 
earshot, her blade cutting the arms off three beasts with one stroke as she spoke. 
Guardian had watched many of his finest fall to these primal creatures, and 
with every kill, could feel himself getting stronger while his men seemed to be 
suffering from exhaustion. Only three people in the Forbidden City were capable 
of executing the task Jayde spoke of, and he was one of them, the other two being 
Jayde and the Emperor himself. The fateful words entailed the awakening of 
Kirari. 
As if the roars and howls of the beasts were his queue, Guardian broke form 
and left his most prized teacher behind, knowing he had no choice to prevent all of 
his men from dying. These knights, monks, warriors and sharpshooters were the 
most skilled martial artists in the world as they had been trained by Jayde herself. 
If they perished, the arts would be lost to the world. 

 
209
Guardian waited until he had the attention of his goddess and most 
admirable teacher, Jayde. He stood still on the battlefield not even fifty yards from 
her, and when they made eye contact, he smiled and kneeled respectfully. 
Jayde returned the gesture and quickly regained her battle prowess as she 
knew her protégé was destined to die on this battlefield. A single tear fell from

Juno looked to the sky just outside the battlefield as she ran toward the

Juno knew that Jayde had given her specific duties, and by custom and law,

to continue to the Temple, most of which looked confused as they were to never

The three men gave each other a final glance before charging toward the

learned, moving with a hurricane of attacks toward the place where the ruler of

“Nina I am coming for you, just stay alive!” Juno cried as the roars of the 
Spiraar and screams of dying citizens flooded the air. Juno blocked out the gutwrenching noises as she carved her way to the prayer room. She knew that from 

 
210
birth, Nina had been revered as a Mancer and Priestess of Zalomoni. Only recently

the outer realms of the city with Juno to prove she was more than just a pretty face

Juno had taught her survival skills, and in turn, Nina had taught Juno

Rushing toward the prayer room, she saw a group of six women fighting with

As Nina stood slowly, she smiled. There were six Spiraar left, as two of her

211

Ahlina was trying not to scream as her hands trembled to the touch of the 
ancient Dragon. She knew that she couldn’t turn back now, as her sister would 
most definitely kill her if the Dragon didn’t first. The large scales were like huge 
shields only Mancers or Jreamers could dare to hold. She felt the rumble of the 
Dragon’s heartbeat like an earthquake pulsing beneath the scales. Ahlina looked up 
at the throbbing statue. She whispered as she placed her head on a scale near her 
hand, “Please help my sister. Zalomoni is in trouble. I know I’ve been selfish, and I 
wanted to leave, but I never will if you can save my people, I promise!”
A loud roar of a yawn filled the Temple, shaking the very foundation of the 
structure. Ahlina stepped back to avoid being squished by the Dragon’s sudden 
movement. She could see the Runes of Magick clearly for the first time in her life. 
They spun in a golden swarm around the scales that were rapidly shifting from 
stone to crystalline. The candles in the room and sunbeams peeking through the 
ceiling were reflecting off the scales, nearly consuming Ahlina.

212

Juno ran steadily to the Emperor’s Temple until Nina stopped in her tracks, 
looking up in the sky. “There’s no time to sight see, Nina.”
Nina slowly pointed upward. “Beautiful.”
Juno looked up in the direction she was pointing to see Ahlina being flung

219
worthy, but take this pure soul.” She then threw Nina into the air, leaving no time

Juno and her Priestesses smiled and waved to her, Kirari smashed into them,

Wavering high above the temples, Nina saw Jayde holding Ahlina’s body in 
her arms. Not once had she ever seen Jayde cry, but the woman sobbed 
nonetheless, as a mother would over a child gone too soon. Nina felt her pain from 
this height and couldn’t help but cry about the devastation brought by a single day. 
She wept for all the dead citizens, for all the fallen humans and Supernaturals 
alike. She sobbed for Juno and couldn’t do anything to ease the pain filling her 
heart. The Sylphs began to weep and lowered Nina to the top of the canyon where

*
Kirari took the form of a man. The muscles of his naked body were covered 
in scars, but he made his way to the sobs that would forever torment him. He saw 
Jayde crying to the point of losing control of her body and shaking with tremors. 
Ahlina looked as if all of her bones were broken, and Jayde was trying to set each 
one as if she could piece her dead sibling back together. The almond eyes were 
permanently closed, and her pale skin was bruised all over. 
Jayde didn’t stop Kirari’s hand from touching her shoulder. “I should have

220
“You didn’t fail her. She was one of the bravest souls I’ve ever known. You 
taught her well. She was prepared to die just like you were.” Kirari’s smooth voice 
soothed Jayde only momentarily. 
Her sobs took hold of Kirari’s heart. He looked at her for a long moment. 
The moon was full overhead, and the assault on Zalomoni had all but diminished. 
Kirari looked around, and with no audience, he whispered into Jayde’s ear, “If I 
give you some of my essence, I will not be able to keep this form. You must take

Jayde looked up at Kirari for the first time. “You would give up your

“I already have.” He gently placed a crystalline jewel in Jayde’s hand and

Before Jayde could object, Kirari spun into a tornado of crystal dust and

the moon. She closed her eyes, and when she awoke from what must have been a 
slumber, her sister Ahlina was all but dust in her lap. 
Jayde cried again but looked at her hand and saw the crystal jewel. She tried 
to smile but could not form the simple expression. She stood up and walked out of

*
(Present Day)
“That was so sad,” Maia said as she regained her focus from the Glamour

Neither woman said anything to Maia as they stared into each other’s eyes. 
Maia tip-toed out of the room and turned the corner to see where the attack on

221
With a sigh and shaking her head to stay focused on the present, she

222
Chapter 25
New Assignment
It had been eighteen days since the incident in Union Square, and Guardian’s 
memory of everything that had happened that day was still fuzzy. Even Psiel 
Agents classified him with the same delusional symptoms that over sixty people 
had suffered from since the night of the ambush on Surge-Overload. Apparently a 
Supernatural with a high level of psychological influence had led a small mob of 
people, including several OZONE agents and soldiers, causing a riot that ended in 
explosions and looting. 
Guardian was briefed on his current condition, which felt like a hangover 
from hell. He didn’t know why they wouldn’t just let him sleep at his crappy 
apartment, instead insisting that he take residence in the Underbase until further 
notice. He had been drawn into a psychological wormhole of memories that caused 
him to jump off a fourth story balcony into an alleyway dumpster.
I must be delusional. I could have sworn I saw my niece, Cindy. No, that’s 
exactly who I saw. She was with that woman, Nina from the Juno files. I wonder if 
the Psiel were able to see that memory?
“Agent Guardian, I’m glad you’re awake. I’m Agent Zeer.”
“I know who you are,” Guardian said groggily as he sat up, ready to leave 
the room until he realized he was in a hospital gown.
“Please do take it easy. You almost died from the impact of the jump. Thank 
goodness you were in the dumpster when the C-4 went off because otherwise, you 
might be in the same shape as Narles.” Agent Zeer was so polite, it almost seemed 
as if he were using his psychic abilities to gain his trust.
Guardian knew better. He was trained in many arts of persuasion; espionage 
and subterfuge were definitely his strong suits. He could hide his emotions from 
someone as easily as drinking a beer. He knew that with agents such as himself on 
the map, others could rarely be trusted, especially the Psiel. Zeer was one of the 
most elite interrogation officers in the world, using a combination of mentalist and 
telepathic techniques in torture. However, he had never known him to retrieve any 
information from a sleeping victim. 
“I suppose you are wondering if I read your mind when you were asleep,” 
Zeer said, his coy smile turning to a cautious smirk as Guardian slowly looked him 

 
223
up and down as if he might tear the bones from the officer’s thin frame. Retrieving 
his confidence once more, Zeer straightened his already immaculate black hair and 
walked over to a supply cabinet. As he pulled a drawer open slowly, he spoke with 
Guardian behind him still sitting on the bed. 
“I apologize for the delay and the tests, but you know protocol, Guardian. 
We needed to find out what happened that night.” He turned around with 
Guardian’s combat-ready clothes, leaving them on the edge of the bed so as not to 
get too close to the assassin. 
“So what happened?” Guardian asked as he stood up and took the gown off, 
not shy or embarrassed that another man was in the room as he dressed. 
Zeer did not turn away, instead focused on every muscle on Guardian’s 
body. This was an OZONE Agent, a perfect specimen. He looked like he was 
ready to run a decathlon before posing for a men’s fitness magazine. He was 
definitely American, and as the model agent continued to dress, Zeer struggled to 
remember the question. “It’s classified. I know that doesn’t override your status. 
Basically, Narles failed to capture Surge-Overload and instead shot the wrong man, 
one of our own, a Technopath. Shot him fourteen times to be exact, killing the him 
and breaking ‘the binding circle,’ as they called it, where Surge-Overload was 
being locked in until one of you snipers made the shot.” Zeer took note of the 
strange markings and tattoos that must have covered at least twenty percent of 
Guardian’s body.
As Zeer talked, Guardian listened carefully while buckling every buckle and 
trying his best to seem preoccupied with his gears. Guardian knew what happened 
next, but he pretended to be on the edge of his seat in anticipation with the report. 
Not doing too much to alert the Agent, but simply to milk him for information in a 
reverse interrogation. 
This was the longest conversation Zeer had ever had with Agent Guardian, 
the most polished and elite of OZONE’s assassins. “Surge-Overload was believed 
to be identified.”
Guardian looked up at him with a questioning expression. “Who is he?”
“He is a she. And, well, her face is unidentified as Surge killed every 
Technomage connected to the web in the binding circle.”
“How?”
“She somehow made their computers melt and explode, which killed them 
all instantly.” Zeer no longer smiled. His pale skin and thin Dutch frame was a 

 
224
definite contrast to the large man looking at him, but he didn’t flinch as he 
continued. “Narles made a mistake and started aiming his gun at everyone, 
including the trillionaire heiress, Sky, and other pedestrians in the busy shopping 
center.” Zeer’s voice was cool and collected as he straightened his black suit. He 
was already bored of this story. It had nothing to do with torture, so he sped it up 
and condensed the details. 
Guardian looked puzzled as Zeer explained that he was under some sort of 
mind control by a powerful empath. This same power had caused a mob of random 
San Francisco citizens with no prior records of violence to tear at the flesh of their 
own Agent Narles before detonating a bomb. 
Zeer tried to wrap up his story, “You were under some sort of mind control 
as you turned your rifle on other soldiers and some Vampyl that were perched on 
the rooftops in the shadows...before you went dumpster-diving. A dozen of our 
best agents died that day.”
Guardian now understood what he had meant about the C-4. He almost 
regretted the next question before he asked it, but he asked anyway, “What
happened to Surge-Overload?”
“She got away.”
Trying not to let out a single sigh or allow his heart to change pace, 
Guardian turned to look for his boots. 
A moment later, Zeer opened a nearby foot locker and came forward. He 
was strong for his size, but nevertheless he gave a slight grunt as he dropped the 
heavy black leather and metallic armory boots into Guardian’s hands. Zeer enjoyed 
torturing people, displaying them in strange contraptions and suspension devices 
when they were near death. In their moments of pain and exhaustion, they always 
let their surface-level thoughts spill forth, providing any information he might 
wish to know. “The most peculiar thing is that we could not get a proper facial 
identification of the girl from you, the remaining Soldiers, Agent Grin nor that 
failure, Narles.”
“Narles is alive?” Guardian asked as he slipped his boots on, “But I thought 
you said…”
“Yes, when the C-4 was detonated, he was under a pile of people attacking 
him, all of whom acted as a meat shield and stopped most of the damage.”
“What do you mean most of the damage?” Guardian’s green eyes met the 
dark grey eyes of the Psiel. 

 
225
“I mean, he lost all of his limbs and suffered from internal bleeding and 
organ failure, but he’s currently in one of Braintech’s conceptual labs awaiting 
another inevitable surgery. Strangely enough, he’s already undergone over a dozen 
surgeries, and he’s still alive. Isn’t that miraculous?” Zeer’s face was painted with 
a renewed smile as if he were observing Narles’ pain that very moment. 
Guardian noticed the expression change, and as he strapped up the second 
boot, he stood up straight, too fast, the headache returning with the nauseating 
pressure behind his temples. He placed a hand on the bed to steady himself. He 
knew never to trust a Psiel; they always gave him a bad taste in his mouth, 
speaking from some self-righteous place, but dripping with the slime of hypocrisy. 
He knew Zeer was watching him and quickly regained his composure as he started 
towards the door. The agent stood in Guardian’s immediate path, and he didn’t 
want to hurt him, but he was prepared to remove this creep if he didn’t step aside.
“You might want to stay close as the Commander in Chief—” Zeer politely 
moved out of the way just as Guardian was about to bump into him.
“—Is already here, and he has the next mission for you, Guardian. Your 
weapons are in the car. I’ll brief you on the way.” 
The Commander in Chief was a tall brooding man with an iron jaw that 
seemed permanently clinched from talking down to his men. He was burly with 
blond hair and a well-kempt beard. He towered over Guardian as he walked down 
the hall beside him with just as much, if not more, confidence. 
Guardian knew that he could not refuse a request from his superior, no 
matter the cost or difficulty. 
“I know you didn’t kill Surge-Overload, but just to let you know, Deme-god 
the Technomage did!” The Commander in Chief didn’t seem to notice that the 
news hit Guardian like a punch in the stomach. “It was actually quite brilliant, 
though I’d never tell him that. He tracked her down in Japan where our soldiers 
infiltrated and destroyed the entire compound.” The Commander displayed a sense 
of pride and certainty as he continued his briefing, all while Guardian was 
struggling to keep track of the conversation that now seemed like a disorienting 
blur of exchanges. 
The floor slightly spun in vertigo, and the lights seemed to make the long

“I want to kill them all!” Sky said, utterly annoyed at the men in black suits 
who followed her around at all times. 
“I understand you have been through a lot, Bunny, and you don’t want your

“I’m not mad at you, Daddums, but I’m pissed as hell at these guys who 
burst into my room randomly whenever I drop my phone or a pencil or whatever.”
“I understand and so does your mother.” Mr. Bradford walked over to his

“No buts, just trust me.”

227
notice. Her long legs strode gracefully out of the room, her micro-mini pleated

could. She stepped into her room, and the auto-hinges of the new poly-fiber 
synthetic smart door prevented it making a loud noise as she slammed it shut. 
“Ugh,” she said aloud, her last expression of rage diffused.
“Did I miss something, or did I miss out on your door slamming effort?” A

Taken off guard in her own room, she turned and said, “Eww! What are you 
doing in here? And stop looking at me like that!”
“No need to get upset, little lady,” Grin walked closer and reached his hand 
to touch her thigh.
“Don’t you dare touch me!” Sky demanded with her nostrils flaring. 
“Such a pretty face, you wouldn’t want to ruin it with all that anger. I can

“Don’t talk to me like that. You don’t know me,” Sky said. 
“I don’t. I don’t know that your birthday is in a few days. Your favorite

“Google.” Sky squinted.
“I don’t know that you like Egyptian cotton sheets. You have a holographic

“Gross, you’re a stalker!” Sky screeched. 
“I’m worse than a stalker, little girl. I know you’re a Mancer! It’s only a

His breath reeked of cigarettes, matching his wrinkled suit that wasn’t like the

228
He made her sick to her stomach, a feeling she’d never received from a

Sky woke up to see her Jewish father sitting on her bed with her African

As Sky looked up, she saw her parent’s happy faces, paralyzed and dull. In a 
panic she yelped “Mum! Daddums?”

Guardian began to breathe steadily in and out as if he were meditating, 
transporting himself to a peaceful spring day. He knew that, like every other 
mission, this one could be his last. When he closed his eyes, all he could see was 
the boy in the forest pleading for him not to shoot. His eyelids flipped open as soon 
as he saw the bullet enter the back of Isja’s skull, a memory he just couldn’t shake. 
Guardian was disgusted with what he’d done a few weeks earlier and had 

 
230
questioned every mission since. This one was no different; he knew it was pure

right. 
His firearms were, of course, custom-made according to his own, like mere 
extensions of his own body. With his heart racing, he placed both weapons to his 
lips and kissed them, then kicked the door down with such force, whoever was on 
the other side would’ve preferred to die on impact rather than face the wrath he 
was about to unleash.
Instantly, Guardian’s mind was clear of anything but his mission. He knew 
this was a den of the Dehmn, which meant he would see unspeakable things, but he 
pushed forward anyway, using the skill that would help him complete this task, his 
deadly accuracy. There was a team of eight men and women awaiting Guardian’s 
call to sweep the building, so he only had a total of two hundred and forty seconds 
before they would automatically storm in after him. 
He had known for a while now that he was different, stronger and faster than 
the average Agent, even without his Braintech armor. His skills were only 
enhanced with every mission completed. With every kill, a small glyph burned its 
way onto or under his skin, as if he absorbed a part of each Supernatural target into 
himself, snowballing into something monstrous. This was no time to wonder about 
such things as he gracefully jogged around the dilapidated biohazard of a structure, 
making sure to track his path through the facility. He passed countless prisoners 
ravaged by drugs, chemicals, disease and other forms of Dehmn influence. When 
he identified them as a threat, he fired two shots into the heads of his first targets. 
There were victims among the hostiles, but most were so far gone that it was 
no use saving them, so Guardian continued his trek. His hyper-speed guided him to 
the third floor of the building, shooting two dozen gun-carrying thugs along the

tainted men, their eyes blood-shot from years of biochemical abuse, their skin 
bubbling with sores. This place was definitely going down one way or another. 
He finally made it to the top floor, using laser-like precision to mow down 
all enemies in his path. He wasn’t known as OZONE’s deadliest agent for no

231
counting; his compact clips could hold ninety four miniature rounds of lethal

Knocking the dead pair of foes through the door, Guardian saw a man

This was the main objective. Guardian had been briefed. This individual’s

“Those petty things won’t kill me,” the grey man said, “But I’m sure you

“Touch that briefcase, and I will shoot,” Guardian said coldly. Two hundred

Reaching down to grab the briefcase, Scag smiled and placed his hands on

233

As if looking at a god or a hero out of some comic book, the soldiers stood

Throwing the item at one of the soldiers, he flashed an approving look and

The entire trip from Syria back to the U.S.A. was quiet. The soldiers had

When Guardian walked out of the airship, he was not expecting to be

Without giving Guardian a chance to say a word, the Commander spoke,

He could barely believe his ears. Had he just survived a near-suicide mission

“You are to take it easy and become a bodyguard.”
Great. They’re gonna have me babysit a fat adulterous politician or some

234

“Fix him, and put him back on assignment,” the Commander said before
