Chapter 26
AENJELS AND ORPHANS
“I’m so sorry for your loss, Lisa. If there’s anything I can do for you, please 
let me know,” Orchid Bradford spoke sincerely to her dear friend in the parlor of 
the multi-million-dollar mansion. 
“I didn’t know who else to call. I mean, I sent her to the same camp where 
we met.” Lisa was an OZONE Agent who usually didn’t allow herself to become 
so defeated. 
“When did you hear that Cindy passed away?” Orchid took a seat on a couch 
that appraised for more than most people’s yearly wage. She crossed her smooth 
brown legs comfortably, accustomed to a life of glamour and luxury. She was the 
heiress to the largest diamond mines in Africa and wife to arguably the most 
powerful man in the world, Samuel Bradford. She hadn’t talked to her friend Lisa 
in some time, but when she saw who was calling, she had to answer. They both had 
daughters, and she would be devastated if anything ever happened to her own. 
“Shortly after I heard your daughter was returned home.” Lisa’s sob was 
infectious. “I’m happy for Sky’s return.”
Orchid’s eyes began to tear up as she thought about her own daughter’s 
recent abduction. She didn’t know what to say to comfort Lisa. The pain of her loss 
was too similar. She’d been crying for days on end in Sky’s absence, but could 
never tell anyone of her sorrow. They both were a part of an organization that was 
as old as religion itself, the DOLLS, which stood for ‘Daughters of Lilith’s Last 
Scorn,’ a secret society of beautiful women who controlled and influenced the 
world. These elite femme fatales were trained to be the next wave of wives and 
mistresses to the most powerful men and women. 
“I haven’t called you in so long, and again, I apologize. It’s just that I saw so 
much potential in my daughter. Rainbow told me she was sick and sent her home. I 
just never thought…” Lisa cleared her throat as she gained her composure, 
realizing the uncomfortable silence coming from the other end. She knew the oath 
she’d taken to avoid emotional attachment to her family, as the DOLLS were her 
first priority, but she couldn’t help feeling like the mourning mother she was. 

 
236
After taking a deep breath, Orchid spoke in her regal tone. “You know as well as I 
do that our family is all but inconsequential. You need to have another child and 
keep Reynolds in line.”
“I know,” Lisa said sadly. She thought about how her husband was going to 
take the news. 
“You haven’t told him have you?” Orchid asked with a moment of 
realization, noticing a shift in Lisa’s tone. 
Lisa began to sob uncontrollably.
“Get your nerves in check, girl,” Orchid commanded. “When you married 
Reynolds, you knew you’d have to be strong. He can never know your true 
allegiance!”
“Yes, thank you for helping me remember that.” Lisa wiped away her tears. 
“Just remember the mission at hand. I’ll see you at the masquerade ball in 
Paris.”
“I’ll see you there.” Lisa knew she had to listen to her instincts. One of the 
most powerful DOLLS was telling her to refocus. She regained some semblance of 
composure and uttered the customary goodbye: “By the mirror.”
“Your pain is mine,” Orchid answered before hanging up the phone. She 
looked out the window, and another tear fell from her eye. “I’m so thankful to have 
Sky back.” 
Lisa hung up, knowing that her upcoming mission involved the transport of 
a young girl to the orphanage. She straightened her pencil skirt and blouse. 
Reaching in her purse, she felt her small OZONE-issued handgun and grabbed the 
tube that was next to it, applying a new coat of red lipstick while attempting to 
calm herself. I’m part of two secret societies and trying to deal with the loss of my 
only daughter. No one is supposed to feel this torn. Have another kid, she says. As 
if I wouldn’t if I could. After Cindy, it’s impossible. Reynolds will be so hurt when 
he reads the report about the explosion. 
After a long sigh, Lisa stood with her head high. Her classic beauty was one 
thing she used to her advantage, but she was cunning and intelligent as well. She’d 
been sent to eliminate high-priority targets based on OZONE’s orders and also 
investigate paranormal activities, but to the outside world, she was just a house 
wife with a rocket scientist husband. This current mission had required her to 
assume the identity of a nurse who was empathetic to the loss of a child’s life. The 
mission was too close to home in her eyes, and she looked at herself in the mirror 

 
237
to force her tears back to the abyss they had come from. She had watched a girl the 
same age as Cindy give birth to a beautiful baby boy who was promptly swapped 
with another child and taken by OZONE agents who assigned him to a family that 
Lisa thought was unfit, but as usual, she didn’t ask questions. 
She remembered walking down the hall of the delivery ward toward a 
particular room, painting a smile so natural on her face that no one could tell she 
was in pain underneath. She remembered the thin young girl rocking back and 
forth on the bed with her shoes and clothes on. 
“Are you ready to go, Aiel?” 
The girl had looked up at the nice smile of the nurse who was volunteering 
to take her to the new Orphanage. “Yes, but why can’t I see my baby?”
“We’ve been over this, Aiel. I’m truly sorry for your loss. I can only 
imagine...” In that moment Lisa could not control her emotions, and she teared up 
heavily. 
The pain was so evident that Aiel had jumped from the bed to comfort her 
own nurse. “You’ve lost a child, haven’t you?”
Lisa had opened her eyes in shock as the girl hugged her. “Yes. Yes, I have.”
Aiel clasped her hand for a long moment.
“I don’t know how to explain it,” Aiel said, “but somehow I know my baby 
is still alive.”
Lisa was silent, unable to look the mother in the eye.
“I’m ready to go now,” she had said, reluctantly releasing her grip. 
Lisa remembered the feeling. It had felt good to hug someone. 
*
Ssiah hugged her knees in the backseat of the car on the way to the church as 
an orphan once again. Her biological mother and father had died when she was just 
a baby. She’d grown up in the system knowing that children were much like 
animals in a pet shop: some were picked, and others were lost. The woman who
was supposed to adopt her had miraculously conceived a child, after Ssiah had 
thought she’d finally have a home after filling out all that paperwork. The woman 

 
238
knew there would be the possibility of complications and had wanted to adopt, but 
at the last minute, she had called the social worker to tell her she could not go 
through with it. They had a healthy new baby boy named Isja. So just as before, 
Ssiah’s hope for adopted parents was unrealized, and she was sent to yet another 
orphanage.
Ssiah was a charming little girl with hazel green eyes and lovely golden 
locks that made her look almost angelic. She’d been told that she was an angel by 
many, but none knew her secret. Ssiah had been able to speak to the Aenjelhn, 
which humans call angels, since the day she was born. They’d played with her in 
the crib and protected her from danger. 
Always feeling safe, but never feeling like she was part of any home, Ssiah 
repeatedly asked why all this was happening to her. The Aenjelhn never answered 
with what she wanted to hear, but told her it was all part of a divine plan. This time 
after feeling hopeless, she refused to feel so defeated and smiled at the possibility 
that maybe one day she might meet her twin brother who had been separated from 
her following the death of their parents. “Is there a reason I am being sent to this 
church instead of the old home?” She seemed to sing the words to the Aenjelhn 
who sat next to her in the backseat. 
The Aenjelhn was a large man who looked too perfect to be human. He 
appeared cheerful, though he was actually far from it. His ability to make Ssiah 
smile was waning today, so deep was her disappointment. He could not disclose 
what he knew without compromising his mission, so he simply replied, “You will 
meet new friends here.”
Ssiah looked out the window. “You said that I would. Are they nice?”
The Aenjelhn nodded. His short black hair was something Ssiah always felt 
compelled to touch. He knew his appearance was appealing to her senses, as it was 
similar to that of her brother, and he kept the illusion up. Ssiah would soon learn 
her destiny and, with his help, live long enough to fulfill it. Her protection was his 
only mission, and he was not going to fail her. 
The car stopped, and Ssiah looked at the church, suddenly filled with a sense 
of dread. “What is this place, Cassiel?”
“I will protect you,” he said.
“You always do, but you haven’t answered me! What is this place?” Ssiah 
pulled on the Aenjelhn’s custom-tailored purple suit. She held his warm hand as 
she took in her unfamiliar surroundings.

 
239
“It’s a tomb,” Cassiel replied flatly. 
The car door opened, and the driver who’d been listening to this girl talk to 
herself shook his head in pity. He smiled at the pretty face of the nine-year-old and 
couldn’t help thinking she looked like an angel. “I’m sure the nuns will take good 
care of you here,” he said, moving toward Ssiah. Flinching as if he were going to 
hurt her, Ssiah hid behind Cassiel. 
“It’s ok, little lady, I’m not going to hurt you.” The man seemed kind 
enough. 
“He won’t harm you,” Cassiel said warmly as he looked to the dark stone of 
the Edwardian-looking architecture before them. His bronze ring began to glow. 
“Your ring,” Ssiah said.
“Oh yes,” the driver said, proudly displaying his wedding ring, “my wife 
gave this to me, God rest her soul.”
Ssiah didn’t say another word, feeling awkward. She didn’t like speaking to 
Aenjels when humans were present if she could help it. Cassiel had always told her 
that she was a Wicasht, that other people couldn’t see the things she could. She had 
tried her best to explain her experiences to others on many occasions, but no one 
ever understood her. She smiled at the driver and returned her gaze to Cassiel’s 
ring. When his ring glows, a Supernatural is present. Maybe he’s right. Maybe I 
will finally make friends here.
At that very moment, a luxury SUV pulled into the cobblestone driveway of 
the church and parked behind the taxi. The dust began to settle, and the doors 
opened as Cassiel’s ring lit up like a beacon. Ssiah and the Aenjelhn watched the 
SUV with suspicion.
A door opened, and Aiel stepped out into the chilly Bay Area breeze. Her 
quiet trip with Lisa had been quite nice. They had listened to music that she liked, 
which had made her smile for the first time in what seemed like forever, since her 
baby had passed away. When Aiel noticed the other young girl and her driver, a 
ray of sun broke through the clouds, and for a moment she could see the shape of a 
third person who was present, a tall well-dressed man. In an instant, the sun was 
blocked by the shadow of a cloud, and the man disappeared altogether, causing 
Aiel to shudder. It would be better if she didn’t tell anyone about whatever she just 
saw. The thirteen-year-old was not amused that she had been sent to yet another 
church to be ridiculed and teased, but she had no choice until she was old enough 

 
240
to leave the system. “In five years, I’ll be able to get my own place and live on my 
own. Right, Lisa?”
“If not sooner, so don’t lose faith. But I’m sure someone will adopt you,” 
Lisa said smiling. 
“Yeah, right. No one wants a kid like me. I’m not stupid enough to forget
that.”
Lisa had no answer for her as she walked to the front steps of the church 
with her small bags. “Well, here we are.”
Aiel tried not to cry. She would love to go home with Lisa, but she didn’t 
know how to ask, so she didn’t mention it. “Will you come visit me?”
“Of course I will. I don’t live too far from here,” Lisa said, cheerful at the 
thought of checking on Aiel. 
She gave Lisa another hug, burying her face in the soft white blouse and 
smiling. Then she turned as the unfamiliar voice of a man made her skin crawl.
“I am Father Bell.” This older gentleman had a wrinkly face and a strangely 
sinister tone of voice.
Lisa knew a villain when she saw one. She quickly assessed that this was an 
OZONE operative of a level she must not have been aware of. No wonder they 
wanted the girl here. “Hello, I’m Lisa, this is Aiel—” The man was impatient.
“—I know who you are. Girl, you run along and allow the nuns to show you 
the quarters.” The priest had a certain rudeness about him, his behavior changing 
drastically with every second. He was visibly repulsed by the little golden-haired 
child as she stood next to the black car, his gaze filled with inexplicable hatred.
The driver noticed his change in tone as he unfolded the documents in his 
hands. “This is Ssiah…”
“What is she doing here?” The priest demanded uncomfortably. 
“Well Sir, this is where I was told to drop her off. She’s an orphan.” 
“No matter… nuns! Show them to their quarters.” 
The nuns rushed out of the church, looking worn out, as if the life had been 
sucked out of their once youthful bones. “Come with us,” they said. 
The priest didn’t bother with formalities or goodbyes as he hurried away. 
Aiel looked back at Lisa in unprecedented horror as a nun took her by the arm. 
Lisa saw the dread, but could do nothing to calm her other than call loud 
enough for the nuns to hear, “I’ll be back to check on you.” I’ll make it my duty to 

 
241
check on Aiel and Isja. I don’t know what mess they have been thrown into, but it’s 
definitely out of their control. 
With that bit of hope, Aiel smiled, but the smile was short lived as she was 
ushered into the church. “Up the stairs,” the nun said.
When they reached the top, the nun barked at one of her inferiors. “Tell 
those god awful kids, Veronica, Melody and Ewm to come see to these new 
children.” 
The woman ran down the long stone hall. When another nun went to grab 
Ssiah, her hand was engulfed by a quick flash of light. She looked repulsed beyond 
words as she stepped back, holding her hand in pain and shrieking, “This… this 
way! Now!”
Through the stained glass windows of the building, Lisa saw the strange 
light from outside, but did not see what had caused it and figured it must have been 
the sun. She knew this was one of OZONE’s facilities. Like most old churches that 
weren’t in complete disrepair, this one was some sort of haven for Supernatural 
activities, one that, sadly, she had no clearance to investigate. Whatever this branch 
was, it was much older than her sect, and like most other agents and operatives, 
individuals in OZONE usually weren’t aware of their comrades, or what capacity 
their position carried, unless that information was given. The churches had always 
made her uneasy because she could only imagine the terrible age-old things that 
their walls had witnessed. She knew that the walls of the Underbase held countless 
secrets both revolutionary and heinous.
Ssiah walked up the stairs without being touched by the withering nun 
escorting her. Cassiel was nearby, and she felt his warmth. She saw the girl, Aiel, 
being ushered down the hall like a criminal, then began to notice the countless 
paintings from centuries ago that hung on the walls. Feeling more anxious than 
before, she stopped at the end of the hall as the nun opened the door to the chapel.
It suddenly felt as if gravity itself had cemented her feet to the floor, attempting to 
prevent her from crossing the threshold. The nun was growing impatient.
“Let’s go!” she said.
Ssiah struggled to take a step, nearly losing her balance altogether. She had 
to focus on her feet with all her willpower for another moment before she was 
finally able to pull her feet from the floor and enter. When she walked into the 
chapel, a sudden and inexplicably certain thought filled her head as she said aloud, 
“Something evil is buried here!”

Chapter 27
SO UNLIKELY
Never was the kid who many people overlooked. He didn’t consider himself 
exceptionally handsome like the bad boys or athlete types, but he knew he was not 
ugly. Most of the time you’d see him walking through the halls with his head down 
and his earbuds in, playing some angsty rock music, but today he was in absolute 
awe staring at Sky. He knew better than to interrupt the flow of things, especially 
the culture of high school that people tend to overlook. There were the popular kids 
and the ones who strived for something; then there were kids like him who just 
hoped to get through the day without being bullied. 
His blond hair was almost white, and though the back of his head was under 
the hoodie as usual, his bangs stuck out in short spikes. Never was part of a 
program that allowed students from public schools to be attend this prep school, a 
privileged scholarship. He had been frequently bullied at his old school, but the 
guidance counselor had seen great things in him. She had even written an essay, 
supporting him as a candidate for the annual charity program of Pegasus Rose 
preparatory school. After touching the hearts of the counselor and many other 
faculty members, Never was accepted by Drakehawk Academy. 
Sitting at his desk in the chemistry room, he looked back on all of the events 
that had led to this moment. He had ended up in this class, a trillionaire heiress 
sitting just a few desks in front of him, and he had just seen her repair the shattered 
glass of a window after everyone had watched the teacher being sucked out of the 
classroom by a force that seemed to defy all laws of nature and physics. Never 
would never be the same. 
*

 
243
His name was Nevin Hollows, and his family was not only poor, but slightly 
more dysfunctional than he’d ever truly admit. His mother worked the nightshift at 
a local diner and was currently dating some random guy she had met at a bar. He 
hated his mother’s promiscuous behavior almost as much as he hated his own 
name. He had decided long ago that Never sounded better. 
When he met Scape for the first time as his personal welcoming committee, 
Never realized this school was a little out of his league. It wasn’t that Scape was 
rude or uppity, because he was anything but those things, though Never could tell 
right away that most of the other kids wouldn’t accept him. Scape was actually the 
nicest person to date who Never had actually spoken to. 
He longed to hang out with Scape and the kids who also looked out of place, 
particularly Kaery, Ahlina and Star. Although Scape was nice, there was rarely an 
opportunity to say anything to him because he was always surrounded by girls who 
glared at Never like they would at an alien. He kept his distance and watched them 
when no one was looking. 
As far as Never was concerned, this school was full of girls who were 
completely unapproachable. He thought it was highly unlikely that any of them 
would take time out of their day to talk to a poor kid like him. The jocks must have 
smelled the poverty on him because they made sure he was uncomfortable as often 
as possible. John Spankston seemed to have a vendetta against anyone who was 
even remotely considered a rebel. Rumor had it he’d been beat up by one a few 
years ago and had never learned his lesson. 
Never was not very tall, and though he might have had some wiry muscle 
underneath his baggy skater clothes, no one would ever notice. Everyone just saw 
the awkward guy with pale skin and white spiky hair. Most just ignored him after a 
simple glance, and he preferred that. 
Today Never’s greatest fear would be brought to reality; he was supposed to 
speak in front of the chemistry class. He dreaded his name being called, but the 
teacher was convinced he was one of his brightest students, even though he was 
definitely the shyest. 
“Nevin Hollows, please come to the front of the class and present your 
project,” Mr. Woodlands said cheerfully. “Everyone give him your undivided 
attention. That includes you, Sky.”

 
244
Sky had been looking out the window at the clouds. She wanted to go home 
or do what most normal kids did, skip school altogether. But outside the door was 
one of her dad’s henchmen. Not the creepy one, Grin, but another guy. She was so 
tired of being so overprotected, but had surrendered to the endless arguments about 
their presence. Her father had even told her that if she could get through the rest of 
the week with no incident, he’d let her take a day for herself. This was enough 
motivation to make her stop arguing. 
“What? Oh yeah, I was paying attention,” Sky said as she turned to the 
teacher and then noticed Never watching her. He instantly looked down at his 
shoes the moment they made eye contact. “I’m sorry; I didn’t mean to ignore you,” 
Sky said apologetically, “I mean, you can continue.” She turned her back to the 
judgmental gazes of the onlookers.
Never didn’t know why, but the blank stares of the students before him 
suddenly didn’t mean much as he spoke confidently about his topic. “Run as hard 
as a wild beast if you will, but you won’t get any greater reward than that destined 
for you.” Never looked up from his note card and smiled awkwardly at Sky. 
“That’s an ancient Egyptian proverb I came across, and it reminds me of what 
we’re all doing here as mankind. All living things contain carbon in some form, 
and it is one of the most important elements on the periodic table because it serves 
as a building block for many other compounds that help us survive. Without 
carbon, we wouldn’t have an atmosphere; we wouldn’t even be here in the first 
place...”
Khenebu had been sitting in the back of the class doodling star charts of 
distant worlds because school was far too easy. He could literally do every 
assignment in his sleep. He was polite and didn’t show any signs of fatigue, but he 
knew he was just going through the motions. His boredom sometimes took over as 
he wondered how the kids could have such frivolous conversations. Then 
sometimes he would think about it further; they were just humans who didn’t have 
much of importance to say.
For the first time, he didn’t look bored, but intrigued. It was at that moment 
he recognized the proverb from scriptures he’d studied in Egypt as a child. This 
young American boy’s knowledge it caught his attention, and in an instant, he 
knew why. I know him from another time. His name is Never, and by Horus’ 
wrath, that is Isis!

 
245
 *
“So you mean to tell me we were destined to meet each other?” Ahlina 
asked Crux, perplexed as she sat with him, Scape, Star and Kaery in Golden Gate 
Park.
“That’s exactly what I’m saying,” Crux said, eating his fourth sandwich.
“Wait, wait, let me get this straight!” Scape said after a long awkward pause, 
“You’re a Mancer?” He pointed to the nodding Crux. “And we’re Witchbreed?” 
Again Crux nodded cheerfully while swallowing his meal. “And all of these 
dreams we’re having are actually memories. And we’ve been best friends not only 
for these last few years, but in every lifetime before this?” 
“Well, I’m not sure about every lifetime, but you guys are actually pretty 
famous the more I think about it.” Crux giggled to himself. “I mean you were—
sorry—are the Coven of Unity!” After finishing his last bite of the buttery bread, 
he grabbed a large bag of chips and sat up enthusiastically, his excitement seeming 
to be a result of the snack more than the information he was relaying. “Look, I 
don’t have to remind you all about the flashback at the Pagoda. We’ve been over 
this, but I can go over it again if you want.” He ripped the bag open and started 
stuffing his mouth full of orange crunchy bits.
“No you don’t!” Star interrupted with the same disgusted look she always 
had watching Crux eat. “We know by now. We’re the gimps who listened to Sky 
and gave the humans powers. The humans almost destroyed the world, and we’re 
probably the reason everyone has such bad style now.”
“Yep,” Crux mumbled while chomping.
Kaery was tired of being quiet, and she was trying to understand it all. “Ok, 
so if this is the case, I’m not the only one who experiences every detail of those 
dreams or flashbacks or memories or whatever they are. I mean—every ray of 
sunshine, every breeze and the smell of every flower, am I right?”
Scape, Ahlina and Star looked at one another with the same expression, 
nodding in agreement.
“I suppose that answers it,” she said quietly. “Then Crux, I bet what you’re 
saying is all true because for some reason, it seems no one can lie to me anymore. 

 
246
But that doesn’t answer a few other questions.” Kaery’s golden eyes probed the 
munching preteen.
“Like, how he knew we were all going to learn Kung Fu, or whatever it was 
we did to kick those guys’ asses?” Ahlina asked abruptly.
“You’re gonna have to tell me that story again one of these days,” Scape 
said to Ahlina before smiling at Star and Kaery. “I’m not one for violence, but that 
just sounds so cool!”
“Or, how you knew we would all meet and form this Coven of Unity?” Star 
asked, sounding more accusing than anything else.
“These are easy answers,” Crux said smugly. “You always knew that 
fighting style because it was taught to you in another lifetime. And I’d also like to 
hear that story again ‘cause I’m kinda having a hard time seeing Star do anything 
more than fall on her big butt in those heels.”
Crux couldn’t dodge the slap to the back of the neck fast enough. Star had 
definitely gotten faster, and judging by the sting he felt, stronger too.
“Ouch!” He rubbed his red skin, dropping the chips, but quickly grabbing 
them before they hit the ground. “I only knew you’d create the Coven of Unity 
because Gemini told me you would. That’s why he had to bring you back home 
instead of training with us.” He was still rubbing his neck with one hand, but was 
able to grab and handful of chips with the other and shove them into his mouth.
“I don’t know how you’re not fat!” exclaimed Star, sitting down on the park 
bench. She crossed her long legs and inspected her nails, bored.
“Did Gemini tell you what we were supposed to do next?” Scape asked, 
sitting up and focusing on the boy.
“He didn’t say what your destiny was supposed to be, and he didn’t say 
much about what you guys can do. He barely spoke about you at all to be honest.” 
Crux gave a satisfied sigh as he continued munching.
“But he did tell you to spy on us, right?” Kaery asked matter-of-factly, 
prompting Crux to gasp and spit out his chips.
*

 
247
“That was a great speech, Nevin,” Mr. Woodlands said in shock to the 
skater, the boy who was usually too quiet to formulate words in his class. “Very 
well done, indeed! You even have me thinking about why we all try so hard when 
it seems like we’re only here to die.” The skinny man clapped, and in turn, the 
entire class gave a round of applause. “Very good grade you have there, young 
man. Whoever is next, I don’t envy you. That’s a hard act to follow!”
Never was walking to the back of the room when he saw Sky smile at him 
warmly. Her brown eyes caught his pitch black ones, and she didn’t look away. 
He bumped into a desk and looked down at the student who’d noticed their gazes 
meet. “I’m sorry, man, I didn’t mean to—”
“—It’s perfectly fine,” Khenebu said charmingly, “You know, up until now 
I didn’t take you for an intellectual.”
Never blushed and sat down in his desk next to him.
“I didn’t mean that in any offensive way,” Khenebu apologized with a warm 
smile.
“No, I’m not really like that,” Never said, almost whispering, “Today was a 
little different.” He looked across the class and smiled when he saw the beautiful 
brown locks of Sky’s hair.
Khenebu followed Never’s glance. “Oh, I see.”
“Well, you know, she’s basically a goddess,” Never sighed, looking down at 
his note cards.
“That’s more correct than you know,” Khenebu said without a smile. “She’s 
a Mancer.”
“What’s a Mancer?” Never asked in a whisper, not wanting to seem stupid. 
Khenebu looked around at the class as if they were all spies. “You can 
understand what I’m saying?” he asked in a dramatic whisper.
“Well, yeah,” Never said to the handsome African. He noticed how neat 
Khenebu’s dreads were, and when snickers from other students began to fill the air, 
he blushed again.
“Actually, that sounded like Arabic to me,” Mr. Woodlands said in his 
scolding voice as another student, Megan, stood awkwardly waiting to deliver her 
speech. “Do I need to separate you two?”
“No sir,” Khenebu said charismatically with a smile, sitting up to focus. “I 
was just intrigued with his Egyptian quotes is all.”

 
248
“Weren’t we all?” Mr. Woodlands smiled as he docked points from Megan’s 
presentation before it had even begun, knowing it wouldn’t be as enthralling as

As Megan started again, Khenebu passed a note to Never. Never looked up 
at Mr. Woodlands, who was already bored, not paying attention to anyone else in

and read the note to himself: ‘I know what you are, and I know what Sky is. If you

A simultaneous gasp came from both Sky and Never. They looked at each 
other from across the room, causing Megan to stop her speech again, silence filling 
the room. He took his hands off the note and looked into her soft brown eyes.
There’s no way—I’m in the back of the class, and she’s way over there. But she’s 
looking at me and Ken as if we were spies or something. Oh no, Mr. Woodlands...
Mr. Woodlands stood up. As he walked to the window, he began to chastise

Megan started screaming as Mr. Woodlands fell backward into a window,

“That’s what a Mancer is,” Khenebu said.

 
249
Never watched in silence. The whole event looked like something out of a

At that moment, one of her bodyguards rushed into the room. He did a

He was getting ready to pull his handgun when he saw Sky and the two boys

Watching the smoldering burn of its eyes reminded him of looking into the sun,

“How do you know that’s an angel or whatever you said?” Sky asked 
Khenebu.

Guardian looked at the three of them, noticing what Sky was holding.

Khenebu looked back down to see the Etu Aenjelhn gone from sight. “It

 ***
“We’ve been walking in Golden Gate Park for hours looking for some stupid 
imaginary Grove, and my feet hurt,” Star said, annoyed. “Answer her question. Did

“Ok, yes, Gemini asked me to keep an eye on you,” Crux admitted, rubbing

“I believe him,” Kaery said firmly.
“Well, are we all supposed to just believe his story?” Star asked. Her short

“Yeah, Kaery, think about it. What were we supposed to think with all of

revealed an invisible barrier that led to a part of the woods she had never seen 

 
251
before, and she’d lived in San Francisco for most of her life. She was reluctant to

near her, but obviously could see neither the barrier nor the mysterious trees that

“Guys, I think I see something,” Ahlina said so low it might as well have

Kaery was so proud to be Scape’s friend. “You’re right. It’s unlikely he’s

supposed to be. Like a lucky charm.” Trying to raise her friends’ spirits, she

figure lurking among the trees. “What is that?” Kaery couldn’t take her eyes off the 
creature, a black wolf with a crescent moon of silver fur on its chest. It was larger 
than any wolf she’d ever seen, and it’s big green eyes looked almost human.
“That’s a Spiraar,” Crux said as he produced several small objects from his 
pocket and prepared to launch them at the beast.
“So I’m guessing that’s the Grove he was talking about?” Ahlina pointed 
past the wolf to the invisible barrier that was now revealing itself with the elegance 
of light waves rippling on a hot day.

Chapter 28
SURGE’S GOLEMS
“Don’t you think it’s a good time to freak out, Blue?” Surge whispered as 
she stared into the eyes of the living monoliths. 
“Let me figure something out,” Blue said, more frustrated that he’d got them 
into this situation than anything else. “They seem pretty slow. Just by looking at 
their size, I mean, they’re probably slow, so we can make a run for it?” Blue was 
unsure of how to get out of this predicament safely. He was not fully aware of what 
these Golems were capable of, and he dared not cause any alarm for fear they’d 
attack his fourteen year old companion. Surge was physically weak, even by 
human standards. As if she’d never gone outside to play with the other kids, let 
alone participate in Physical Education at school, her body was frail and lithe. 
Blue weighed his options, and he didn’t think he had enough time to grab 
Surge and risk her life running back into the booby trapped tunnel where the dead 
creature was lying. As he thought about the situation, he heard a familiar voice 
speak to him as if someone else was suddenly in the room with them.
“Blue, how typical of you to mess things up like this.”
He did not bother answering because he’d already thought of that himself. 
Instead, he looked at the design of the large stone and metal guardians in front of 
him, knowing that the showdown had already started the moment they’d trespassed 
on this sacred place. 
“You can’t get out of this by running like you always do. Or by punching, 
for that matter, which was always your second go-to.” The female voice was calm, 
but it in Blue’s eyes, it was not helping the current situation.
“What do you expect me to do then, Charity?” Blue asked, “Are you going 
to tell me that Mom and Dad are disappointed too? I’m sorry, Ok?” The familiar 
atmospheric smell entered the room as he prepared himself for battle.
Surge looked shocked at her friend’s sudden screaming. “What? Mom and 
Dad? I didn’t say anything!” She instantly began to think of all the files she’d ever 
come across on the internet that dealt with mental ailments. She had come to 

 
253
understand that she could recall just about anything that had ever been saved on a 
hard drive as well as every component that made up the information. She had quite 
literally memorized the internet. She watched Blue talk to himself as if he were a 
mental patient. 
Charity began to speak in a much sweeter tone, knowing her brother always 
responded well when she was calm and patient. “I’m sorry, Blue, I didn’t mean it 
that way. You did a good job saving Crux, but now you have to make sure nothing 
happens to Surge. She may be the one person who can stop OZONE for good.” 
Even though Blue couldn’t see his sister, but he sensed her presence and 
heard her words, deciding to sacrifice himself in order to save Surge. “You’re 
right, and I think I know what I have to do.”
“What? Wait, Blue, what are you talking about? Who are you talking to?” 
Surge asked desperately as she saw Blue take a step forward toward the machine. 
His forehead became slick with sweat despite the cold temperature of the 
cave. He was going to face the living creature and buy Surge some time to get out. 
“I’m sorry, Surge, I didn’t mean to get you into this kind of trouble. Run as soon as 
the things attack me.”
Before Surge could open her mouth to object, Blue took off running toward 
the living tanks. She saw a blur of attacks as he punched and kicked the walls of 
metal and stone in reckless abandon. His fists seemed to glow an electric blue, his 
eyes reflecting the same color as he swung through the air screaming. The Golems 
were a lot faster than they had thought, seeming to anticipate Blue’s next move as 
he ran between them in a streak of light. The action was happening so fast that 
Surge was amazed she could see anything at all. 
The Golems of metal and stone had eyes that glowed mint green as golden 
steam spewed from the crevices in their large joints. Their arms morphed into 
crude weapons as they swung at their foe, but Blue managed to evade every strike 
despite their attempts to sabotage his next step. 
Surge continued to watch, noticing something peculiar in her study of the 
battle: the Golems were getting smarter with every attack Blue tried. These 
machines must have been forged by a genius. They were not only anticipating his 
every move, but they were also changing their strategy according to his attacks, 
and they’d catch him off guard eventually. 
“Blue, watch out! They are adapting to your attacks!” Surge screamed as she 
watched the chaotic fight. 

 
254
Like an owl, one of the Golems turned its head 270 degrees, stalking Blue 
with precision, stopping its pursuit only when the other thirty Golems seemed but a 
second away from intercepting the trespasser.
Surge had nowhere to run. She knew the booby-trapped hall they had come 
from was suicide. A Golem stomped toward her, its large fist raised above its head 
ready to crush her. The Golem was easily fifteen feet tall, and judging by its 
expandable joints that moved fluidly with bursts of steam, it could stretch to even 
greater heights. What kind of steam punk nightmare is this? Wait—Wait, that’s it! 
The steam is coming from the fuel source. She looked back at the enemy as she ran, 
noticing Golden Runes of Magick barely visible through the large cracks in the 
body of the stone giant. Runes! They say a whole lot about the engineering of this 
thing. Too bad it’s about to kill me.
In what must have been an instant, Surge studied every file in her mind on 
Femtotechnology, the means by which subatomic particles had stimulated the 
otherwise inanimate Golems, altering their cellular structure and giving them life. 
The only metal that could sustain such a force was called Electrum. Surge opened 
her eyes, but it was too late, and the Golem’s fist came crashing down on her. 
Blue was dashing toward Surge through hundreds of swings and slices. He 
had heard her warning about the adaptability of his enemies, but realized during his 
relentless attacks that she had no place to run. He’d have to destroy these creatures 
if they were going to make it out alive. Dodging a series of energy waves that shot 
from the Golem’s open palms, he managed to survive yet another attack from the 
inhuman protectors of the cave. He desperately tried to make it to Surge before the 
Golem closed in, his heart nearly jumping out of his chest as he saw the stone arm 
plummet at an alarmingly fast rate. Even he’d be obliterated by the force of 
something that large and powerful. 
“NO!” he screamed, knowing it was too late.

Jayde made her way through Zalomoni quite easily with long a stride that 
was unique to residents of the city. The Grove that this place had been built on was 
a canyon she had personally discovered thousands of years before the Ice Age. It 
had been pioneered by Supernaturals, created to be their sanctuary. 
Jayde knew this Grove, like many others, was a sacred place that Gaia held 
dear, as it was one of the rare places Magick flowed easily and unrestrained. She 
also knew they were playing a dangerous game as Gaia no longer favored too 
much power being locked in one place for very long. 
Like the early years of the planet, Pangaea was now full of powerful beings 
who took their environment for granted. They had egotistically cast the powerful 
Spell of Enlightenment, giving humans the ability to use Magick, which caused a 
rift that nearly destroyed Earth in a single day. That night when the moon was full, 
Gaia had created her own guardians and protectors, the Spiraar. 
With a wave of unbridled passion and rage, these man-beasts overran the 
earth, destroyed everything in their path with but one order from their goddess. As 
the first Mancer who had evolved into the very essence of the planet, Gaia had 
become a Titan with only one objective, the preservation of Earth. Everyone 
instantly became her enemies, and in some cases, they had to be cleansed from her 
planet. 
This was the tale of the Mother Gaia and her retaliation to regain control of 
what was hers. The shapeshifting Spiraar took every shape, from small insects to 
large mammals of proportions the world had never seen before. They were not 
only palpable foes for the powerful Supernaturals who had already existed, but 
they were also a threat to the newly enlightened humans. They purged the lands of 
any and all life until a balance was restored. 
The Spell of Enlightenment had been reversed, and a Veil of Ignorance had 
reclaimed the omnipotent powers of the humans, making them once again ignorant 
and innocent. Gaia stopped the war she had waged, vowing that she’d resurrect her 
peaceful beasts if the balance was ever disturbed. She did not want too much 
power concentrated in one place ever again, and so began the Age of Kingdoms. 
The Mancers, Wicasht and Jreamers were forced to separate their vast 
realms into smaller domains. This appeased Gaia as she sent her Spiraar only as 
messengers and protectors of the remaining Groves while also cleansing the lands 
of tainted beings that the humans had released upon the Earth, like the Vampyl, 

 
256
who were a product of their fear. The Spiraar still had a vicious instinctual hatred 
of these undead and unnatural immortals. 
Jayde was not a Mancer who had survived the Great War, but upon her 
rebirth, she had vowed to protect her people with her last breath. She gathered as 
many as she could find and claimed the canyon Grove as their home. The amount 
of power she brought together led to frequent battles with Spiraar who were sent to 
restore the balance. This was a battle Jayde knew she could win, as the social 
structure of her city was a peaceful one. She prayed to Gaia, stressing that her 
people were pacifists, wanting nothing more than to remain within the city without 
disturbing the outlands.
Gaia seemed to be appeased until some curious citizens ventured too far 
from Zalomoni, deciding to claim the surrounding lands as their own. The egos of 
her once proud warriors became their own downfall as they breached Gaia’s laws. 
“Too much power is what corrupted my people,” Jayde said quietly to 
herself as she hurried into the cave and carefully made her way through the 
intricate hallway to the blacksmith’s. She didn’t trigger a single trap, as she already 
knew the patterns of these safeguards. When she reached the end of the hall, she 
saw the horrific inevitable scene that was playing out too quickly for her to 
prevent. One of Nadir’s golems is about to kill the girl! Even Blue can’t reach her 
in time, and neither can I! Jayde started toward Surge, but a moment later, the 
Golem’s fist came down upon the girl with enough force to leave her in pieces.
Jayde thought of the time she had been introduced to these Golems with 
Guardian, how the Magickal machines had wiped the floor with some of her best 
Royal Guards. This was not going to be a pleasant fight, but vengeance for Surge 
was the only option at this point. She stopped almost as fast as Blue did at the next 
revelation. 
The Golem did not smash Surge-Overload after all, its attack halted as if the 
small girl were a wall stronger than the stone machine itself. From the moment its 
giant fist made contact with the top of her head, she somehow had control of this 
inhuman creature’s every function.
Surge turned around dramatically to see Jayde standing behind her. “It’s 
wonderful! They are machines!”
Blue was still being attacked when Surge’s Golem went into defense mode, 
leaping over him to oppose its former brothers in arms. A desperate battle for 

 
257
dominance ensued, the Golems attempting to suppress the traitor when Surge 
approached them. 
Blue reached grabbed her arm. “I don’t know how you survived that attack, 
but I don’t think you should interrupt them.” His kind eyes would have stopped her 
if she wasn’t so resolute on what she had to do. 
“I have to do this,” Surge said reassuringly while gently pulling away from 
Blue. “Trust me.” She ran into the middle of the fight.
Even Jayde was confused about how this girl hadn’t been killed by the first 
Golem, that was until focused her eyes on its body, reading the Runes of Magick 
that were glowing in the stone skin. “She has somehow reprogrammed the Golem 
to do her bidding,” Jayde said, “It’s her power.”
Blue looked at her and then back to Surge while the Golems fought like ageold enemies, flinging metal and stone in all directions. Some were firing bursts of 
golden plasma while others were tearing each other to pieces. Despite the havoc 
being wreaked around her, Surge stood composed beneath the feuding creatures. “I 
should go in and help her,” Blue said, readying himself to dash into the fray.
“Trust her,” Jayde said wisely. “Maybe it’s her turn to save us?”
After sustaining so much damage, the exoskeletons of the Golems had been 
shredded, revealing the Runes underneath that were began to emit gold and blue 
steam. In a matter of seconds, the hard flesh of each creature began to repair itself, 
chunks of shrapnel reaffixing themselves to the giant bodies as if they were 
magnetized. Surge lightly touched each machine as she courageously weaved her 
way through the chaos. As if a virus had been spread from Surge’s hands, the 
Golems become obedient, infecting the few that were left uncontrolled until every 
last one of them was standing still like a statue. Surge skipped among the giant feet 
of her quiet minions.
“What just happened?” Blue asked, puzzled. 
“They won’t hurt us anymore,” Surge said as one of the Golems lifted her 
onto its shoulder. “See?” She knocked on its head with her knuckles. “I’ve learned 
more from them than I did from those OZONE files I stole. I think it’s about time 
you told us about what really happened here, Jayde.” 
Jayde simply nodded and said, “As do I.”

Deme looked over the damage report for the hundredth time, trying to 
further analyze each step Surge-Overload had taken to retrieve the files. The 
floating holographic screens before him were scrolling through the seemingly 
endless list of files believed to be compromised, displaying information according 
to his whims. He had almost missed the cat and mouse battle he was so frustrated 
about just weeks ago. 
He stood like a statue in his minimalist apartment, techno music blaring. He 
needed the ambience in order to concentrate on the overwhelming threat. The agent 
list was bad enough, but once he noticed that files detailing OZONE’s latest 
technological achievements were also stolen, he began to smile like a madman. 
She had easily outwitted the entire organization as if they were all a bunch of 
unsuspecting suckers in her game of poker. He knew she had fun toying with them, 
just as he would if he were in her place. He stood still, staring blankly ahead, his 
simple black garments fluttering from the air conditioner’s breeze in this sterile 
environment. 
His mind could barely fathom how much damage one hacker had
accomplished in so little time. Even though he was furious, he was equally 
intrigued. Deme sipped his coffee, placed the cup on his ergonomic desk and began 
to stand on one leg without taking his eyes off the files.
She had accessed prototype blueprints that no one else in the world knew 
about. If a genius like her had been able to harness even a fragment of that 
technology, she could’ve practically run the world.
“So this is the power of a Mancer? Thank god we killed her!” Deme laughed 
to himself.

“Lisa, please stop crying. It’s not your fault, hun.” Reynolds looked at his 
sobbing wife and tried to stay strong for her. “She—she ditched camp to answer 
some ad for gamers in Union Square. The cab driver confirmed he dropped her off, 
and well—we found her luggage and—”
“Stop! I read the damn report.” Lisa was a naturally charming woman, 
seasoned in psychological analysis and always in control of her outward 
expression, today her training was all but lost as she, trapped in this painful 
moment, just wanted to forget her identity as an OZONE agent. “It’s my fault!” 
“You can’t blame yourself.”
“Yes I can! I was the one who forced her to go to the camp. I was the one 
who told her to meet new people and get out there.” Lisa’s eyes were so red and 
sore that it hurt to open them. “I should’ve let her stay in her room. I should 
have…”
“It wasn’t your fault she ran right into some covert mission that ended in 
explosions.” Now his eyes were tearing up, but he kept them out of Lisa’s sight as 
he hugged her. He too was trying to forget his allegiance to the organization that he 
knew must have been responsible for his daughter’s death. His tears couldn’t be 
held back any longer. No father should feel this way.
“I know what you’re doing, and I love you for it, but I know who’s to blame 
here,” Lisa sobbed into her husband’s button up shirt. “I’ll never be able to live 
with myself.”
“She’s in a better place,” Reynolds said, having nothing else to say in an 
attempt to comfort her, though at the same time he was starting to believe what she 
had said, contemplating her level of responsibility for his only daughter being 
dead. His once hopeful soul was already growing more bitter each day, and now 
his favorite person had left the planet much too early. “She’s in a better place.”

Chapter 29
FALL OF OLYMPUS II
(391 B.C.)
Kaery stood still for a long moment while she thought deeply of the troubles 
she faced. Blue was making a terrible decision to harm his own people while Nina 
was threatening the Supernatural society that the Coven of Unity had taken an oath 
to protect. The jasmine and rosemary-infused water filled her senses as she focused 
on the here and now. There were decisions she had to make, and she had to be 
hasty. The natural balance was in danger, and in a moment of clarity, like a sweet 
dream, she realized what she had to do, destroy Olympus. 
“Oh, Kaery, I need your assistance,” Nina said gently, as if the air around 
her was filled with fairies. She calm, more in control of her feelings today than she 
was most of the time. With Kaery’s blessings, she was going to take her people 
into a new era where everyone would love one another. This was the greatest day 
ever. 
Kaery walked into the room where Nina was preparing for her appearance. 
She didn’t normally do this sort of work, deciding what costumes Nina was going 
to wear. That was specifically a Star role. When she looked into Nina’s eyes, she 
saw through all the smoke and mirrors. She had to turn away, so she focused on the 
garments, sorting through some dresses until in the corner of her eye she saw a 
man standing at the other end of the room. Without missing a beat, she assumed 
her combat stance. 
“Whoa, Kaery, I apologize for the informal arrival. I'm just the messenger.” 
Hermes spoke in an elegant voice that was almost too smooth for his image. He 
was a strikingly handsome man, and like most gods of Olympia, adorned in gold 
bands that were no doubt blessed with powerful Magick. Hermes was one of the 
rare Gods who was specifically blessed with artifacts created by the Coven of 
Unity, the winged boots he loved so much for his cross-dimensional travels. 
Kaery and Star had known that Hermes was a Cronomancer who could 
travel between dimensions, but he lacked control over his amazing power, so 
Ahlina had stepped in with her powers of the Daeva, and with the Coven’s 
permission, created the boots with Star determining their aesthetic. Ahlina had 

 
261
truly focused her intentions on the boots, and like a spontaneous miracle, the 
artifacts were created.
Hermes caught Kaery staring at the gifts he treasured most. “I still adore you 
all for these lovely instruments, but that is not what I came here for. Zeus wants me 
to deliver a message to Aphrodite at once.” His smile was humble and genuine.
Nina was still naked, but just as comfortable in her bare skin as she was in 
her gowns. She walked in front of the protective Kaery, placing a loving hand on 
her shoulder. “It’s ok. Please continue, Hermes.” 
“Zeus would like all the humans to mate tonight, so bless the festivals with 
your most potent love potions. He has an announcement to make tomorrow, but 
wants everyone to enjoy themselves this eve,” he said, bowing slightly at the naked 
goddess. 
Star came running into the room upon hearing the announcement. “Oh, my 
goddess, why aren’t you dressed?” 
“Tell him it shall be done,” Nina said. “Oh, and I have a few 
announcements of my own.”
“Of course. What would you like me to tell him?” Hermes asked. 
Kaery’s body was still tense, even though she was no longer on high alert. 
She was terrified that Star would find out this way and had to interrupt the 
message. “Well wouldn’t you like to keep that announcement a secret, your 
highness?”
“Oh, the intrigue! I must admit, I do like your style, Kaery. Tell him I will 
make my announcement before I make my departure to Egypt.” Nina smiled as 
Star dressed her diligently. 
“As you wish.” Hermes smiled at the women, and with a flash of his white 
teeth, was gone as if he’d jumped into a Port Rune.
Most of the gods of the courts relied on the astounding artifacts the Covens 
created for them. The Coven of Unity was one of the favored Witchbreed 
communities, even worshipped as gods like most Mancers. Their artifacts were 
legendary and arguably the means by which the gods maintained control of 
humanity. 
“After today’s ceremony, I would like to request that Star be taken to Egypt 
with you,” Kaery said, her back still turned.
Nina stopped looking in the mirror and went to Kaery, “Are you sure?” 

 
262
“You’d do that for me?” Star asked in shock, “I mean, I know how you’re 
always the one who gets chosen to go.” 
“I insist. Is that ok with you, Nina?” Kaery looked over her shoulder as she 
prepared to walk out the door. 
“Well, of course, that’s the least I can do,” Nina said, confused at the 
informal request. As part of the festival, she was supposed to announce her 
entourage. Usually it was one of her maidens, if not all three, and then there was 
always her lover, Juno. Nina simply looked happy for Star, who was ecstatic about 
the recent events. By the time Nina looked up to say something to Kaery, she was 
already long gone. 
*
Kaery was out in the hall with her back to the wall, out of sight. She didn’t 
want to speak with Nina at the moment, and she didn’t want Star to see her sob. 
I can’t allow this to happen. I love Blue, and I love Nina, but they must know 
the error of their ways. They didn’t take our warnings humbly, but instead they 
have abused their subjects. Nina has always thought her gift was the ability to 
bend the wills of entire armies. Yes it’s an astounding skill, but her true power is 
the capacity to channel pure love from the Soulstream into a womb, creating 
Supernatural children.
There’s no guarantee that any of the Supernaturals will have a baby with 
powers, as some have gifts and others don’t, but Nina is our guarantee of being 
blessed with a child at all, and she handles this responsibility as whimsically as 
she handles her feelings instead of thinking of what’s best for her subjects. Well 
that’s enough! I know what I propose next will hurt the kingdom of Olympia, which 
I’m also loyal to, but I cannot sit idly by and watch Blue destroy the mankind 
simply because he can, nor can I allow Nina’s decision to go unpunished.
I’ll see Blue one more time and see what he has to say. I’ll give Nina the 
benefit of the doubt, but if she strays, she will force my hand. I will destroy the city 

 
263
that I’ve held together with my love and strength for hundreds of years. Stop 
crying, Kaery! This is the time for you to be the strongest you’ve ever been.
Now get to Blue and hope he isn’t really planning to unchain a Dehmn beast that 
will end innocent lives. Releasing a Kraken on his own people like a tyrant... 
Either way, I will make my move at the Witching hour.
Kaery regained her composure and continued her trek to the God of gods, 
her secret lover, Zeus.
*
Ahlina was kneeling as she prayed in Argos with the loyal followers of 
Aphrodite. She’d happily visited many cities throughout the day with her newly 
mastered powers of teleportation. This was her last stop of the journey, to bring 
hope to the young maidens here, Supernatural and human alike. 
Though Hera was revered in this city, the festival for Aphrodite was 
unmatched in praise and festivities. This was one of Ahlina’s proudest moments as 
she accepted the prayers of the locals. They were mundane and humble wishes, and 
though Ahlina knew that Nina had the power to wash away their fears with 
unwavering love, she just smiled peacefully and tried to focus on the sincerity of 
each plea. Overall it seemed there just wasn’t much happening here in the midst of 
human society, not much that was of concern anyway. 
Forcing herself to have some patience, she continued listening to the prayers 
as High Priestess. The soft music of harps and lyres kissed the air, the anticipation 
becoming contagious as cheers from the surrounding streets began to fill the 
temple. I miss my lover, Hermes. I can’t wait for Nina to announce that I will bear 
a child for my beloved. It seems like forever, but it’s only been a hundred years, all 
finally coming to an end. Supernaturals deserve to have children like these 
humans. Our births are as random as my powers, but it’s about time we had the 
same luxury as these peasants who have no idea how wonderful it must be to have 
a child at will. Ugh, Nina, hurry up and make the announcement already. I’ve done 
everything you’ve ever asked for, listening endlessly to the prayers of your 

 
264
followers. I really miss Hermes! She maintained her smile as she continued her 
duties. 
Of all the problems Ahlina had to listen to today, she did not want hear 
about anymore relationship issues, or even worse, libido issues. Her ears were 
already overflowing with them, and at this point after thousands of requests and 
acknowledgments, she was starting to loathe her job. That’s when she knew it was 
high time for the ceremony to start. As she kept her respectful demeanor in check, 
a very poor young girl walked up to her in the temple. 
Let me guess, she wants a love spell cast on her boyfriend, or better yet, she 
wants to make suitors of the men who ignore her. I can already hear her asking 
Aphrodite to bless her with an enchanting perfume. Ok, stop being judgmental, 
Ahlina, you have a job to do. Just listen to her petty concerns. 
The girl’s hair was the color of wheat on a breezy spring afternoon. Her skin 
was fair, but unwashed, as if she had endured hardship to reach the temples of the 
great Goddess of Love in Argos. Like a lot of the peasants, this woman did not 
know the formal etiquette expected of temple visitors, nor had she brought a gift 
for the altar. She looked distraught, her innocent eyes holding something distinct 
from what Ahlina had seen in other worshippers today, the look of hopelessness.
From the moment Ahlina made eye contact with the girl, she wanted to help 
her. After gathering some semblance of composure, the girl finally spoke.
“I’m sorry, High Priestess of Aphrodite. I am a simple girl from Carthage.” 
She was out of breath, on the brink of tears.
“You’ve traveled far, child,” Ahlina said, keeping her regal disposition but 
showing sympathy. “You don’t look like you’ve had much to eat.” She signaled to 
one of her priestesses.
“I promise I did not come all the way here to beg for food.” 
“I understand, but you shall eat while we speak, young one.” 
“Yes, Mistress.”
The priestess returned with a tray of warm bread, cheese and wine, making 
no effort to conceal her pity as she saw the fear and fatigue written all over the 
girl’s face. Ahlina quickly dismissed the priestess and took a mental note to 
reprimand her for her obvious judgment. But then she thought, I did the same thing 
just moments ago. What has happened to me? I love my duties as High Priestess, 
even if most human wishes are predictable and frivolous. This girl is different. She 
may be a peasant, but something tells me that she isn’t completely human. 

 
265
“Please kill my baby,” the girl said, sobbing through a small bite of the 
moist bread.
Ahlina’s look of shock displayed the fact that she’d never heard anything 
like this in all of her years listening to prayers as she took a step back from the 
young woman. “Excuse me? What kind of request is that?” Her green eyes looked 
the girl up and down in suspicion. 
“I don’t mean to sound ungrateful, but I woke up pregnant having never 
courted a man. My mother and father think I am cursed by the gods for this.” Her 
small hands parted her sandy colored robes to show a baby bump. 
The sign of the girl’s early months of pregnancy made Ahlina smile. “Why 
do you think this is a curse?”
“I’m sorry, Mistress, but I don’t know what else to call it. I have not made 
love to a man, yet here I am. I’m not married, and now I’ll never be.” 
“What is your name, and who accompanied you on your journey here?” 
“My name is Dido, and I came with my childhood friend, Mathos.” She 
motioned to a shy young man standing at the edge of the temple. “Please come.”
Ahlina immediately noticed the look of adoration in the young man’s eyes. 
She did not want to alarm him, but for the very first time, she could see the Runes 
of Magick at work, nurturing this innocent affection. She smiled as the man 
kneeled. “You love her.” 
He looked so surprised to be put on the spot that he could only look up at the 
girl silently as if he’d never told her himself. “Yes I do,” he said.
Ahlina smiled and walked to Dido. “May I?” 
The girl was still in disbelief about Mathos’ feelings for her, but she agreed. 
Ahlina knelt before the girl, placing her ear to the soft belly. She closed her 
eyes and listened for a few awkward seconds, detecting the second heartbeat. She 
thought of the baby she so hoped Nina would announce at today’s festival. And it 
was then that what could only be described as a miracle occurred. A little voice 
was suddenly audible, singing from within the womb. Ahlina’s eyes shot open in 
surprise, but she kept her head on Dido’s belly. 
“What is it?” Dido asked at the gasp from Ahlina. 
The Priestesses surrounded them so as to block onlookers from witnessing 
anything further. The soft melody reminded Ahlina of her walks through the open 
fields full in the morning sunshine, of the colorful butterflies that sat on the 
flowers. She immediately knew that this was a Supernatural child. This girl was 

 
266
carrying a Mancer, and judging by the power signature she distinguished, he was a 
new one. She reluctantly lifted her head from Dido’s bell, unable to stop smiling at 
the song she would never forget. “The Earth Song.”
“I’m sorry, Mistress what does that mean?” 
“This is going to be a baby boy,” Ahlina said happily. “I do not want to 
alarm you because this baby is definitely not a curse, actually more of a blessing 
than you know. The Gods favor you.” 
Mathos smiled as he felt the heavy burden of the uncertain journey finally 
being lifted from his beloved Dido’s shoulders.
“How is that possible?” the girl asked, breaking into sobs.
“The Gods work in mysterious ways,” Ahlina said. “I don’t believe you 
should destroy this baby. It is not the will of the Gods.”

escape route. “What the hell is a wolf doing in Golden Gate Park anyway?” 
“That’s a wolf?” Star eyed the hulking four-legged beast in disbelief. “I 
could swear I’ve seen joggers with bigger dogs.”
“Star, trust me it’s a Spiraar,” Crux said, his voice wavering.
Star looked to Crux as they all continued to take blind steps backward. His 
tone was so serious that she turned her attention to the large black creature and 
made sure to keep up with her friends’ steady retreat. 
“We can’t run from him,” Scape said fearfully, “The moment we turn our 
backs, that thing will rip our heads off.” 
“Thanks for that visual,” Ahlina said, now even closer to tears.

of the dark predator only a few feet away, “And I’m sorry I forgot to mention, it 
can understand us.” The Spiraar had taken the form of a legendary wolf that was 
easily the size of a horse on all four paws. The beast neither snarled nor flinched 
before its young audience, but it looked alarmed as if wondering how these kids 
had noticed its presence in the first place.
“You are the guardian of this Grove?” Kaery asked in a low voice.
