clouds above.
The entire forest was full of strange lights as the Runes of Magick ran 
through every blade of grass, every leaf of the trees and plants. It was breathtaking, 
so beautiful that Kaery almost forgot she was in battle. She dropped low into the 
splits, then rolled backwards in one fluid motion until she was underneath Rook, 
and the next moment right behind him.
The jaws snapped with such force it scared her friends. Crux turned away, 
unable to watch Kaery being devoured. The tears that were in his eyes flew onto 
the dry soil at his feet as he grabbed his marbles. He didn’t know how to tell his 
new friends to run and let him take this creature on, so he turned as if to attack. 
With wide-eyed anticipation, he looked upon the most unexpected sight. Kaery 
was now behind the wolf, and she straight-kicked him in the tail bone, sending 
him flying toward a large tree that stopped his flight with a heavy crash. 
The dust from the Grove floor formed a cloud around Rook as he recovered 
and shifted into a two-legged creature easily twenty-feet tall with razor-sharp 
claws. His enormous chest heaved, large enough to swallow a car. The black fur 
was still as dark and thick as ever, and his glowing eyes tried to pierce Kaery’s to 
no avail as he dashed toward her, ripping up the soil in his pursuit. 
Kaery used combat techniques that had long been lost to the world, her 
movement a flurry of misdirection, parries and side-steps that allowed her to glide 
past every attack. As if she could see every move Rook was going to make three 
steps ahead of time, she evaded his every strike. 
Rook howled and tore through the air as if fighting an ancient war, though 
none of his moves connected. He soon realized Kaery was light on her feet, 
deciding to use some of his innate abilities to summon the elements. The roots on 

 
281
the ground shifted the closer he got to Kaery until they reached up and ensnared 
her feet like vipers. This was it. No more dodging his attacks. She was very 
quickly becoming immobile. 
Kaery was too focused on the body movement and muscle motion of the 
wolf to notice the branches and roots of the nearby trees shifting around her feet. 
Her shoe became snagged and she looked up to see the jaws of the Spiraar 
surrounded her torso in one bite. There was no dodging. 
Rook’s jaws snapped on Kaery’s body and tore her from the roots, sending 
her shoe flying into the forest. He slung her like a ragdoll into the trunk of a tree 
and then slammed her unceremoniously onto the forest floor. 
With a simultaneous gasp, all of Kaery’s friends watched in horror her bones 
must have been snapped, not to mention the wounds inflicted from the rows of 
monstrous teeth. The moment was so disheartening that Scape fell to his knees 
whispering, “No!”
“It looks like your leader has been defeated,” Rook said in his giant twolegged form with the torso of a man, his large head peaking down into the forest at 
his prey.
The kids were silent. They could not believe Kaery was dead or dying, and 
there was nothing they could do about it. Lightning struck in the background. The 
storm was getting closer, and the winds were picking up. Star and Ahlina stood 
next to each other in their fighting stances, realizing it all might be futile. Right as 
they gave each other a signal to get ready, six wolves of various sizes walked out 
of the shadows. 
Rook continued, “You have trespassed onto sacred grounds, and for that 
violation we will choose whether you live or not.”
Kaery felt as if she were hit by the BART train and fell into the ocean from a 
cliff. The tree somehow stopped her impact, and she felt like it had caught her. 
When she landed with an ungraceful crash, the world around her was blurry. She 
just knew she was going to die from the wounds of the bite, but she didn’t feel any 
broken bones. Looking down at her hands, they seemed to glow a golden color as 
if the sun was shining on them. She saw her t-shirt was shredded, but her bra was 
intact, and there was not a single puncture wound on her body. How could this be?
There was no time to question her current state, which as far as she could see 
was miraculously untouched. She ran as fast as she could up the back of the large 
beast and clotheslined him to the ground with so much force, it seemed as if a tree 

 
282
had fallen in the forest with a thunderous crescendo. Like a gymnast, she did a 
back hand spring off the furry beast and landed near her friends with her back to 
them, her clothes shredded to the point of nonexistence. She didn’t seem to be 
bothered by the fact that her bra was the only top she was wearing, and her jeans 
hung on her legs in ribbons. 
“I said, no one is hurting my friends!” Kaery’s voice boomed throughout 
Golden Gate Park as lightning struck overhead and a curtain of rain came pouring 
down. Her purple bangs flowed like a wet waterfall in front of her eyes. She shook 
and breathed deeply, still in her fighting stance. She looked around the forest at the 
wolves hiding in the shadows. 
They backed into the forest slowly, seeing their alpha on the ground in 
severe pain. Bones crushed and trying to regenerate, Rook attempted to stand, but 
couldn’t. He knew this was no ordinary Witchbreed; he’d killed plenty in his 
lifetime. They were manipulators of Magick, but most couldn’t concentrate when 
he moon glide attacked them in his initial surprise. This one had somehow dodged 
his superhuman speed and his primal bite. When he thought of that bite he tasted 
blood in his own mouth. This can’t be. Some of my teeth are shattered. As if she’s 
invulnerable. 
Kaery stood up with her back stretched, heart racing like never before. She 
balled up her fists and said, “Please stop this fight. I don’t want to hurt you 
anymore. I’ll do whatever you want as long as you don’t allow them to hurt my 
friends. I don’t know if they’ll listen to me, but I know they’ll listen to you. I beg 
of you.” With her fists slowly becoming soft hands again, she kneeled down to 
nurse Rook’s wounds. 
Rook winced at the pain of her touch, Then he saw the golden light upon 
him, an extension of her hand like sunshine on a cold day. In the midst of the 
pouring rain, the warmth healed him faster than he had ever regenerated. “Why 
would you heal your enemy?”
“Because I don’t believe you are my enemy,” Kaery said as she healed the 
broken limbs of the large beast. She could feel the repair happening at an unnatural 
rate underneath her palms. 
Rook shifted into a naked man right before her eyes, his ribs broken, the 
crackling sound audible over the rainfall. His tanned sand-colored skin was visible 
underneath thick black wiry hair covering his chest, forearms and legs. His eyes 

 
283
were a soft greenish blue, staring at Kaery like she was something foreign. “Who 
are you?”
“I am Kaery. These are my friends.” She could not find the energy to smile 
as she spoke her truth. “I just don’t want them to be hurt.”
A deep and familiar voice invaded the conversation as Gemini walked into 
the rainfall. “So must it be.” Gemini smiled a white smile that seemed to make 
everyone feel more at ease.
Kaery looked up from the now human form of Rook and took her hands off 
his ribs. Like the Runes of Magick Kaery now saw everywhere, the figure in front 
of her was glowing with sheer power that radiated from his inner being like a 
beacon in the darkness. She was forced to pay attention to the second figure whose 
energy signature was the same as Mother Gaia’s. 
“Meet your new advisor, The Avatar Gauntlets,” Gemini said.
“Rook is the guardian of this most revered Grove,” Gauntlets said as he 
came walking slowly from behind a tree. His steps seemed to be guided by the lay 
of the land. The dark moss-colored robes he wore looked like blankets of grass and 
wet earth. “He and his pack have done a wonderful job thus far.” 
“Who is she?” Rook asked Gauntlets, bowing in respect.
“She and her friends are the true owners of this Grove. They are the ones 
you were trained to look for when the time came. She is Kaery, and this is her 
Coven of Unity. They set claim to these woods long before humans lived in this 
area, casting the Magick that allowed the Grove to be hidden for so long. Now they 
are back to reclaim it,” Gauntlets answered respectfully.
“What’s going on?” Star asked, stepping out of her stance and walking like a 
model to stand next to Kaery. Ahlina followed and stood on the other side of their 
best friend who would have died for them. 
Gauntlets’ wise words cut through the rain like the sun rays that had parted 
the clouds for Kaery. “Great question, that will be answered in due time. But I will 
tell you, your training starts right now, and you’ve passed the first lesson with 
flying colors.” 

 
284
PROLOGUE TO BOOK 3
ONE YEAR LATER
As Sky was beginning to waver in hand-to-hand combat, barely dodging the 
attacks of countless OZONE Agents in the desert sand, Maia knew she had to do 
something. Standing atop a dune and focusing her intellect, she telepathically 
disoriented the agents, causing them to falter in confusion. Gaining the upper hand, 
Sky circled her enemies with lightning speed and grabbed them one by one, 
throwing them high up into the air in rapid succession. With the agents now lying 
motionless in the sand or plummeting toward certain death, Sky stopped in the 
middle of the battlefield to rest. Maia tried to call out the mysterious figure as it 
crept up behind Sky in this moment of vulnerability, but her cries weren’t heard. It 
was inexplicably difficult for Maia to send even the smallest telepathic image in 
this place, and she didn’t know why. Before Sky could turn to defend herself, the 
Stealth Assassin emerged from his reflective camouflage and struck with precision, 
knocking her to the ground and leaving several slash marks on her chest. As he 
raised the glowing steel blade above his head, charging it with Rune energy for a 
swift execution, it was clear he was no ordinary assassin, but a Jreamer. As Sky 
closed her eyes to welcome the final blow, she was suddenly blinded by a flash of 
light, her ears beginning to ring. Without concern for the gunfire directed at him, 
Blue raced across the battlefield to Sky’s aid as she lay in the sand, staring at the 
ashen shadow of the vaporized Jreamer. As Maia was watching the scene in 
disbelief, she noticed a small moving shadow on the ground and looked up to see a 
flash grenade sailing toward her. As she reached out her hand in a futile attempt to 
deflect the blast, everything went black.
*

 
285
Maia sat against the dune in the hot desert with tears in her eyes. The pain 
was unbearable as she nursed the burn that had rendered her hand unrecognizable. 
The sky was blocked by a cloud of dark smoke that loomed like a sickness over a 
hospital patient. She could no longer see the stars in her palm, the once soft flesh 
now charred, and she cried at the thought of what it once looked like. Shaking 
uncontrollably, she tried to clench her blistered fist shut so she wouldn’t have to 
look at the horror of her wounds. Feeling helpless in this moment when her friends 
needed her most, she mustered what little courage she had left to help them.
Sky was sitting next to her with slashes across her torso that she was 
somehow unable to heal. She was not the same brat Maia once knew in school, but 
a powerful woman who fought for her right to exist. Now her life’s blood flowed 
from the fatal wound in her abdomen as she stared at the horizon in anguish.
When Maia looked to her strongest friend for comfort, her hope was lost. 
Blue was barely able to focus on anything but the pain in his own ragged body, 
which was covered in bruises, burns and bullet holes. Although he was physically 
tougher than Maia and Sky, the antimatter rounds of the OZONE agents and smite 
attacks of the Jreamers had left him nearly incapacitated. There was still 
determination in his burning blue eyes to complete the mission, though he knew 
just as Maia did that this was all futile. OZONE was too powerful, and it was a 
fact.
Time seemed to stand still as Maia went over the last year in her head and 
tried to understand where things had gone so wrong. 
This can’t be right. We’ve worked so hard for this moment, and the odds are 
totally against us. What did Gemini miss? What did I miss? Were we destined to 
fail like this? I should have seen this coming, but my foresight...why did it suddenly 
become so limited? 
We have to go back for the body. Poor Crux—but how will we have a proper 
burial when we ourselves are lying in coffins? My friends...they depended on me to 
find the answers that would get Surge-Overload out of that OZONE 
compound...when it’s my fault she was captured in the first place. 
This is all such a mess. We should’ve run like Crux once told us to. Now 
look at us! Are Gemini and Jayde really not coming back? How could they have 
been bested by another Mancer? What would Gemini do right now? I can’t keep 
asking these questions…He would want me to come up with solutions. Oh no, Blue 

 
286
is getting ready to attack, but we aren’t ready. We’re going to join our friends in 
death...maybe they’ll welcome us to the underworld, Juno and Nina too. 
How do I tell Blue that I’ve lost faith in this plan? How do I help Sky when it 
seems our healing powers are useless right now? Gemini had such faith in 
us...Gemini...I can’t let him down. If Blue stands, then so will I. Our fate lies on the 
other side of this wall of sand, and there’s no escaping it now.
Maia looked down at Sky while attempting to bandage her wounds. Her 
goddess friend was smiling despite the fact that she was staring death in the face. 
She watched as Sky and Blue’s eyes met, lifetimes of love expressed in their 
affectionate glances. 
Seeing Sky this strong makes me feel even more insecure than I did when I 
first laid eyes on her in high school. After all this time, Blue still loves her, and 
now they’re going to die together. But what about Kaery? He’ll never get to see 
her again. This is impossible. It’s not supposed to be like this.
As if guided by the same fleeting burst of energy, Sky and Blue stood up 
together in silence, she using one hand to stop the blood flowing from her 
abdomen. As if forming a discreet pact, they looked into each other’s eyes, Blue 
giving an assuring wink as Sky kissed the air. Then he reached to Maia, helping 
her stand. 
Ouch! My legs were singed from the explosion too? Nothing to do about it 
now, time to face the end. 
Wait, this can’t be! How did we overlook them before? This fight is already 
over, and those missile blasts are only getting closer. I’m sorry, Mamí, I should’ve 
said ‘I love you’ more often. I’m sorry, Crux. There’s no way we’re going to 
survive this, and with our deaths, the world will be doomed. It’s all our fault. All of 
this destruction is our fault. It’s my fault!
The next wave of the OZONE army launched a hail of missiles that ravaged 
the desert terrain and engulfed the Mancers, instantly disintegrating their flesh and 
obliterating the very shards of the Soulstream that had created them as an opaque 
white light swept over everything in sight.
***

 
287
(Present Day)
Gasping for air, Maia opened her eyes. Tears running down her cheeks and 
tremors running through her body, Maia gazed about, noticing the probing eyes of 
those around her as well as her own reflection in the marble floor. The ancient 
architecture of this familiar temple in Zalomoni and the concerned looks from her 
friends should have brought her comfort, but she couldn’t muster the words to tell 
them how they would inevitably fail their quest. 
Her friends were safe, watching her for some answer as to what the future 
held, but she was silent, unable to shake the gruesome images of their individual 
deaths that came with each glance.
She felt a strong sense of déjà vu as if she were reliving the nightmare from 
her childhood, only this one had been much more personal, much more painful to 
watch over and again. 
“You all die again?” Gemini asked. Maia’s grim expression confirmed his 
suspicions. “We must be missing a part of the equation. What is it?”
Despite analyzing every aspect of the plan countless times before this, it 
wasn’t until this moment that Maia finally made a realization that caused her to 
rise to her feet. She suddenly knew that the Coven of Unity was the missing piece. 
Her soft voice still rattled with fear, she answered, “The Witchbreed!”
